## 32. Post-Section-31 continuation: exact odd-edge divisor coordinates and a correction to the old physical-box argument


This section records the genuinely new continuation after Section 31. It also corrects one old statement that must **not** be used in future work.


### 32.1 Critical correction to Section 2.2 / old A1 discussion


The earlier implication


```text
R >= X >= |r^2-s^2|  and  R<=B  ==>  r,s = O(sqrt(B))
```


is **not valid**. The bound `|r^2-s^2|<=B` does not imply `r,s=O(sqrt(B))` when `r-s` is small. Any future counting argument that truncates the raw Master-Hit parameters by a square box using this implication is invalid.


The exact odd-edge coordinates below avoid this issue entirely: the physical height is imposed exactly from the beginning, with no fake rectangular truncation.


### 32.2 Exact divisor parametrization of every Pythagorean face sharing the unique odd edge


Let `X` be the unique odd edge of a primitive Euler brick. If `Y>0` satisfies


```text
X^2 + Y^2 = h^2,
```


set


```text
A := h-Y.
```


Then


```text
(h-Y)(h+Y)=X^2,
```


so `A|X^2`, `0<A<X`, and


```text
Y = Y_A := (X^2/A - A)/2 = (X^2-A^2)/(2A).
```


Conversely, because `X` is odd, every positive divisor `A|X^2` with `A<X` is odd and produces an integer positive `Y_A` satisfying `X^2+Y_A^2` square. Thus


```text
{Pythagorean partners Y of X}
<--->
{A : A|X^2, 0<A<X}
```


is an exact bijection.


For the two even edges of an Euler brick use divisors `A,C|X^2`. Since `Y_A` is strictly decreasing in `A`, the unordered pair of even edges is the same data as the unordered pair `{A,C}`; imposing `A<C` removes the face-swap multiplicity. The odd edge is intrinsic, so this gives a one-object/one-count coordinate system for physical bricks after final canonical sorting of the three edge lengths.


### 32.3 Exact primitivity criterion in divisor coordinates


Define


```text
d_X(A) := gcd(A, X^2/A).
```


Then


```text
gcd(X,Y_A) = d_X(A).
```


Proof prime-by-prime: if `v_p(X)=v` and `v_p(A)=a`, then for odd `p|X`,


- `a<v`: `v_p(Y_A)=a`;
- `a>v`: `v_p(Y_A)=2v-a`;
- `a=v`: `v_p(Y_A)>=v`, and the gcd exponent is capped at `v`.


Hence the gcd exponent is `min(a,2v-a)`, exactly the exponent in `gcd(A,X^2/A)`.


Therefore


```text
gcd(X,Y_A,Y_C) = gcd(d_X(A),d_X(C)),
```


and the brick is primitive iff


```text
gcd(d_X(A),d_X(C)) = 1.
```


### 32.4 Exact third-face and physical-height equations


The Euler third-face condition is


```text
Y_A^2 + Y_C^2 = square,
```


equivalently


```text
C^2(X^2-A^2)^2 + A^2(X^2-C^2)^2 = square.
```


For the Euclidean space height one gets the exact factorization


```text
4 A^2 C^2 R^2 = (A^2+C^2)(X^4 + A^2 C^2).
```


Hence the exact physical cutoff is simply


```text
(A^2+C^2)(X^4 + A^2 C^2) <= 4 A^2 C^2 B^2.
```


No approximate parameter box is needed.


Subject only to the canonical choice `A<C`, this gives the exact count


```text
M3(B) = sum_{X odd}
          sum_{A,C | X^2, 0<A<C<X}
            1_{gcd(d_X(A),d_X(C))=1}
            1_{Y_A^2+Y_C^2 is square}
            1_{X^2+Y_A^2+Y_C^2 <= B^2}.
```


This is the cleanest exact physical-height adapter currently available.


### 32.5 Relation back to Master-Hit / Pythagorean parameters


For one primitive face written as


```text
X=d(r^2-s^2),
Y=2drs,
h=d(r^2+s^2),
```


we have


```text
A=h-Y=d(r-s)^2,
A/X=(r-s)/(r+s).
```


Similarly, for the second face,


```text
C=e(m-n)^2,
C/X=(m-n)/(m+n).
```


Thus the divisor coordinate is not a competing parametrization; it is an exact integral encoding of the native half-angle coordinate already present in the K_c fibration.


Writing


```text
u=A/X,
v=C/X,
f(t)=(1/t-t)/2,
```


one has


```text
Y_A/X=f(u),
Y_C/X=f(v),
```


so the Euler condition becomes


```text
f(u)^2 + f(v)^2 = w^2,
```


and


```text
R = X * Phi(u,v),
Phi(u,v)^2 = 1 + f(u)^2 + f(v)^2
           = (u^2+v^2)(1+u^2 v^2)/(4u^2v^2).
```


This is the exact scale/shape decomposition that was missing from the old raw-box approach.


### 32.6 Independent consistency with the already-proved local law (not a new Task-4 theorem)


In these normalized coordinates define


```text
E(u,v) = v^2(1-u^2)^2 + u^2(1-v^2)^2,
S(u,v) = (u^2+v^2)(1+u^2v^2).
```


Then


```text
S-E = 4u^2v^2.
```


This gives a short alternative route to the same good-prime character-sum shape already proved in Section 6. It should be treated only as a coordinate simplification / cross-check. **Do not count it as a new proof of Section 6.**


---


## 33. Exact physical census extension and diagnostics


An exact physical enumerator based on the odd-edge divisor representation was used to extend the census substantially while preserving primitive/canonical/physical counting.


Exact counts obtained:


```text
M3(2,000)        = 7
M3(10,000)       = 18
M3(50,000)       = 42
M3(100,000)      = 60
M3(200,000)      = 82
M3(1,000,000)    = 219
M3(10,000,000)   = 656
M3(100,000,000)  = 1757
```


These are finite facts only; do not fit an asymptotic theorem from them.


### 33.1 Fixed-fiber concentration is not visible


Using the corrected genuine Pythagorean base of Sections 26-27, the numbers of distinct occupied fibers were:


```text
B=10^4:  18 bricks,   15 fibers
B=10^5:  60 bricks,   52 fibers
B=10^6: 219 bricks,  183 fibers
B=10^7: 656 bricks,  550 fibers
B=10^8:1757 bricks, 1534 fibers
```


At `B=10^8`, about 87.3% as many fibers as bricks occur. This is finite evidence, not a theorem, but strongly supports the earlier conclusion that M3 growth is mainly a many-fiber problem rather than repeated generation inside a few Mordell-Weil fibers.


### 33.2 Fixed-g truncation is also not a plausible global mechanism


In the same physical census, `g=gcd(U1,U2)` is broadly distributed and grows with height. The fraction with `g=1` falls sharply from small B and is only a few percent by `10^8`; the median `g` grows into the thousands. Thus a proof that handles only fixed `g`, or assumes `g=O(1)` for almost all physical bricks, is not supported by the actual population.


The useful statement remains the weighted one: if a congruence theorem is proved on `g`-slices, one needs an aggregate error of the form


```text
sum_g |E_g(B)| = o(M3(B)),
```


not pointwise control for finitely many fixed `g` values.


### 33.3 Exceptional torsion search was pushed much farther


The Section 27.4 exceptional condition


```text
k^4 - 6k^2 l^2 + l^4 = square
```


was exhaustively tested for primitive opposite-parity pairs with `k<=10^6`; no hit was found. This remains a **finite computation, not a proof** of emptiness, but the old `k,l<200` evidence is now much stronger.


---


## 34. Completing Section 31's degree-2 quotient: explicit equation, bidegree-(4,4) model, and an integral physical model


### 34.1 Explicit quadratic for the Section-31 quotient


Let


```text
t := t1 = a2/a3,
c := t2 = (a2+a3)/(b2+b3).
```


Eliminating `w` from Section 31 gives the exact quadratic


```text
c u^2 + [c^2(t-1) - (t+1)]u + c = 0.
```


Its two roots are exactly `u` and `1/u`, making the quotient involution completely explicit.


The involution `u -> 1/u` has a simple physical meaning: since


```text
a1/a3 = (1-u^2)/(2u),
```


it is exactly the sign change


```text
a1 -> -a1.
```


Thus `t2` is the coordinate on the quotient of K_c by the edge-sign involution in the `a1` direction.


### 34.2 Restore the genuine Pythagorean base


Section 26's bug fix must be built in from the start: `t` is not an arbitrary rational number. Write


```text
t = (lambda^2-1)/(2 lambda),
lambda = L/M.
```


The discriminant of the quadratic in `u`, after clearing the denominator `2lambda`, becomes


```text
Y^2 = (c^2-1)
      [ c^2(lambda^2-2lambda-1)^2
        - (lambda^2+2lambda-1)^2 ].
```


This is bidegree `(4,4)` in `(lambda,c)`. It is a new explicit degree-2 rational model of K_c arising directly from the two transverse rational fibrations. It should **not** be silently identified with the earlier Stage14 toric-host double cover: the branch decomposition is different, so any birational identification requires an explicit map.


### 34.3 Integral bidegree-(2,2) coordinates


Set


```text
P=L^2-M^2,
Q=2LM,
W=L^2+M^2,
A=P-Q,
B=P+Q,
c=C/D,  gcd(C,D)=1.
```


Then the Euler condition is exactly


```text
K^2 = (C^2-D^2)(C^2 A^2 - D^2 B^2).
```


Whenever this is square, the following six integers satisfy all three K_c equations identically:


```text
a1_raw = K,
a2_raw = 2CD P,
a3_raw = 2CD Q,
b1_raw = 2CD W,
b2_raw = D^2 B - C^2 A,
b3_raw = D^2 B + C^2 A.
```


Direct symbolic substitution gives


```text
b1_raw^2 = a2_raw^2+a3_raw^2,
b2_raw^2 = a1_raw^2+a3_raw^2,
b3_raw^2 = a1_raw^2+a2_raw^2.
```


For `(44,117,240)` one may take


```text
(L,M)=(8,5),
(C,D)=(119,123),
```


and the raw tuple reduces exactly to the known brick after the primitive gcd is removed.


### 34.4 Exact plus/minus twin for Euler and space conditions


The same coordinates give


```text
E_- := (C^2-D^2)(C^2A^2-D^2B^2) = K^2,
```


while the space-diagonal numerator is


```text
E_+ := (C^2+D^2)(C^2A^2+D^2B^2).
```


If `G` is the primitive common gcd of the six raw coordinates, then


```text
G^2 R^2 = E_+.
```


Thus M3 and the final perfect-cuboid predicate become an exact `minus/minus` versus `plus/plus` pair in the same bidegree-`(2,2)` integral coordinates.


This is a useful reformulation of the physical-height wall: the Euclidean height is no longer an opaque nonlinear function of four raw Master-Hit variables.


---


## 35. Primitive normalization and a new full-2-descent / squareclass allocation


### 35.1 Corrected primitive gcd formula


A provisional formula written during the continuation missed a 2-adic factor and is superseded here.


Let


```text
h = gcd(P,Q) in {1,2},
c0 = gcd(C,B),
d0 = gcd(D,A).
```


Then the primitive common divisor of the raw sextuple is


```text
G = epsilon * c0 * d0,
```


with


```text
epsilon = 2h   if C,D are both odd,
epsilon = 1    if C,D have opposite parity.
```


This formula was derived prime-by-prime and cross-checked on exact small Euler hits. Future work should use this version, not the earlier provisional `epsilon=2` statement.


Write


```text
C=c0*c,
B=c0*b,
D=d0*d,
A=d0*a.
```


Then the primitive physical formulas simplify to


```text
R^2 = (C^2+D^2)(c^2 a^2+d^2 b^2) / epsilon^2,
```


and


```text
a1^2 = (C^2-D^2)(c^2 a^2-d^2 b^2) / epsilon^2.
```


So after the cross-gcds are stripped off, both the Euler condition and the physical height are products of two binary quadratic expressions.


### 35.2 Common signed squarefree kernel delta


Because the Euler product is a square, there is a signed squarefree integer `delta` such that


```text
C^2-D^2       = delta * r^2,
c^2 a^2-d^2 b^2 = delta * s^2
```


for suitable rationals/integers after the harmless 2-adic normalization. The important point is that `delta` is not arbitrary.


Factor


```text
F1=C-D,
F2=C+D,
F3=CA-DB,
F4=CA+DB.
```


For every odd prime, the cross-gcds satisfy


```text
gcd(F1,F3)_odd | Q,
gcd(F2,F4)_odd | Q,
gcd(F1,F4)_odd | P,
gcd(F2,F3)_odd | P,
```


while


```text
gcd(F1,F2)_odd = 1
```


under `gcd(C,D)=1`, and an odd prime cannot divide three or four of the `F_i` simultaneously (the only possible common factors would force an odd prime into both `A` and `B`, impossible after the primitive Pythagorean normalization).


Consequently


```text
delta_odd | rad(PQ).
```


This is a strong restriction: for a fixed genuine Pythagorean base `(L,M)`, the number of possible odd squareclasses is at most


```text
2^{omega(PQ)} = (PQ)^{o(1)}.
```


Including sign / the 2-adic choice changes only a bounded factor.


### 35.3 Four-edge squareclass allocation


The same gcd graph gives a universal-torsor-like four-edge allocation. Up to the controlled 2-part, the squarefree labels can be distributed on the four factors so that primes from `Q` only travel across the `(F1,F3)` or `(F2,F4)` edges, while primes from `P` only travel across `(F1,F4)` or `(F2,F3)`.


Equivalently, one can choose pairwise-compatible squarefree labels `q1,q2|Q_odd` and `p1,p2|P_odd` with schematic pattern


```text
sf_odd(F1) ~ q1 p1,
sf_odd(F2) ~ p2 q2,
sf_odd(F3/repeated-gcd) ~ q1 p2,
sf_odd(F4/repeated-gcd) ~ p1 q2.
```


This explains why the new `delta` is a finite squareclass-allocation problem over the base, rather than a completely free squareclass.


### 35.4 Each fixed-delta equation is individually genus 0, but the coupled slice is not automatically toric


For fixed `delta`, the first equation


```text
C^2-D^2 = delta r^2
```


is a conic and has the standard parametrization


```text
C : D : r
 = f^2+delta e^2 : f^2-delta e^2 : 2ef.
```


The second difference-of-squares equation has the same form after setting its two linear variables appropriately.


However, imposing simultaneously the base identities linking `(a,b)` back to `(P,Q)` leaves a genus-1 core in general. Therefore the statement


```text
"delta slice = two conics, hence immediate Davenport/toric counting"
```


is false. The descent is still useful because it reduces the squareclass complexity to only `(PQ)^{o(1)}` slices, but a uniform counting theorem on those coupled slices is still needed.


This is currently one of the most promising routes around the Class-3 wall.

## 36. Routes checked and not promoted


### 36.1 Rank-2 / fixed elliptic-fiber approach is structural, not yet a physical counting theorem


The transverse-fibration calculation suggests a quadratic-base-change description and a small new Mordell-Weil part over the geometric function field. This is interesting geometry, but it does not by itself count the rational base specializations that are actually occupied. The exact census (Section 33.1) reinforces this: M3 growth is spread over many fibers.


Do not promote geometric Mordell-Weil rank information to a physical M3 asymptotic without a family-uniform specialization/height theorem.


### 36.2 Existing toric/K3 height-counting theorems remain near-misses


The physical model now exposes a very natural `(2,2)` height on the rational quotient, but M3 points are still selected by the degree-2 K3 condition `E_-=square`. Ambient toric equidistribution still does not automatically become the exact primitive/canonical M3 measure. The core firewall from the original brief remains in force.


### 36.3 The old Section-6 local theorem was already done


During this continuation, the local conditional law was independently rediscovered in the divisor coordinates. This is **not new credit**: Section 6 already proves


```text
Pr(H square | M square) = 1/2 + O(p^{-1/2})
```


on the Master-Hit local parameter measure. The new work is the physical/global adapter, not another local character-sum proof.


---


## 37. Low-degree curves: a useful separation between the M3 denominator and the perfect-cuboid numerator


This section uses the published / independently verified Testa--Stoll low-degree geometry of the cuboid surface.


Let


```text
pi : S -> K_c
```


be the degree-2 map from the full perfect-cuboid surface `S` to the Euler-brick K3 quotient `K_c`, obtained by forgetting the space-diagonal coordinate.


Testa--Stoll prove (and their public Magma verification checks) that the full surface `S` has **no integral degree-6 curves**.


### 37.1 Any fixed degree-6 M3 curve has a non-split space-diagonal cover


Let `C subset K_c` be an integral degree-6 curve carrying physical Euler-brick points. If the restricted double cover


```text
pi^{-1}(C) -> C
```


split generically, one component would map birationally with degree 1 to `C`. Since the hyperplane coordinates on `S` and `K_c` are the same six edge/face coordinates under `pi`, that component would be an integral degree-6 curve on `S`, contradicting the Testa--Stoll theorem.


Therefore


```text
the space-diagonal double cover is non-split on every integral degree-6 curve C in K_c.
```


Equivalently, on a rational normalization of such a curve, the space condition is represented by a genuinely nonsquare rational function, not a square in `Q(C)`.


### 37.2 Perfect points on a fixed critical degree-6 curve are automatically thinner


Let `D` be the integral dominating component of the restricted cover. Since `D->C` has degree 2 and the hyperplane pulls back, one has


```text
deg(D)=12.
```


Hence, for a fixed `C`:


- if `D` is rational, standard height counting gives `O(B^{2/12}) = O(B^{1/6})` rational points of physical height at most `B`;
- if `D` has genus 1, a fixed Mordell-Weil group gives only polylogarithmic growth;
- if `D` has genus at least 2, there are only finitely many rational points.


Thus every fixed degree-6 M3 curve contributes at most


```text
O(B^{1/6+o(1)})
```


perfect-cuboid candidates, whereas the certified global denominator satisfies


```text
M3(B) >> B^{1/3}.
```


Therefore any **fixed finite collection** of such critical degree-6 curves is negligible in `P(B)/M3(B)`.


This does not prove that all of M3 is controlled by finitely many degree-6 curves; it says something more targeted and rigorous: low-degree accumulating curves can be separated off on the **terminal numerator side** without solving the full M3 equidistribution problem first.


### 37.3 Local consequence on a fixed rational degree-6 curve


If `C` is rational and `t` is a rational parameter on its normalization, the restricted space condition has the form


```text
y^2 = f_C(t)
```


with `f_C` nonsquare in `Q(t)` by 37.1. For every good odd prime, the usual character-sum / Weil bound therefore gives


```text
#{t mod p : f_C(t) square} / #C(F_p)
 = 1/2 + O_C(p^{-1/2}).
```


So the potentially dangerous `B^{1/3}`-scale rational curves are not invisible to the space-square sieve: their final double cover is genuinely nontrivial.


This is useful later if the physical M3 population is decomposed into accumulating curves plus a Zariski-dense residual part.


---


## 38. Current status after the continuation and recommended next task


### 38.1 What is genuinely new after Section 31


The main new items are:


1. **Exact odd-edge divisor physical coordinates** with one-object/one-count, exact primitivity, exact third-face condition, and exact `R<=B` inequality (Section 32).
2. **Removal of the invalid raw Master-Hit square-box truncation** (Section 32.1).
3. **Large exact physical census through `B=10^8`**, showing many-fiber and broad-g behavior (Section 33).
4. **Explicit completion of the Section-31 quotient**, including its quadratic equation, genuine Pythagorean-base bidegree-(4,4) model, and an integral bidegree-(2,2) physical model (Section 34).
5. **Correct primitive gcd normalization and restricted squareclass descent**, with `delta_odd | rad(PQ)` and only `(PQ)^{o(1)}` squareclass slices (Section 35).
6. **A terminal-side theorem for fixed degree-6 M3 curves**: their space-diagonal cover cannot split because the full cuboid surface has no integral sextics; hence their perfect lifts are `O(B^{1/6+o(1)})` (Section 37).


### 38.2 What is still open


The central Class-3 problem is not solved. The residual problem is now more sharply stated:


> Prove fixed-modulus / finite-prime equidistribution, or at least a strong relative sieve inequality, for the **exact physical** primitive M3 population after the Euler condition is imposed, preferably using either the odd-edge divisor height or the `(L,M;C,D)` / `delta` descent. The error must be summable over the relevant squareclass/gcd slices without assuming an unproved lower bound such as `M3(B) >> B^{1/2-epsilon}`.


The local law itself is already available (Section 6). The missing ingredient is the global physical weighting.


### 38.3 Recommended next route for Claude


Highest priority:


```text
A. Start from the exact Section-35 delta decomposition.
B. Fix a modulus q and a signed squarefree delta with delta_odd|rad(PQ).
C. Parameterize the first conic exactly and write the remaining coupled genus-1 condition plus the exact physical height in those parameters.
D. Seek a fixed-q counting statement whose relative error is uniform enough in delta and in the cross-gcd labels.
E. Only after obtaining such a slice estimate, sum over the (PQ)^{o(1)} delta/allocation choices.
```


A secondary route is to split off explicitly classified low-degree accumulating curves (Section 37) and prove the physical congruence theorem only for the Zariski-dense residual population. This may be easier than demanding one theorem that simultaneously handles the accumulating curves and the generic K3 population.


### 38.4 Standing cautions for the next session


- Do **not** use `|r^2-s^2|<=B => r,s=O(sqrt(B))`.
- Do **not** recalculate Section 6's local `1/2+O(p^{-1/2})` law as if it were new.
- Do **not** identify the new Section-34 `(4,4)` model with the old toric-host `(4,4)` model without an explicit birational map.
- Do **not** promote the finite `B<=10^8` census or the `k<=10^6` exceptional search to global theorems.
- Do **not** treat two individual conics in a fixed-delta slice as independent: their coupling retains a genus-1 core.
- Keep the exact physical `R<=B`, primitive normalization, canonical counting, and same-M3 measure firewall at every step.

## 0. 現在のゴール
primitive canonical Euler brick 集団 `M3(B)` の exact physical measure
- primitive
- canonical ordering

- one object = one count
- 3 face diagonals integral
を保ったまま、perfect endpoint の追加条件を `o(M3(B))` まで薄くする。

`M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness`:
`#{E in M3(B): L_-(E) is a square} = o(M3(B))`
である。
ここで unique odd edge を `X`、even edges を `Y,Z`、対応する odd face diagonals を `P,Q` とし
`G=gcd(PQ,YZ)`,

したがって `L_-` だけを density-zero にできれば `P(B)/M3(B)->0` が従う。

## 1. Accepted foundations — 再証明しなくてよい
### Physical / Master coverage
Every primitive Euler brick は unique odd edge を共有する2本の primitive Pythagorean triples から
gcd-normalized Master-Hit として exact に回収される。
`(U1,V1,W1)=(r^2-s^2,2rs,r^2+s^2)`
`(U2,V2,W2)=(m^2-n^2,2mn,m^2+n^2)`
`g=gcd(U1,U2)`
`X=U1 U2/g`
`Y=V1 U2/g`
`Z=U1 V2/g`.
parameter multiplicity は physical brick あたり Y/Z swap の exactly 2。
### M3 / endpoint quantities
Third-face condition:
`M=(V1 U2)^2+(U1 V2)^2` is square.
Space quantity:
`H=(U1 U2)^2+(V1 U2)^2+(U1 V2)^2`.
`M3` は `M square` population。
perfect endpoint はさらに `H square`。
### Physical-counting firewall
ambient parameter-space / toric / finite-field density をそのまま physical `M3(B)` density に昇格しない。
`R<=B`, primitive, canonical, gcd-normalization を保つ same-measure transfer が必要。
### Known global scale
`M3(B)` の precise asymptotic / true exponent は未知。
既知の lower/upper corridors は研究の背景として使えるが、
それを勝手に `M3(B) ~ B^alpha` と置かない。
### Fixed-fiber firewall
fixed source / fixed elliptic fiber の finiteness、rank、Mordell-Weil enumeration は
uniform moving-family count を意味しない。

# ACTIVE DETAILED RECORD
以下は現在も直接参照する recent detailed sections。
Section39 と Section89 以降を保持する。
## 39. Uniform anti-concentration on the rational `t=a2/a3` elliptic fibers
This section gives a rigorous replacement for the earlier empirical statement that the growth of `M3(B)` comes from many moving fibers rather than from a few high-rank fibers.  The key input is Marta Dujella's uniform bounded-height theorem for elliptic curves with rational prime-order torsion (Acta Arith. 217 (2025), arXiv:2312.03655).
### 39.1 External theorem used
Dujella proves the following uniform statement.  If an elliptic curve `E/k` has a `k`-rational point of exact prime order `ell`, then the number of rational points of bounded exponential height is
```text
exp( O_{k,ell}( log B / log log B ) )
```
once the height cutoff `B` is at least `exp(max{h(E),e})`.  Her Corollary 1.3 gives the same quality of bound when the cutoff is imposed on the Weil height of the `x`-coordinate.  For the present application `k=Q` and `ell=2`, so the implied constant is absolute.
### 39.2 Exact adapter to the K_c fibration
Recall the verified fibration from Sections 21--27:
```text
E_t : Y^2 = X^3 + (4t^2-2)X^2 + X,
X=u^2,
```
where `t=a2/a3` and a physical point has
```text
u = (b2-a1)/a3 = a3/(b2+a1),
```
with `0<u<1`.  Every nonsingular fiber has the rational point `(0,0)` of exact order 2.  Hence Dujella's theorem applies uniformly to every physical `t`-fiber.
For a primitive canonical Euler brick of physical height `R<=B`, all edge and face-diagonal coordinates satisfy
```text
0 < a_i <= B,   0 < b_i <= B.
```
Therefore
```text
H(u) <= 2B,
H(X=u^2) <= 4B^2.
```
Also `t=a2/a3` in lowest terms has `H(t)<=B`, and for
```text
P_t = 4t^2-2
```
one has the crude but sufficient bound
```text
H(P_t) <= 6B^2,
```
so the Weierstrass-equation height of `E_t` is `h(E_t)=O(log B)` uniformly over all physical fibers occurring below height `B`.
Taking, for example, the Dujella `x`-height cutoff to be `24B^2`, both requirements are satisfied for all sufficiently large `B`:
```text
H(X) <= 4B^2 <= 24B^2,
24B^2 >= exp(h(E_t)).
```
The map from a primitive positive physical brick in a fixed `t`-fiber to its point on `E_t(Q)` is injective after the fixed positive-sign convention: `t` and `u` recover all edge ratios, and primitivity fixes the common scale.
Consequently, if
```text
N_t(B) := #{ E in M3(B) : a2/a3 = t },
```
then uniformly in every physical rational `t`,
```text
N_t(B)
 <= exp( C log B / log log B )
 = B^{o(1)},
```
for an absolute effective constant `C`.
This is a genuine uniform theorem; it does not assume a rank bound for the moving family and does not require knowing `rank(E_t(Q))` fiber by fiber.
### 39.3 Many occupied fibers are forced
Let
```text
T(B) := { t=a2/a3 : some E in M3(B) has height <=B }.
```
Since
```text
M3(B) = sum_{t in T(B)} N_t(B)
```
and the certified lower bound gives `M3(B) >> B^(1/3)`, Section 39.2 implies
```text
#T(B) >= B^(1/3-o(1)).
```
Thus the earlier numerical observation that most of the growth comes from moving fibers is now replaced by a rigorous statement: no finite, polylogarithmic, or `B^{o(1)}` collection of `t`-fibers can carry a positive proportion of the physical M3 population.
More quantitatively, for every fixed `epsilon>0`, any family `A(B)` of rational `t`-values satisfying
```text
#A(B) <= B^(1/3-epsilon)
```
contributes only
```text
sum_{t in A(B)} N_t(B)
 <= B^(1/3-epsilon+o(1))
 = o(M3(B)).
```
### 39.4 What this does and does not solve
This is a real advance on the physical-measure side, but it is not yet Target A/B equidistribution.
It proves **fiber anti-concentration** under the exact physical height: any bias or failure of local equidistribution in `M3(B)` must be spread over at least `B^(1/3-o(1))` genuinely different Pythagorean base fibers.  In particular, the Class-3 wall cannot be caused by a bounded collection of exceptional high-rank fibers.
It does **not** prove that the occupied `t`-values themselves are equidistributed modulo a fixed modulus; `B^(1/3-o(1))` occupied fibers could still, in principle, be arithmetically biased.  The remaining task is therefore a moving-base distribution theorem, not a fixed-fiber Mordell--Weil theorem.
### 39.5 Updated tactical consequence
The useful decomposition is now:
```text
fixed/low-complexity exceptional curves        -> Section 37, terminally negligible;
any <= B^(1/3-epsilon) collection of t-fibers -> Section 39, M3-negligible;
remaining population                           -> genuinely many moving fibers.
```
Hence future attempts at the physical local-to-global theorem should spend no effort trying to classify a few special fibers.  They must exploit arithmetic cancellation/distribution across a polynomially large moving family, most naturally through the Section-35 squareclass descent or through a two-transverse-fibration incidence formulation.
---
# 【統合追記】SECTION89
# K16-C3-M3-LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL — PART2 SECTION89
## 2026-09-26
## Canonical physical factorization of the terminal condition and the one-factor density receiver
### 0. Main advance
The Stage35/Peschmann E1 factorization and the Section86 primitive terminal
factorization are the same arithmetic decomposition.
More importantly, the two factors can be defined directly on a primitive
physical Euler brick, without any Master-tuple coordinates.
Let a primitive Euler brick be canonically labeled so that `X` is its unique
odd edge and `Y,Z` are the two even edges. Let
`P^2=X^2+Y^2`,
`Q^2=X^2+Z^2`,
`D^2=Y^2+Z^2`
be the three integral face diagonals.
Put
`G = gcd(PQ,YZ)`,
and define
`L_- = (PQ-YZ)/G`,
`L_+ = (PQ+YZ)/G`.
Then:
1. `L_-,L_+` are positive odd coprime integers;
2. the terminal identity is
   `L_- L_+ = (X R/G)^2`;
3. a perfect endpoint is equivalent to
   `L_-` and `L_+` both being integer squares;
4. therefore the single condition `L_-=square` is a canonical necessary
   condition for a perfect endpoint;
5. proving that `L_-=square` has density zero in `M3` is already enough for
   `P(B)/M3(B)->0`.
This creates a new minimal receiver:
`M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness`.
It is strictly weaker than Peschmann E1 nonexistence and weaker than the
Section88 terminal-character contraction theorem.
---
## 1. Direct physical factorization
For every Euler brick,
`P^2=X^2+Y^2`,
`Q^2=X^2+Z^2`.
Therefore
`P^2 Q^2 - Y^2 Z^2
 = (X^2+Y^2)(X^2+Z^2)-Y^2Z^2
 = X^4+X^2Y^2+X^2Z^2
 = X^2(X^2+Y^2+Z^2)`.
Writing
`R^2=X^2+Y^2+Z^2`,
we get the exact identity
### Theorem 89-A — PhysicalTerminalDifferenceOfProducts
`(PQ-YZ)(PQ+YZ)=X^2 R^2`.
No parametrization is involved.
---
## 2. Coprime normalized factors
For a primitive Euler brick the unique odd edge is `X`; the other two edges
`Y,Z` are even. The two face diagonals `P,Q` adjoining `X` are odd.
Thus
`PQ` is odd,
`YZ` is even.
Let
`G=gcd(PQ,YZ)`.
Then `G` is odd. Put
`A=PQ/G`,
`B=YZ/G`.
Now
`gcd(A,B)=1`,
`A` is odd,
`B` is even.
Hence
`L_-=A-B`,
`L_+=A+B`
are positive odd integers and
`gcd(L_-,L_+)
 =gcd(A-B,A+B)
 =1`.
The positivity of `L_-` follows from
`P>Y`,
`Q>Z`,
so `PQ>YZ`.
Thus:
### Theorem 89-B — CanonicalCoprimeTerminalFactors
For every primitive physical Euler brick,
`L_-=(PQ-YZ)/G`,
`L_+=(PQ+YZ)/G`
are positive odd coprime integers.
They are invariant under the swap `Y<->Z`, `P<->Q`.
So they are canonical invariants of the physical Euler brick once the
unique odd edge has been selected.
---
## 3. Exact endpoint iff both factors are squares
Divide Theorem89-A by `G^2`:
`L_- L_+ = X^2 R^2/G^2`.
If `R` is an integer, the right side is a rational square. The left side
is an integer, hence an integer square. Since `L_-` and `L_+` are coprime
positive integers, each is an integer square.
Conversely, if
`L_-=r^2`,
`L_+=s^2`,
then
`R^2=(G r s/X)^2`
is a rational square. But `R^2` is an integer. Therefore `R` is rational,
and a rational number whose square is an integer is an integer.
Hence:
### Theorem 89-C — PhysicalCoprimeEndpointFactorization
For a primitive physical Euler brick,
`R in Z`
iff
`L_- in Z^2`
and
`L_+ in Z^2`.
This is a purely physical M3 theorem.
No Master-Hit coverage theorem is needed to state or prove it.
---
## 4. Exact identification with the Master-Hit / E1 factors
Use the unique-odd-edge Master-Hit coordinates
`U1,V1,W1`,
`U2,V2,W2`
and
`g=gcd(U1,U2)`,
with primitive physical brick
`X=U1U2/g`,
`Y=V1U2/g`,
`Z=U1V2/g`.
The adjacent physical face diagonals are
`P=W1U2/g`,
`Q=U1W2/g`.
Therefore
`PQ=(X/g) W1W2`,
`YZ=(X/g) V1V2`.
Consequently
`gcd(PQ,YZ)
 =(X/g) gcd(W1W2,V1V2)`.
Stage35-EX proves
`gcd(W1W2,V1V2)
 = p d`,
where
`p=gcd(W1,V2)`,
`d=gcd(V1,W2)`.
Thus
`G=(X/g) p d`.
It follows immediately that
`(PQ-YZ)/G
 =(W1W2-V1V2)/(pd)`,
`(PQ+YZ)/G
 =(W1W2+V1V2)/(pd)`.
Hence the physical factors are exactly the Stage35 factors:
### Theorem 89-D — E1PhysicalFactorDictionary
`L_-^physical = L_-^Stage35`,
`L_+^physical = L_+^Stage35`.
The E1 receiver is therefore not merely a source-coordinate obstruction.
It is a canonical factorization of the physical terminal condition.
---
## 5. Exact identification with Section86
Section86 defined
`g_- = gcd(am-bn,an-bm)`,
`g_+ = gcd(am+bn,an+bm)`
and primitive Gaussian norms
`N_-`,
`N_+`.
It proved
`R^2=N_-N_+`
and
`h=gcd(N_-,N_+)
  =(W1,V2)(W2,V1)
  =p d`.
Write
`N_-=h A`,
`N_+=h B`,
`gcd(A,B)=1`.
Also
`W1W2-V1V2 = g_-^2 N_-`,
`W1W2+V1V2 = g_+^2 N_+`.
Therefore
`L_-=g_-^2 A`,
`L_+=g_+^2 B`.
So:
### Theorem 89-E — Section86E1Unification
`L_- square <=> A square`,
`L_+ square <=> B square`.
The Section86 endpoint split packet
`N_-=h r^2`,
`N_+=h s^2`
is exactly the Stage35/Peschmann two-factor E1 receiver.
These are not separate research routes and must not be double-counted.
---
## 6. The one-factor receiver
Every perfect endpoint satisfies
`L_-=square`.
Define
`G_-(B)
 = #{E in M3(B): L_-(E) is an integer square}`.
Then pointwise
`P(B) <= G_-(B) <= M3(B)`.
Therefore:
### Theorem 89-F — OneFactorSuffices
If
`G_-(B)/M3(B) -> 0`,
then
`P(B)/M3(B) -> 0`.
Call the required theorem
### M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness
`#{E in M3(B): (PQ-YZ)/gcd(PQ,YZ) is square}
  = o(M3(B)).`
This is strictly weaker than:
- perfect-endpoint nonexistence;
- Peschmann's universal E1 conjecture;
- proving both `L_-` and `L_+` square impossible;
- full terminal-character equidistribution;
- full Euler-K3 residue-class equidistribution.
---
## 7. Why this is a real reduction, not a rebranding
The previous Route-B receivers tracked the terminal square
`R^2=e^2+z^2`
directly.
The new condition discards the entire residual factor `L_+`.
A point may satisfy `L_-=square` while not being terminal.
Indeed the Stage35 bounded Master-Hit diagnostic found 131 Master-Hits, of
which exactly three satisfy `L_-=square`, while none satisfies the residual
`L_+=square`.
This is finite evidence only, but it shows the one-factor over-cover is
strict in actual source data.
The target has therefore been genuinely enlarged and weakened.
---
## 8. Important firewall: do NOT import the full-E1 local routing automatically
Stage35-EX 35EX-10/11 derives strong prime-routing constraints under a
hypothetical full E1 counterexample.
Many of those deductions use both square factors or the primitive
Pythagorean triple forced by the full terminal norm.
They cannot automatically be imposed on the larger population
`Master-Hit + L_-=square`.
Therefore:
`FULL_E1_LOCAL_ROUTING -> ONE_FACTOR_COUNT`
is not a direct reuse.
A fresh one-factor local analysis is required.
The part that does transfer exactly is only the Stage35-EX 35EX-13/14
identity
`Gaussian source square condition <=> L_-=square`.
Its bounded `3/131` survival is diagnostic only.
---
## 9. Relation to the Section88 character receiver
Section88 reduced terminality to the single physical observable
`H=X^2+Y^2+Z^2`.
Section89 gives a different one-observable necessary condition
`Lambda_-(E)
 =(PQ-YZ)/gcd(PQ,YZ)`.
They should not be multiplied as independent filters.
There are now two competing minimal density receivers:
### Route B1 — base character
`AccumulationSafeInertCharacterGap(H)`.
### Route B2 — canonical minus factor
`M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness(Lambda_-)`.
B2 is attractive because it needs only one square factor rather than the
whole terminal square and because its exact arithmetic factorization is
already exposed in the Master-Hit/Gaussian coordinates.
B1 is attractive because its fixed-prime local indicator is elementary.
Neither currently implies the other.
---
## 10. New local form of the one-factor condition
In Master-Hit variables,
`L_-=(W1W2-V1V2)/(pd)`.
Equivalently,
`W1W2-V1V2=(am-bn)^2+(an-bm)^2`.
Thus
`L_-=square`
asks that a canonically gcd-normalized sum of two squares be a square.
This is a primitive-Pythagorean condition on one Gaussian norm after the
cross-gcd normalization.
The correct next question is not whether this is ever possible — it is.
The M3 question is:
> among Master-Hits under the exact physical height, how often does this
> additional primitive Gaussian norm become a square?
This suggests a square-sieve / thin-cover count on the Master-Hit marginal.
But no same-measure theorem is currently proved.
---
## 11. Relation to old conditioned-character work
The Stage14 S-route developed character decompositions and discrepancy
receivers for a different already-space-integral physical population.
Its principal-vs-nonprincipal character machinery cannot be imported merely
because a quadratic character reappears here.
The old route itself stopped at an external discrepancy/equidistribution
gate when the conditioned measure was too thin and witness-dependent.
Therefore its correct reuse class is:
`SAME_ANALYTIC_SPECIES_DIFFERENT_MEASURE`.
It supplies warnings and possible analytic templates, not a theorem.
The new M3 receiver is simpler in one respect:
the modulus may be kept fixed and the arithmetic observable is canonical on
each physical Euler brick.
But the Master-Hit / third-face-square conditioning remains the unresolved
measure issue.
---
## 12. Revised Route-B priority after Section89
Primary:
`M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness`.
Suggested attacks:
A. derive the exact fixed-prime distribution of `L_-` on the Master-Hit
marginal and look for a sequential square-residue contraction;
B. formulate `Master-Hit + L_-=square` as an explicit double-cover /
fiber-product over the Stage15 toric base, then determine its physical
polarization and low-degree accumulating curves;
C. use the Section86 primitive Gaussian norm
`N_-=h A` and seek a square-sieve directly on the coprime cofactor `A`,
where the cross-gcd square has already been removed.
Secondary:
`AccumulationSafeInertCharacterGap(H)`.
Independent fallback:
Section79 primitive-shape/two-twist absolute route.
---
## 13. Current exact state
New exact results:

# 【統合追記】SECTION90–95
# K16-C3-M3-LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL — PART2 SECTION90
## 2026-09-26
## One-factor receiver: physical four-gcd skeleton, moving-prime sieve, and fixed-source genus-3 fibers
### Status
Section89 reduced the M3 density target to
`M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness`:
`L_-=(PQ-YZ)/gcd(PQ,YZ)` is a square on a density-zero subset of `M3`.
Section90 analyzes `L_-=square` alone.  No `L_+` square and no full E1
counterexample assumption is used unless explicitly stated.
No proof of `P(B)/M3(B)->0` is claimed.
---
## 1. Canonical four-gcd skeleton on a physical Euler brick
Let `X` be the unique odd edge of a primitive Euler brick, with even edges
`Y,Z`.  Let
`P^2=X^2+Y^2`,
`Q^2=X^2+Z^2`.
Define the four cross gcds
`g1=gcd(P,Y)`,
`g2=gcd(Q,Z)`,
`p =gcd(P,Z)`,
`d =gcd(Q,Y)`.
### Theorem 90-A — PhysicalFourGcdSkeleton
The four integers
`g1,g2,p,d`
are pairwise coprime and
`gcd(PQ,YZ)=g1*g2*p*d`.
Proof is primewise.
If an odd prime divided two different channels, then it would divide enough
of `P,Q,Y,Z` to force it to divide `X` through one of
`P^2=X^2+Y^2`,
`Q^2=X^2+Z^2`,
contradicting primitive normalization.  Every prime dividing
`gcd(PQ,YZ)` must lie in exactly one of the four possible intersections
`P/Y`, `Q/Z`, `P/Z`, `Q/Y`, so valuations add exactly.
This agrees with the Master-Hit dictionary
`g1=U2/g`,
`g2=U1/g`,
`p=gcd(W1,V2)`,
`d=gcd(V1,W2)`.
---
## 2. Primitive rectangular core
Define
`r=P/(g1*p)`,
`s=Z/(g2*p)`,
`u=Q/(g2*d)`,
`v=Y/(g1*d)`.
Then
`P=g1*p*r`,
`Z=g2*p*s`,
`Q=g2*d*u`,
`Y=g1*d*v`.
The four-gcd maximality gives
`gcd(ru,vs)=1`.
More specifically,
`gcd(r,s)=gcd(r,v)=gcd(u,s)=gcd(u,v)=1`.
The only same-side gcds `gcd(r,u)` and `gcd(v,s)` need not be one
individually, but no prime can occur in both products `ru` and `vs`.
Since `P,Q` are odd, `r,u` are odd.
Since the even leg of a primitive Pythagorean triple is divisible by four
and every scale dividing the odd shared edge is odd,
`4|v`,
`4|s`.
Hence `16|vs`.
Now Section89's physical factors become
### Theorem 90-B — PrimitiveBilinearFactorization
`L_-=ru-vs`,
`L_+=ru+vs`.
Thus the one-factor receiver is exactly
`x^2=ru-vs`
with
`gcd(ru,vs)=1`,
`ru` odd,
`16|vs`.
This is a canonical physical formulation; no Master coordinates are needed
to state it.
---
## 3. Immediate 2-adic necessary condition
If
`x^2=ru-vs`,
then `x` is odd and `16|vs`.  Therefore
`ru == x^2 mod 16`.
In particular
### Theorem 90-C — OneFactor2AdicBit
`L_-=square  =>  ru == 1 mod 8`.
Equivalently,
`(2/(ru))=+1`
in Kronecker/Legendre notation.
This is only one fixed bit and cannot by itself prove density zero.
Finite diagnostic only:
on one bounded Master-Hit panel of 230 hits, `ru mod 8` was
`1` on 96 hits,
`5` on 134 hits,
while only 4 hits had `L_-=square`.
So the observed thinness is much stronger than the mod-8 filter.
No asymptotic inference is made from these counts.
---
## 4. Moving split-prime residue sieve
Every odd prime dividing `r` or `u` divides the primitive hypotenuse of a
Pythagorean triple after removal of cross gcds.  Hence every such prime is
`1 mod 4`.
Let `ell|ru` be odd.  Since `gcd(ru,vs)=1`, reducing
`x^2=ru-vs`
modulo `ell` gives
`x^2 == -vs mod ell`.
Since `(-1/ell)=+1`,
### Theorem 90-D — MovingHypotenusePrimeCondition
For every odd `ell|ru`,
`(vs/ell)=+1`.
Likewise, for every odd `ell|vs`,
`(ru/ell)=+1`.
Thus a one-factor point must satisfy a prime-by-prime quadratic-residue
compatibility at every moving prime divisor of the primitive rectangular
core.
Heuristically this resembles a factor
`2^{-omega(ru)}`
of survival, but **no independence theorem is proved** and this heuristic
must not be promoted.
Global quadratic reciprocity alone gives no contradiction: the prime
supports move and the relations can be mutually compatible.
The useful feature for M3 is quantitative rather than existential:
if a sieve can average these moving residue conditions under physical
height, any logarithmic decay would already be enough for the relative
density target.
---
## 5. Face-divisor crosswalk to the Section74/79 shape route
For the two faces containing the odd edge `X`, define
`A=P-Y`,
`C=Q-Z`.
Then `A,C` are positive odd divisors of `X^2` and
`P=(X^2+A^2)/(2A)`,
`Y=(X^2-A^2)/(2A)`,
`Q=(X^2+C^2)/(2C)`,
`Z=(X^2-C^2)/(2C)`.
Direct expansion gives
### Theorem 90-E — ShapeFormOfRawTerminalFactors
`PQ-YZ = X^2(A^2+C^2)/(2AC)`,
`PQ+YZ = (X^4+A^2 C^2)/(2AC)`.
Write
`d0=gcd(A,C)`,
`A=d0*a`,
`C=d0*c`,
`gcd(a,c)=1`,
and
`k=X^2/lcm(A,C)=X^2/(d0*a*c)`.
Then all `d0,a,c,k` are odd and
`PQ-YZ = d0*k*(a^2+c^2)/2`,
`PQ+YZ = a*c*(d0^2+k^2)/2`.
This is the first exact bridge between the Section89 one-factor route and
the earlier primitive-shape/divisor route.
It shows that the two raw terminal factors are dual sums of two squares:
`(a,c)` on the shape side,
`(d0,k)` on the complementary-scale side.
The exact gcd normalization between these two expressions remains a live
counting object; no pairwise-coprime simplification of `d0,a,c,k` is
asserted.
---
## 6. Fixed first Euclid triple: the one-factor normalization has only finitely many gcd strata
Fix one primitive Euclid triple
`(U,V,W)`,
which will play the first source triple.
Write the second primitive Euclid pair as `(m,n)`:
`U2=m^2-n^2`,
`V2=2mn`,
`W2=m^2+n^2`.
For the one-factor normalization,
`p=gcd(W,V2)` divides the fixed integer `W`,
`d=gcd(V,W2)` divides the fixed integer `V`.
Therefore
`delta=p*d`
ranges over only divisor-many choices depending on the fixed first triple.
This is important: after the first source is fixed, the arithmetic gcd
normalization does **not** create an unbounded new parameter.
---
## 7. Master condition plus one-factor condition
With affine parameter
`t=m/n`,
the Master-Hit / third-face condition is the genus-one quartic
`S^2 = F(t)`,
where
`F(t)=V^2(t^2-1)^2+4U^2 t^2`.
On a fixed gcd stratum `delta=pd`, the one-factor condition is
`X1^2 = Q_delta(t)`
with
`Q_delta(t)=[W(t^2+1)-2Vt]/delta`.
The scalar `delta` changes only the quadratic twist; the branch polynomial
is
`Q(t)=W(t^2+1)-2Vt`.
The exact discriminants/resultant are
`Disc_t(F)=4096 U^4 V^4 (U-V)^2 (U+V)^2`,
`Disc_t(Q)=-4U^2`,
and, using `W^2=U^2+V^2`,
`Res_t(F,Q)=16 U^8`.
For every nondegenerate primitive Pythagorean triple,
`U,V!=0`,
`U!=V`.
Therefore the four branch points of `F` and the two branch points of `Q`
are all simple and disjoint.
---
## 8. Fixed-source one-factor fiber is smooth genus 3
Consider the fiber product
`C_delta:`
`S^2=F(t)`,
`X1^2=Q(t)/delta`.
It is a `(Z/2)^2` cover of `P1_t`.
Its three nontrivial character quotients are:
1. `S^2=F(t)`, genus 1;
2. `X1^2=Q(t)/delta`, genus 0;
3. `Z1^2=F(t)Q(t)/delta`, genus 2.
Hence
### Theorem 90-F — FixedSourceOneFactorGenusThree
For every fixed nondegenerate primitive first Euclid triple and every
admissible fixed `delta=pd`, the normalized curve `C_delta` is a smooth
genus-3 curve.
This uses only
`Master-Hit + L_-=square`.
The residual `L_+` square is not imposed.
By Faltings,
`C_delta(Q)`
is finite for every fixed source triple and fixed gcd stratum.
Since there are only divisor-many `delta`, each fixed first Euclid triple
supports only finitely many one-factor Master-Hits.
Therefore no **fixed first source triple** can be an accumulating
`L_-=square` stratum as `B->infinity`.
This is pointwise/fiberwise finiteness, not a uniform moving-family count.
---
## 9. Reciprocal genus-2 quotient and three-elliptic Jacobian decomposition
The genus-2 quotient is
`Z1^2=F(t)Q(t)/delta`.
Both `F` and `Q` are reciprocal polynomials, so this genus-2 curve has the
involution
`t -> 1/t`.
Put
`X=t+1/t`.
Then
`F(t)/t^2
 = V^2 X^2 + 4(U^2-V^2)`,
`Q(t)/t
 = W X - 2V`.
Using the invariant and anti-invariant combinations of the genus-2
hyperelliptic coordinate gives two elliptic quotients
### Theorem 90-G — ExplicitEllipticQuotients
`E_+:`
`delta*Y_+^2
 =(X+2)(W X-2V)(V^2 X^2+4(U^2-V^2))`,
`E_-:`
`delta*Y_-^2
 =(X-2)(W X-2V)(V^2 X^2+4(U^2-V^2))`.
The roots are pairwise distinct:
- `X=+-2` do not zero the quadratic factor;
- `2V/W` is distinct from `+-2`;
- evaluating the quadratic factor at `2V/W` gives
  `4U^4/W^2 != 0`;
- `U!=V` prevents degeneration of the quadratic factor.
Thus both are smooth genus-one curves.
Consequently
### Theorem 90-H — FixedSourceThreeEllipticSplit
Up to isogeny over `Q`,
`Jac(C_delta)`
is isogenous to
`E_Master x E_+ x E_-`,
where `E_Master` is the elliptic curve represented by `S^2=F(t)`.
This is a lighter analogue of the Stage35 full-receiver genus-5
five-elliptic decomposition.
All three factors still move with the first source triple, so this is not a
fixed-elliptic closure theorem.
---
## 10. Gaussian form of the one-factor receiver
Section86 supplies the primitive Gaussian pair
`(P_-,Q_-)=1`
with norm
`N_-=P_-^2+Q_-^2`.
It also gives the common norm core
`h=gcd(N_-,N_+)=p*d`.
Section89 showed
`L_-=square`
iff
`N_-/h=square`.
Thus on the one-factor locus
`N_-=h*r0^2`.
Because `(P_-,Q_-)=1` and the odd support of `h` is split in `Q(i)`,
unique factorization in `Z[i]` gives, up to the finite 2-adic/unit
convention,
### Theorem 90-I — PrimitiveGaussianSquareAfterCore
`P_-+iQ_- = epsilon * kappa * (u+iv)^2`
with
`Norm(kappa)=h`.
The possible Gaussian orientations of `kappa` are divisor/prime-orientation
many:
`<= 2^omega(h)=B^o(1)`
under the physical height bound.
This is the exact algebraic core of the Stage35 Gaussian sieve
`GAUSS = Lminus`.
It suggests the same two-regime strategy that succeeded in earlier
Stage14 counting work:
- large common core `h`: seek modulus/root-line spacing;
- small common core `h`: use Gaussian-square reconstruction and count the
  remaining source variables.
No exponent saving has yet been proved for the exact M3 physical measure.
---
## 11. What did not close
The following tempting promotions are invalid:
1. Fixed-source genus 3 + Faltings does **not** give a uniform count as the
   first source moves.
2. The elliptic splitting does not give a fixed Mordell-Weil group; all
   quotient curves move with `(U,V,W)`.
3. The moving-prime conditions
   `(vs/ell)=1` for every `ell|ru`
   do not by themselves give an independent `2^{-omega(ru)}` probability.
4. Stage35 full-E1 local-routing theorems cannot be silently imported to
   the larger one-factor population.
5. The physical gcd normalization `G` is arithmetic; `L_-` is not simply a
   globally regular rational function on the Euler K3 without gcd
   stratification.
---
## 12. Revised next receiver
Section90 isolates a sharper quantitative target:
### OneFactorMovingPrimeSieve
For primitive Master-Hits under the exact physical `R<=B` measure, prove
that the simultaneous moving-prime residue conditions forced by
`x^2=ru-vs`
have survival `o(1)` after the finite low-degree accumulator package is
removed.
Equivalent Gaussian formulation:
### OneFactorGaussianCoreSieve
Count Master-Hits for which
`P_-^2+Q_-^2 = h*r0^2`,
`h=gcd(N_-,N_+)`,
and prove that these form `o(M3(B))`.
The second formulation is currently preferred because it exposes:
- a primitive Gaussian pair;
- an exact common core;
- only `B^o(1)` Gaussian orientations after the core is fixed;
- direct compatibility with Stage14 root-line/common-core counting weapons.
---

# SECTION91-95 — one-factor Eulerian residue graph and revised sieve architecture
## 2026-09-26
## Status: provisional research continuation after Section90; theorem credit only where explicitly marked
Section90 reduced the one-factor receiver to
```text
x^2 = ru-vs,
gcd(ru,vs)=1,
ru odd,
16|vs,
```
with every odd prime dividing `ru` congruent to `1 mod 4`, and with the necessary moving-prime conditions
```text
(vs/ell)=+1  for every odd ell|ru,
(ru/q)=+1    for every odd q|vs.
```
The purpose of Sections91--95 is to reorganize these primewise conditions into a finite-dimensional parity obstruction that is better suited to a large-sieve transfer on the exact `M3` physical measure.
---
## 14. Section91 — squarefree-kernel reduction and the Legendre incidence matrix
Put
```text
A=ru,
B=vs.
```
Then
```text
gcd(A,B)=1,
A odd,
16|B,
x^2=A-B.
```
Write the squarefree-kernel decompositions
```text
A = A_sf * a0^2,
B = 2^e * B_sf * b0^2,
```
where
```text
A_sf = product_{ell in S} ell,
B_sf = product_{q in T} q,
```
and `S,T` consist exactly of the odd primes occurring to odd valuation in `A,B` respectively.  Thus `e=v_2(B)` and only `e mod 2` matters for quadratic characters.
Every `ell in S` satisfies
```text
ell == 1 mod 4.
```
Reducing `x^2=A-B` modulo `ell in S` gives
```text
(B/ell)=+1.
```
After deleting square factors this becomes
```text
(2/ell)^(e mod 2) * product_{q in T} (q/ell) = +1.
```
Likewise, for `q in T`, reduction modulo `q` gives
```text
(A/q)=+1,
```
hence
```text
product_{ell in S} (ell/q)=+1.
```
Because every `ell in S` is `1 mod 4`, quadratic reciprocity has no sign:
```text
(ell/q)=(q/ell).
```
Therefore the odd-prime compatibility is encoded by the binary matrix
```text
M_{ell,q}=0  if (q/ell)=+1,
M_{ell,q}=1  if (q/ell)=-1,
```
with rows indexed by `S` and columns by `T`.
For the odd-prime subsystem, one-factor squareness forces
```text
M * 1_T = c_2,
M^T * 1_S = 0,
```
where the `ell`-row correction is
```text
(c_2)_ell = (e mod 2) * [ (2/ell)=-1 ]  in F_2.
```
When `e` is even this simplifies to
```text
M*1_T=0,
M^T*1_S=0.
```
When `e` is odd, adjoining a distinguished right-hand vertex `2` with incidence
```text
M_{ell,2}=1  iff (2/ell)=-1
```
again converts the condition into an even-degree condition at every vertex.
### Theorem 91-A — OneFactorEulerianResidueGraph
For any one-factor point `x^2=ru-vs`, form the bipartite graph whose left vertices are the odd-prime support of the squarefree kernel of `ru`, whose right vertices are the odd-prime support of the squarefree kernel of `vs`, and whose edge `ell--q` is present exactly when
```text
(q/ell)=-1.
```
If `v_2(vs)` is odd, adjoin the distinguished right vertex `2` with edge `ell--2` exactly when `(2/ell)=-1`.
Then every vertex has even degree.
Equivalently, the nonresidue graph lies in the cycle space of the corresponding complete bipartite graph after the deterministic `2`-column is adjoined when needed.
This theorem is an exact reformulation of the Section90 moving-prime residue conditions; no independence or density statement is used.
---
## 15. Section92 — exact codimension of the odd-prime symbol patterns
Ignore the distinguished `2`-column for the moment and suppose
```text
m=|S|>=1,
n=|T|>=1.
```
In the even-`e` case the admissible odd-prime Legendre matrices satisfy
```text
M*1_n=0,
M^T*1_m=0.
```
The space of `m x n` binary matrices with all row sums and all column sums even has dimension
```text
(m-1)(n-1).
```
Indeed there are `m+n` parity equations with exactly one linear dependence, so the codimension is
```text
m+n-1.
```
Hence the exact number of admissible odd-prime sign patterns is
```text
2^((m-1)(n-1))
```
out of
```text
2^(mn)
```
possible binary matrices.
Therefore, under a hypothetical uniform random-symbol model, the formal survival factor is
```text
2^{-(m+n-1)}.
```
This count was independently brute-force checked for `1<=m,n<=4`; the observed counts agree with `2^((m-1)(n-1))` in every tested case.
### Important firewall
The count above is a combinatorial theorem about sign patterns.  It is **not** yet a theorem that the corresponding Legendre symbols are equidistributed on the exact primitive canonical `M3` population under physical height `R<=B`.
The unresolved step is still the same-measure local-to-global transfer.
### Theorem 92-A — OneFactorSymbolCodimension
Conditional on fixed odd squarefree supports `S,T`, the one-factor compatibility occupies a linear subspace of codimension
```text
|S|+|T|-1
```
inside the full odd-prime Legendre sign cube.
The `2`-adic column supplies an affine translate/deterministic extra column when `v_2(vs)` is odd and can only add structure; it does not invalidate the odd-prime codimension statement.
---
## 16. Section93 — low-support patterns are rigid, not merely rare
The cycle-space formulation gives exact small-support classifications.
### Case `m=1`
There are no nonzero cycles in `K_{1,n}`.  Hence every odd-prime edge must be absent:
```text
(q/ell)=+1  for every q in T.
```
If the distinguished `2`-vertex is present, then also
```text
(2/ell)=+1,
```
so in particular
```text
ell == 1 mod 8.
```
### Case `n=1`
Similarly the unique right prime `q` must be a quadratic residue modulo every `ell in S`.
### Case `m=n=2`
The only even-row/even-column matrices are
```text
[0 0]      [1 1]
[0 0]  or  [1 1].
```
Thus either all four cross Legendre symbols are `+1`, or all four are `-1`.
No pattern with exactly one or exactly three nonresidue pairs can occur on the one-factor locus.
### Case `m=2,n=3`
Each column must be either
```text
(0,0)^T
```
or
```text
(1,1)^T,
```
and the number of `(1,1)^T` columns must be even.  Hence only four of the `2^6=64` raw sign patterns survive.
### Consequence
The previous informal split
```text
kernel-rich / kernel-poor
```
should not treat the low-support side merely by a generic "few prime factors are sparse" estimate.  Low support carries increasingly rigid reciprocity configurations that can be attacked arithmetically and can be combined with the Gaussian reconstruction of Section90.
---
## 17. Section94 — revised same-M3 transfer target
The original `K16-C3-M3-LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL` wall asked for equidistribution of correlated local squareclass states on exact primitive canonical `M3` under `R<=B`.
Sections91--93 weaken the amount of local randomness actually needed for the one-factor receiver.
For a Master-Hit define
```text
kappa_res = omega(A_sf)+omega(B_sf),
```
where `A_sf,B_sf` are the odd squarefree kernels of `ru,vs`.
A sufficient rich-support theorem would have the following form.
### Receiver 94-R1 — OneFactorEulerianLargeSieve
For some function
```text
K(B)->infinity
```
as slowly as necessary, prove on the exact primitive canonical `M3` measure that
```text
# { one-factor survivors with kappa_res >= K(B), R<=B }
    / M3(B)
 <= 2^{-K(B)+1} + E(B,K(B)),
```
with
```text
E(B,K(B))->0.
```
The displayed `2^{-K+1}` is the natural cycle-space codimension scale, not a currently proved probability law.
The important reduction is that `K(B)` need only tend to infinity.  It does not need to be comparable to `log log B`.
For example, even a theorem valid for
```text
K(B) = floor(c log log log B)
```
would force the combinatorial main factor to vanish.
This is materially weaker than proving full independence of all moving prime conditions.
### Current theorem status
```text
ONE_FACTOR_EULERIAN_GRAPH=true
ONE_FACTOR_SYMBOL_CODIMENSION=true
SAME_M3_EULERIAN_LARGE_SIEVE=false
GROWING_SUPPORT_TRANSFER=false
```
---
## 18. Section95 — corrected treatment of the kernel-poor side
A tempting but currently unjustified step would be
```text
omega(A_sf)+omega(B_sf) < K(B)
   => density-zero inside M3.
```
This is **not proved** and should not be assumed.
The reason is specific to the exact physical `M3` measure.  Earlier `M3` work showed that the physical cutoff involves the arithmetic normalization
```text
g=gcd(U1,U2),
```
and that `R<=B` may strongly bias the parameter population toward atypically large gcd strata.  Therefore ordinary integer heuristics for the distribution of `omega(n)` cannot be transplanted directly to `M3`.
The low-support side should instead be combined with the Section90 Gaussian representation
```text
P_-+iQ_- = epsilon*kappa*(u+iv)^2,
Norm(kappa)=h,
```
where
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+)
```
and the number of Gaussian orientations of `kappa` is at most
```text
2^omega(h)=B^o(1).
```
This yields the revised two-axis decomposition
```text
                    residue-rich            residue-poor
large h             Eulerian sieve          low-cycle/root-line spacing
                    + core spacing
small h             Eulerian sieve          Gaussian exact reconstruction
```
with the following provisional receivers.
### Receiver 95-R1 — OneFactorEulerianLargeSieve
Use the codimension `m+n-1` of the Legendre cycle-space constraints on the support-rich part.
### Receiver 95-R2 — OneFactorLowCycleGaussianReconstruction
On the support-poor part, exploit simultaneously:
```text
few squarefree-kernel prime vertices,
few admissible Legendre cycle patterns,
B^o(1) Gaussian core orientations,
primitive Gaussian-square reconstruction.
```
The aim is an explicit count under physical `R<=B`, not a generic probabilistic assertion.
### Receiver 95-R3 — OneFactorLargeCoreRootLineSpacing
For large `h`, combine the Gaussian core with Stage14-style modulus/root-line spacing.  The exact admissibility and reuse conditions from Stage14 still need to be source-locked before theorem credit.
### Receiver 95-R4 — OneFactorSmallCoreExactCount
For small `h`, enumerate/reconstruct possible Gaussian square lifts after fixing the core orientation and prove that the resulting source-variable count is `o(M3(B))` or otherwise power/log saving.
---
## 19. Revised preferred architecture after Section95
The one-factor route is now organized as
```text
L_-=square
  |
  +-- exact Eulerian residue graph
  |      |
  |      +-- residue-rich -> OneFactorEulerianLargeSieve
  |      |
  |      +-- residue-poor -> low-cycle arithmetic rigidity
  |
  +-- primitive Gaussian square after common core
         |
         +-- large h -> root-line / modulus spacing
         |
         +-- small h -> exact Gaussian reconstruction
```
The two decompositions are intended to be intersected, not treated as competing routes.
The currently preferred global strategy is therefore a `2 x 2` decomposition by
```text
(residue support rich/poor) x (Gaussian common core large/small).
```
No cell of this table is yet proved to have negligible exact `M3` mass.

Unchanged global firewalls:

### Next research item
The next high-value task is to attack the support-poor / small-core cell first, because it avoids requiring a full same-M3 growing-prime equidistribution theorem and may admit an exact reconstruction/counting argument from the already available Gaussian square form.
Concretely:
```text
NEXT_ITEM=OneFactorLowCycleGaussianReconstruction
```
The first subproblem is to express `P_-+iQ_-=epsilon*kappa*z^2` back in the physical four-gcd / face-divisor variables while keeping `omega(A_sf)+omega(B_sf)` bounded, then determine whether fixing `(h,kappa-orientation,cycle-pattern)` leaves only divisor-many or genuinely one-dimensional source freedom under `R<=B`.

# SECTION96 — physical minus-root modulus and quartic Gaussian root lines
## 2026-09-26
## Status: exact algebra proved below; global density consequence still open
Section95 left the support-poor / small-core cell at
```text
P_- + i Q_- = epsilon * kappa * (u+iv)^2,
Norm(kappa)=h,
```
with `(P_-,Q_-)=1`.
This section finds a second modulus which is not the Gaussian common core `h`.
It comes directly from the physical odd edge.
Let the two primitive Euclid source pairs be
```text
(a,b), (m,n),
U1=a^2-b^2,
U2=m^2-n^2,
g=gcd(U1,U2),
X=U1*U2/g.
```
Define
```text
A_-=am-bn,
B_-=an-bm,
g_-=gcd(A_-,B_-),
P_-=A_-/g_-,
Q_-=B_-/g_-.
```
Thus `gcd(P_-,Q_-)=1`.
## 96.1 Cross gcd divides both odd Euclid legs
The elementary identities
```text
a*A_- + b*B_- = m*U1,
b*A_- + a*B_- = n*U1,
```
and `gcd(m,n)=1` imply
```text
g_- | U1.
```
Likewise
```text
m*A_- - n*B_- = a*U2,
n*A_- - m*B_- = b*U2,
```
and `gcd(a,b)=1` imply
```text
g_- | U2.
```
Hence
### Theorem 96-A — MinusCrossGcdDividesMasterGcd
```text
g_- | gcd(U1,U2)=g.
```
This is exact and uses only primitive Euclid source pairs.
Put
```text
D1=U1/g_-,
D2=U2/g_-.
```
Then
```text
m*D1 = a*P_- + b*Q_-,
n*D1 = b*P_- + a*Q_-,
```
and
```text
a*D2 = m*P_- - n*Q_-,
b*D2 = n*P_- - m*Q_-.
```
## 96.2 A physical modulus dividing P_-^2-Q_-^2
From the first pair,
```text
D1 | a(P_-^2-Q_-^2),
D1 | b(P_-^2-Q_-^2).
```
Since `gcd(a,b)=1`,
```text
D1 | P_-^2-Q_-^2.
```
The second pair similarly gives
```text
D2 | P_-^2-Q_-^2.
```
Now
```text
gcd(D1,D2)=g/g_-,
```
so
```text
lcm(D1,D2)
 = U1*U2/(g*g_-)
 = X/g_-.
```
Define the physical minus-root modulus
```text
C_- := X/g_-.
```
Therefore
### Theorem 96-B — PhysicalMinusRootModulus
```text
C_- | P_-^2-Q_-^2.
```
Because `X,g_-` are odd, `C_-` is odd.
Moreover
```text
gcd(C_-,P_-*Q_-)=1.
```
Indeed if a prime dividing `C_-` divided one of `P_-,Q_-`, the congruence
`P_-^2 == Q_-^2 mod p` would force it to divide both, contradicting
`gcd(P_-,Q_-)=1`.
Consequently, for every prime power `p^k || C_-`,
```text
P_- == +Q_- mod p^k
```
or
```text
P_- == -Q_- mod p^k.
```
The whole primitive pair therefore lies on at most
```text
2^omega(C_-)
```
CRT projective root lines modulo `C_-`.
This is an exact ±1-root analogue of the Stage14 primitive root-line mechanism.
No Stage14 population theorem has been imported.
## 96.3 The physical modulus is coprime to the Gaussian core
On the one-factor locus,
```text
P_-^2+Q_-^2 = h*r0^2.
```
For any odd prime `p|C_-`, Theorem96-B gives
`P_-^2==Q_-^2 mod p`, while neither coordinate is zero modulo `p`.
Thus
```text
P_-^2+Q_-^2 == 2Q_-^2 != 0 mod p.
```
Hence
### Theorem 96-C — MinusModulusCoreCoprimality
```text
gcd(C_-,h*r0)=1.
```
In particular
```text
gcd(C_-,h)=1.
```
This is important because it removes all primes of the root modulus from the
discriminant of the Gaussian quartic below.
## 96.4 Push the root condition through the Gaussian square
Fix one Gaussian orientation
```text
kappa=c+i*d,
c^2+d^2=h.
```
Absorb the finite unit `epsilon` into the orientation and write
```text
P_-+iQ_- = kappa*(u+iv)^2.
```
Then
```text
P_-^2-Q_-^2
 = Re(kappa^2*(u+iv)^4).
```
Define the homogeneous binary quartic
```text
F_kappa(u,v)
 := Re(kappa^2*(u+iv)^4).
```
Explicitly,
```text
F_kappa(u,v)
 = (c^2-d^2)(u^4-6u^2v^2+v^4)
   - 8cd*uv*(u^2-v^2).
```
Theorem96-B becomes
```text
C_- | F_kappa(u,v).
```
For `t=u/v`, the quartic factors as
```text
F_kappa(t,1)
=
((c-d)t^2-2(c+d)t-(c-d))
*
((c+d)t^2+2(c-d)t-(c+d)).
```
A symbolic exact calculation gives
```text
Disc_t(F_kappa(t,1))
 = 2^14 * (c^2+d^2)^6
 = 2^14 * h^6.
```
This discriminant identity was independently checked with Wolfram Language.
By Theorem96-C, every prime dividing `C_-` is odd and coprime to `h`.
Therefore the quartic has no multiple projective root modulo any prime
dividing `C_-`.
Hensel lifting is consequently unique root-by-root at all powers
`p^k || C_-`.
### Theorem 96-D — GaussianQuarticRootLinePacket
For fixed `kappa`, the primitive Gaussian square parameter `(u:v)`
attached to a one-factor point lies on at most
```text
4^omega(C_-)
```
projective root classes modulo `C_-`.
Equivalently,
```text
# admissible projective roots mod C_- <= 4^omega(C_-)=C_-^{o(1)}.
```
No bad-prime correction is needed beyond the already separated prime 2,
because the full quartic discriminant is supported only on `2h` and
`gcd(C_-,2h)=1`.
## 96.5 Primitive lattice spacing after fixing an orientation
The representation
`P_-+iQ_-=kappa(u+iv)^2` with `gcd(P_-,Q_-)=1`
forces
```text
gcd(u,v)=1.
```
Indeed a common divisor of `u,v` would contribute its square to both
`P_-` and `Q_-`.
Thus every projective root class in Theorem96-D is a primitive lattice
root line modulo `C_-`.
The same determinant-spacing argument as the abstract Stage14 root-line
lemma now applies after this exact adapter:
two distinct primitive pairs `(u,v),(u',v')` on one root line satisfy
```text
C_- | u*v' - u'*v.
```
If the determinant is nonzero, its absolute value is at least `C_-`;
if it is zero, primitivity leaves only the same projective primitive pair
(up to the fixed sign convention).
Hence in a dyadic box
```text
|u| ~ U0,
|v| ~ V0,
```
one root class contains
```text
O(1 + U0*V0/C_-)
```
primitive pairs.
After summing the at most `4^omega(C_-)` classes,
```text
#(u,v)
 << C_-^{o(1)} * (1 + U0*V0/C_-).
```
This is a genuine modulus-spacing gain, conditional only on the outer
data fixing `C_-` and the Gaussian orientation.
## 96.6 Physical height cancellation in the root-line cost
Since
```text
Norm(P_-+iQ_-)
 = h*(u^2+v^2)^2,
```
we have
```text
r0=u^2+v^2.
```
Also Section89 gives
```text
L_- = g_-^2*r0^2.
```
If `x^2=L_-`, then
```text
x = g_-(u^2+v^2).
```
Under physical `R<=B`, both adjacent face diagonals are at most `B`,
hence
```text
L_- < P*Q <= B^2
```
and therefore
```text
x<=B.
```
Thus
```text
u^2+v^2 <= B/g_-.
```
Since
```text
C_-*g_-=X,
```
the area/modulus term in a square box of natural radius satisfies formally
```text
(U0*V0)/C_-
 <= O((B/g_-)/C_-)
 = O(B/X).
```
Therefore the conditional root-line count has the particularly clean scale
```text
#(u,v)
 << B^o(1) * (1+B/X)
```
after `C_-` and one Gaussian orientation are fixed.
This is not yet a whole-M3 count: the outer choices of `X,g_-,h,kappa`
and the Master-Hit / third-face condition still have to be charged exactly.
## 96.7 Reconstruction after fixing the Gaussian square data
The linear identities in 96.1 show that, once
```text
g_-,
P_-,
Q_-,
D1
```
are fixed,
```text
m=(aP_-+bQ_-)/D1,
n=(bP_-+aQ_-)/D1.
```
Symmetrically, fixing `D2,m,n,P_-,Q_-` reconstructs `a,b`.
Also
```text
C_-=lcm(D1,D2).
```
For fixed `C_-`, the possible divisor allocations `D1,D2` are
divisor-many, hence `B^o(1)); representing an odd leg
`U=a^2-b^2=(a-b)(a+b)` by a primitive opposite-parity Euclid pair is
again divisor-many.
Therefore:
### Theorem 96-E — FixedGaussianPacketReconstruction
After fixing
```text
g_-,
C_-,
kappa orientation,
(u,v),
```
the compatible Master source tuples have at most `B^o(1)` multiplicity
before imposing the already-required Master third-face-square check.
This is a reconstruction theorem, not an absolute counting theorem.
## 96.8 Consequence for the 2x2 architecture
The Section95 split should be refined.
The most useful modulus on the low-cycle Gaussian side is not only the
common norm core `h`.  There is an independent physical modulus
```text
C_-=X/g_-,
```
with
```text
gcd(C_-,h)=1.
```
The support-poor / small-core cell therefore has a new exact route:
```text
L_-=square
  -> primitive Gaussian square
  -> physical modulus C_-=X/g_-
  -> quartic F_kappa(u,v)==0 mod C_-
  -> <=4^omega(C_-) Hensel-stable projective root lines
  -> primitive determinant spacing
  -> divisor-many source reconstruction.
```
This is substantially sharper than the previous placeholder
`OneFactorLowCycleGaussianReconstruction`.
Define the next receiver
### Receiver 96-R1 — OneFactorPhysicalMinusRootLineCount
Prove that after the exact outer variables are charged on physical `M3(B)`,
the root-line estimate
```text
B^o(1)*(1+B/X)
```
together with divisor-many reconstruction gives `o(M3(B))` on the
support-poor / small-core cell.
The remaining obstacle is now explicit:
```text
OUTER_X_GMINUS_H_DISTRIBUTION_ON_M3=false.
```
The inner Gaussian lift itself is no longer uncontrolled.
## 96.9 Exact state after Section96
```text
GMINUS_DIVIDES_U1=true
GMINUS_DIVIDES_U2=true
GMINUS_DIVIDES_MASTER_G=true
PHYSICAL_MINUS_ROOT_MODULUS_DEFINED=true
C_MINUS_EQUALS_X_OVER_GMINUS=true
C_MINUS_DIVIDES_PMINUS2_MINUS_QMINUS2=true
GCD_C_MINUS_PMINUS_QMINUS=true
GCD_C_MINUS_H=true
GAUSSIAN_QUARTIC_DISCRIMINANT_EQUALS_2POW14_H6=true
GAUSSIAN_QUARTIC_ROOTS_HENSEL_STABLE_ON_C_MINUS=true
GAUSSIAN_QUARTIC_PROJECTIVE_ROOT_COUNT_LE_4POWOMEGA=true
PRIMITIVE_GAUSSIAN_ROOT_LINE_SPACING=true
FIXED_GAUSSIAN_PACKET_RECONSTRUCTION_BO1=true
ONE_FACTOR_PHYSICAL_MINUS_ROOT_LINE_COUNT=false
OUTER_X_GMINUS_H_DISTRIBUTION_ON_M3=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=OuterMinusModulusDistributionOrDualConstraint
```
Two candidate attacks are now concrete:
1. show that the large-`C_-` region already gives a same-M3 saving after
   summing the root-line bound over the legal outer packets; or
2. combine `C_-|F_kappa(u,v)` with the Master third-face square condition
   to obtain a second transverse congruence/root-line, analogous to the
   dual-primitive-root-line saturation mechanism from late Stage14.
No density-zero claim is made in Section96.

# SECTION97 — canonical physical recovery of g_- and odd-edge anti-concentration
## 2026-09-26
## Status: exact pointwise identities plus a rigorous M3-negligibility corollary
Section96 introduced
```text
C_-=X/g_-.
```
This section shows that both `g_-` and `C_-` can be recovered from the
physical one-factor data, and that fixing the physical odd edge already has
only divisor-many multiplicity.
## 97.1 The physical odd edge is the lcm of the two odd Euclid legs
Recall
```text
X=U1*U2/g,
g=gcd(U1,U2).
```
Therefore, pointwise,
### Theorem 97-A — OddEdgeIsSourceLegLCM
```text
X=lcm(U1,U2).
```
In particular
```text
U1|X,
U2|X.
```
This gives an elementary multiplicity bound for a fixed physical odd edge.
For a fixed odd integer `X`, the ordered pair `(U1,U2)` has at most
```text
tau(X)^2
```
possibilities.
For each odd `U`, a primitive opposite-parity Euclid representation
```text
U=a^2-b^2=(a-b)(a+b)
```
is determined by a divisor factorization of `U`, hence has at most
`tau(U)=X^{o(1)}` possibilities.
Thus:
### Theorem 97-B — FixedOddEdgeMasterMultiplicity
For every fixed odd edge `X<=B`,
```text
#{primitive Master source tuples producing odd edge X}
 <= X^{o(1)}
 <= B^{o(1)}.
```
The physical canonical projection and the Master swap contribute only a
fixed multiplicity.
Consequently the same bound holds a fortiori for the subset satisfying the
third-face / M3 condition.
## 97.2 Odd-edge anti-concentration on exact physical M3
Let
```text
M3_X(B)
 := #{E in M3(B): unique odd edge of E equals X}.
```
Then uniformly for `X<=B`,
```text
M3_X(B)<=B^{o(1)}.
```
Since the certified global lower bound is
```text
M3(B) >> B^(1/3),
```
for every fixed `epsilon>0`,
```text
sum_{X <= B^(1/3-epsilon)} M3_X(B)
 <= B^(1/3-epsilon+o(1))
 = o(M3(B)).
```
Hence:
### Theorem 97-C — SmallOddEdgeRegionNegligible
For every fixed `epsilon>0`, the exact physical M3 population with
```text
X <= B^(1/3-epsilon)
```
has relative density zero in `M3(B)`.
Equivalently, after deleting an `o(M3(B))` exceptional population, one may
work in the region
```text
X >= B^(1/3-epsilon).
```
This is a same-measure statement; no parameter-space equidistribution is
used.
## 97.3 g_- is the physical gcd of X and sqrt(L_-)
On the one-factor locus write
```text
x^2=L_-.
```
Section96 gives
```text
x=g_-*r0,
X=g_-*C_-,
gcd(C_-,r0)=1.
```
Therefore
### Theorem 97-D — PhysicalRecoveryOfMinusCrossGcd
```text
g_- = gcd(X,x)
    = gcd(X,sqrt(L_-)).
```
Consequently
```text
C_-
 = X/gcd(X,sqrt(L_-)),
```
and
```text
r0
 = sqrt(L_-)/gcd(X,sqrt(L_-)).
```
Thus the Section96 modulus is canonical on the physical one-factor locus.
It does not depend on choosing a Master representation after the physical
brick and the square root of `L_-` are fixed.
Since
```text
r0=u^2+v^2,
```
we also obtain the physical reconstruction
```text
sqrt(L_-)/gcd(X,sqrt(L_-))
 = u^2+v^2.
```
## 97.4 Consequence for Section96 root-line spacing
Section97-C allows the Section96 conditional estimate
```text
#(u,v) << B^o(1)*(1+B/X)
```
to be restricted, after an M3-negligible deletion, to
```text
1+B/X
 <= 1+B^(2/3+epsilon).
```
This does not by itself close the density problem; the bound is still too
large as a standalone absolute count.
Its value is structural:
1. the troublesome tiny-`X` packets are now rigorously negligible on the
   exact physical M3 measure;
2. the modulus `C_-` is a canonical physical cofactor of `X`;
3. all remaining failure of the root-line route lies in the distribution
   of the canonical pair
   `(X, sqrt(L_-))`, not in source-coordinate multiplicity.
The revised outer receiver can therefore be stated physically.
### Receiver 97-R1 — PhysicalOddEdgeMinusSquareGcdDistribution
Control on exact `M3(B)` the distribution of
```text
d_-(E):=gcd(X(E),sqrt(L_-(E)))
```
on the one-factor locus, equivalently the modulus
```text
C_-(E)=X(E)/d_-(E).
```
A theorem showing that the region where `C_-` is too small is
M3-negligible would combine immediately with Section96 root-line spacing.
## 97.5 Important negative check on the proposed dual constraint
A first attempt to use the Master third-face square modulo primes of
`C_-` does not automatically produce an independent quadratic-character
filter.
Indeed a prime dividing `D1=U1/g_-` already divides `U1`, so
```text
M=(V1*U2)^2+(U1*V2)^2
  == (V1*U2)^2 mod p,
```
and similarly on the `D2` side.
Thus the Master-square condition is locally a square for a tautological
reason at first order on these same primes.
Therefore the proposed Section96 candidate
```text
C_- root line + Master square
```
does not yet supply two independent mod-`p` character bits.
Any genuine dual constraint must use higher prime-power information,
a different modulus, or a transverse global/fibration coordinate.
This rules out a tempting but false shortcut.
## 97.6 Exact state after Section97
```text
ODD_EDGE_EQUALS_LCM_U1_U2=true
FIXED_ODD_EDGE_MASTER_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
FIXED_ODD_EDGE_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
SMALL_ODD_EDGE_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
GMINUS_EQUALS_GCD_X_SQRT_LMINUS=true
C_MINUS_CANONICAL_PHYSICAL=true
FIRST_ORDER_MASTER_SQUARE_TRANSVERSE_AT_C_MINUS=false
PHYSICAL_ODD_EDGE_MINUS_SQUARE_GCD_DISTRIBUTION=false
ONE_FACTOR_PHYSICAL_MINUS_ROOT_LINE_COUNT=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=LargeGminusSmallCminusOrHigherPowerTransverseConstraint
```
The cleanest next split is now physical:
```text
C_- large  -> Section96 root-line spacing,
C_- small  -> g_-=gcd(X,sqrt(L_-)) large.
```
The remaining question is whether a large common divisor between the odd
edge `X` and the canonical square root `sqrt(L_-)` can itself be shown
to occupy an M3-negligible subset, or whether its prime-power structure
forces a stronger congruence on the Gaussian / third-face data.
