# SECTION98 — four-cross-gcd coordinates and the small-C_- genus-one fibers
## 2026-09-26
## Status: exact algebra through the smooth genus-one reduction
Let
```text
x=a-b, y=a+b, s=m-n, t=m+n.
```
Then `x,y,s,t` are positive odd integers,
`gcd(x,y)=gcd(s,t)=1), and
```text
U1=x*y,
U2=s*t.
```
Define four cross gcds
```text
alpha=gcd(y,t),
beta =gcd(x,s),
gamma=gcd(y,s),
delta=gcd(x,t).
```
They are pairwise coprime.
For
```text
A_-=am-bn,
B_-=an-bm,
```
we have
```text
A_- - B_-=y*s,
A_- + B_-=x*t.
```
Since `g_-=gcd(A_-,B_-)` is odd,
### Theorem 98-A — MinusCrossGcdLinearFactorization
```text
g_-=alpha*beta.
```
For the plus pair
```text
A_+=am+bn,
B_+=an+bm,
```
we have
```text
A_+ - B_+=x*s,
A_+ + B_+=y*t,
```
hence
### Theorem 98-B — PlusCrossGcdLinearFactorization
```text
g_+=gamma*delta.
```
Also
```text
g=gcd(U1,U2)
 =alpha*beta*gamma*delta
 =g_-*g_+.
```
Thus `g_-` is exactly the matched-sign cross core and `g_+` the
opposite-sign cross core.
Peel the four cross gcds:
```text
x=beta*delta*x0,
y=alpha*gamma*y0,
s=beta*gamma*s0,
t=alpha*delta*t0.
```
Then
```text
R:=U1/g=x0*y0,
S:=U2/g=s0*t0,
gcd(R,S)=1.
```
Since `X=g*R*S`,
### Theorem 98-C — ExactMinusModulusPacketProduct
```text
C_-=X/g_-
   =gamma*delta*R*S
   =gamma*delta*x0*y0*s0*t0.
```
Therefore, for fixed `C_-=c`, the ordered residual packet
```text
(gamma,delta,x0,y0,s0,t0)
```
has at most
```text
tau_6(c)=c^{o(1)}
```
possibilities.  All exact coprimality requirements only reduce this count.
## 98.1 M3 inside one fixed residual packet
The primitive even legs are
```text
V1=(y^2-x^2)/2,
V2=(t^2-s^2)/2.
```
Because
```text
Y=V1*S,
Z=R*V2,
```
we obtain
```text
2Y
 = S*(alpha^2*gamma^2*y0^2
      -beta^2*delta^2*x0^2),
2Z
 = R*(alpha^2*delta^2*t0^2
      -beta^2*gamma^2*s0^2).
```
For fixed residual data put
```text
A=S*gamma^2*y0^2,
B=S*delta^2*x0^2,
C=R*delta^2*t0^2,
D=R*gamma^2*s0^2.
```
Then
```text
2Y=A*alpha^2-B*beta^2,
2Z=C*alpha^2-D*beta^2.
```
The M3 condition `Y^2+Z^2=T^2` becomes, for
```text
lambda=alpha/beta,
w=2T/beta^2,
```
the even quartic
```text
w^2
 =(A*lambda^2-B)^2
 +(C*lambda^2-D)^2
 =A0*lambda^4+B0*lambda^2+C0,
```
where
```text
A0=A^2+C^2,
B0=-2*(A*B+C*D),
C0=B^2+D^2.
```
Thus fixing `C_-) and one residual factor packet leaves a single
genus-one candidate fiber in the moving ratio `lambda=alpha/beta`.
## 98.2 Smoothness
For `f(z)=A0*z^4+B0*z^2+C0`,
```text
Disc(f)
 =16*A0*C0*(B0^2-4*A0*C0)^2,
```
and
```text
B0^2-4*A0*C0
 =-4*(A*D-B*C)^2.
```
Now `A*D=B*C` is equivalent to
```text
gamma^2*y0*s0=delta^2*x0*t0,
```
hence to
```text
y*s=x*t.
```
But
```text
2B_-=x*t-y*s.
```
Therefore degeneracy forces `B_-=an-bm=0`.
Primitive positivity then gives `a/b=m/n`, hence
`(a,b)=(m,n)`, so the two physical even edges coincide.
That is excluded by the strict canonical edge ordering.
### Theorem 98-D — FixedResidualM3QuarticSmooth
Every primitive canonical physical M3 point lies on a smooth genus-one
even quartic after its residual packet is fixed.
The quartic discriminant identity was checked independently by symbolic
calculation.
## 98.3 Rational 2-torsion structure
The four branch points of
```text
w^2=A0*lambda^4+B0*lambda^2+C0
```
occur in opposite pairs
```text
{r,-r}, {s,-s}.
```
The unordered partition into these two opposite pairs is Galois-stable.
Hence the Jacobian has a nonzero rational 2-torsion point.
So every occupied residual packet is a genus-one torsor whose Jacobian
belongs to the same rational-2-torsion species used by the Section39
bounded-height argument.
Exact status:
```text
FOUR_CROSS_GCD_COORDINATES=true
GMINUS_EQUALS_ALPHA_BETA=true
GPLUS_EQUALS_GAMMA_DELTA=true
MASTER_G_EQUALS_GMINUS_GPLUS=true
C_MINUS_RESIDUAL_PACKET_PRODUCT=true
FIXED_C_MINUS_PACKET_COUNT_DIVISOR_MANY=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_M3_EVEN_QUARTIC=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_M3_QUARTIC_SMOOTH=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_JACOBIAN_RATIONAL_2TORSION=true
```
## 98.4 Uniform bounded-height count on one residual packet
For physical height `R_phys<=B`, the adjacent face diagonals satisfy
```text
W1*S<=B,
W2*R<=B.
```
Hence
```text
a,b,m,n <= B^(1/2),
alpha,beta <= 2*B^(1/2).
```
Because `gcd(alpha,beta)=1`, the rational coordinate
```text
lambda=alpha/beta
```
has height at most `2*B^(1/2)`.
For a residual packet with `C_-<=B`, all six packet variables divide the
product `C_-`, so the quartic coefficients `A0,B0,C0` have polynomial
height `B^{O(1)}`.  The physical third-face diagonal is at most `B`, so
the corresponding `w=2T/beta^2` also has polynomial height.
A binary quartic genus-one model has a Jacobian Weierstrass model whose
invariants are polynomial expressions in the quartic coefficients.  For an
occupied fiber, choosing one rational point identifies the torsor with its
Jacobian; standard fixed-degree height comparison gives
```text
elliptic model height = O(log B),
mapped x-height        = B^{O(1)}.
```
This is the only external height-adapter input in this section.  It is the
same species of polynomial-height conversion already used in Section39,
not an equidistribution assumption.
By Section98.3 the Jacobian has rational 2-torsion.  Therefore the Dujella
uniform bounded-height theorem imported in Section39 applies uniformly.
### Theorem 98-E — FixedResidualPacketM3Multiplicity
For every occupied fixed residual packet,
```text
#{physical M3 points in the packet with R_phys<=B}
 <= exp(O(log B/log log B))
 = B^{o(1)}.
```
The implied constant is uniform over the packets.
The map from a physical point to `lambda=alpha/beta` is injective inside
a fixed residual packet, because `alpha,beta` are positive coprime
integers and the four peeled-factor formulas then recover
`x,y,s,t`.
## 98.5 Small-C_- region is negligible on exact M3
For fixed `c=C_-`, Section98-C gives at most
```text
tau_6(c)=c^{o(1)}
```
residual packets, and Section98-E gives `B^{o(1)}` physical M3 points per
packet.
Therefore, uniformly for `1<=Y<=B`,
```text
#{E in M3(B): C_-(E)<=Y}
 <= sum_{c<=Y} tau_6(c)*B^{o(1)}
 <= Y^{1+o(1)}*B^{o(1)}.
```
Using the certified lower bound
```text
M3(B) >> B^(1/3),
```
we obtain:
### Theorem 98-F — SmallPhysicalMinusModulusNegligible
For every fixed `epsilon>0`,
```text
#{E in M3(B):
    L_-(E) is square,
    C_-(E)<=B^(1/3-epsilon)}
 = o(M3(B)).
```
Thus the small-`C_-` / large-`g_-` branch from Section97 is closed up to
the natural `B^(1/3)` threshold.
No ambient parameter density or independence heuristic is used.
## 98.6 Revised surviving region
Combining Sections97 and 98, after deleting an `o(M3(B))` subset, every
one-factor survivor satisfies, for any fixed `epsilon>0`,
```text
X    >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
C_-  >= B^(1/3-epsilon).
```
Since
```text
X=g_-*C_-,
```
the remaining problem is entirely the large-`C_-` side where the
Section96 Gaussian quartic root-line spacing is strongest.
The previous receiver
```text
LargeGminusSmallCminusOrHigherPowerTransverseConstraint
```
is therefore superseded.
### Receiver 98-R1 — LargeCminusRootLineGlobalSummation
Sum the Section96 estimate
```text
#(u,v)
 << C_-^{o(1)}*(1+U0*V0/C_-)
 << B^{o(1)}*(1+B/X)
```
over the exact residual packets in the region
```text
C_- >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
X   >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
```
without paying for `C_-` as a completely free integer.
The key remaining question is whether the packet factorization
```text
C_-=gamma*delta*R*S
```
and the quartic root-line condition can be summed together so that the
modulus saving survives the moving-`C_-` summation.
## 98.7 Exact state after Section98
```text
FOUR_CROSS_GCD_COORDINATES=true
GMINUS_EQUALS_ALPHA_BETA=true
GPLUS_EQUALS_GAMMA_DELTA=true
MASTER_G_EQUALS_GMINUS_GPLUS=true
C_MINUS_RESIDUAL_PACKET_PRODUCT=true
FIXED_C_MINUS_PACKET_COUNT_DIVISOR_MANY=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_M3_EVEN_QUARTIC=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_M3_QUARTIC_SMOOTH=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_JACOBIAN_RATIONAL_2TORSION=true
FIXED_RESIDUAL_PACKET_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
SMALL_C_MINUS_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
ONE_FACTOR_PHYSICAL_MINUS_ROOT_LINE_COUNT=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false

```

# SECTION99 — correction of the C_- root-line and the genuine square-kernel modulus
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact correction + exact new local receiver; no global density closure yet
Section98 remains valid, including the small-C_- negligibility theorem.
However, the proposed Section96/98 large-C_- root-line route needs a correction:
the congruence modulo C_- is not an independent sieve condition.
## 99.1 Exact residual formulas for the primitive minus/plus Gaussian pairs
Use the Section98 coordinates
```text
x=beta*delta*x0,
y=alpha*gamma*y0,
s=beta*gamma*s0,
t=alpha*delta*t0,
```
with
```text
g_-=alpha*beta,
g_+=gamma*delta,
C_-=gamma*delta*x0*y0*s0*t0.
```
From
```text
A_-=am-bn=(y*s+x*t)/2,
B_-=an-bm=(x*t-y*s)/2,
```
we get, after dividing by g_-,
```text
P_-=(gamma^2*y0*s0+delta^2*x0*t0)/2,
Q_-=(delta^2*x0*t0-gamma^2*y0*s0)/2.
```
Therefore P_- and Q_- depend only on the residual packet
```text
(gamma,delta,x0,y0,s0,t0)
```
and are completely independent of alpha,beta.
Similarly,
```text
P_+=(beta^2*x0*s0+alpha^2*y0*t0)/2,
Q_+=(alpha^2*y0*t0-beta^2*x0*s0)/2.
```
Hence
### Theorem 99-A — ExactResidualNormFormulas
```text
N_-
 =P_-^2+Q_-^2
 =(gamma^4*y0^2*s0^2+delta^4*x0^2*t0^2)/2,
```
while
```text
N_+
 =P_+^2+Q_+^2
 =(beta^4*x0^2*s0^2+alpha^4*y0^2*t0^2)/2.
```
Thus N_- is a residual-packet invariant and all alpha,beta motion sits in N_+.
The identities above were independently checked symbolically.
## 99.2 Correction: the C_- divisibility is tautological
From the explicit P_-,Q_- formulas,
```text
P_-^2-Q_-^2
 = gamma^2*delta^2*x0*y0*s0*t0.
```
Using
```text
g_+=gamma*delta,
C_-=gamma*delta*x0*y0*s0*t0,
```
we obtain
### Theorem 99-B — MinusDifferenceProductIdentity
```text
P_-^2-Q_-^2 = g_+*C_-.
```
Therefore the Section96 statement
```text
C_- | P_-^2-Q_-^2
```
is correct but automatic after the four-cross-gcd decomposition.
Consequently the derived quartic condition modulo C_- is not an independent
local bit and must not be credited as a large-C_- sieve saving.
The following Section96 flags are therefore structural identities only, not
independent counting weapons:
```text
C_MINUS_DIVIDES_PMINUS2_MINUS_QMINUS2=true
GAUSSIAN_QUARTIC_ROOTS_HENSEL_STABLE_ON_C_MINUS=true
```
but
```text
C_MINUS_ROOT_LINE_INDEPENDENT_SIEVE=false.
```
Accordingly,
```text
LargeCminusRootLineGlobalSummation
```
is superseded as the primary receiver.
The Section98 theorem
```text
SMALL_C_MINUS_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
```
does not use this false independence and remains valid.
## 99.3 The genuine modulus: the squarefree kernel of N_-
Let
```text
K_- := sqf(N_-)
```
denote the squarefree kernel, i.e. the product of primes occurring to odd
valuation in N_-.
Since (P_-,Q_-)=1 and N_-=P_-^2+Q_-^2 is odd, every prime dividing N_-
is congruent to 1 mod 4.  Hence K_- is an odd squarefree product of
1 mod 4 primes.
Write
```text
N_-=K_-*r^2.
```
Recall
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+)
```
and
```text
L_-=g_-^2*(N_-/h).
```
Because g_-^2 is already a square,
```text
L_- is square
<=> N_-/h is square.
```
Prime-by-prime valuation parity gives the exact reformulation:
### Theorem 99-C — OneFactorSquareKernelAbsorption
On the one-factor locus,
```text
h=K_-*s^2
```
for some integer s dividing r.
Equivalently,
```text
sqf(h)=sqf(N_-)=K_-.
```
In particular,
```text
K_- | h | N_+.
```
This is the genuine moving common-core condition hidden inside
L_-=square.
## 99.4 K_- is coprime to the residual physical modulus C_-
Section96 already implies gcd(C_-,N_-)=1; it is also immediate from
Theorem99-A/99-B because every prime of C_- divides exactly one of the two
coprime odd quantities
```text
gamma^2*y0*s0,
delta^2*x0*t0.
```
Thus
### Theorem 99-D — SquareKernelMinusModulusCoprimality
```text
gcd(K_-,C_-)=1.
```
In particular all coefficients
```text
x0*s0,
y0*t0
```
are units modulo K_-.
## 99.5 Genuine quartic root lines in alpha/beta
Set
```text
E=x0*s0,
F=y0*t0.
```
The necessary condition K_-|N_+ becomes
```text
beta^4*E^2 + alpha^4*F^2 == 0 mod K_-.
```
On a one-factor point, gcd(alpha,beta,K_-)=1:
if a prime p|K_- divided alpha, the congruence above would force
p|beta*E, impossible because gcd(alpha,beta)=1 and p is coprime to E.
The same argument applies to beta.
Hence for every p|K_-,
```text
(lambda)^4 == -E^2/F^2 mod p,
lambda=alpha/beta.
```
The polynomial
```text
F^2*T^4+E^2
```
has derivative 4*F^2*T^3, which is nonzero at every admissible root modulo
p.  Thus all roots are simple.
There are at most four roots modulo each p and therefore at most
```text
4^omega(K_-)=K_-^{o(1)}
```
projective root classes modulo K_-.
### Theorem 99-E — GenuineSquareKernelRootLines
For a fixed residual packet, every one-factor point has (alpha:beta) on at
most
```text
4^omega(K_-)
```
primitive projective root lines modulo K_-.
Unlike the C_- condition, this is not automatic from the packet
factorization: it is exactly the first parity content of the one-factor
condition.
For a dyadic box
```text
alpha~A0,
beta~B0,
```
the primitive determinant-spacing lemma gives
```text
#(alpha,beta)
 << K_-^{o(1)}*(1+A0*B0/K_-).
```
Since
```text
alpha*beta=g_-=X/C_- <= B/C_-,
```
we get the conditional bound
```text
#(alpha,beta)
 << B^{o(1)}*(1+B/(C_-*K_-)).
```
This is the correct root-line scale replacing Section96-R1.
## 99.6 Cross-routing interpretation of K_-
Recall
```text
h
 = gcd(W1,V2)*gcd(W2,V1).
```
The two factors are coprime: a common odd prime would divide both W1 and V1
(or both W2 and V2), contradicting primitivity of the corresponding
Pythagorean triple.
Since K_- is squarefree and K_-|h, there is a unique coprime splitting
```text
K_-=K_1*K_2,
```
with
```text
K_1 | gcd(W1,V2),
K_2 | gcd(W2,V1).
```
For each prime q|K_1, q divides exactly one of m,n and also
```text
a^2+b^2 == 0 mod q.
```
Thus q==1 mod4 and the primitive source pair (a:b) lies on one of the two
Gaussian root lines
```text
a == +/- i_q*b mod q.
```
Similarly, each q|K_2 divides exactly one of a,b and puts (m:n) on one of
the two root lines
```text
m == +/- i_q*n mod q.
```
Therefore K_- simultaneously creates:
1. coordinate divisibility on one Euclid source pair; and
2. a Gaussian root-line congruence on the transverse source pair.
This is the exact large-sieve geometry that should replace the tautological
C_- root line.
## 99.7 Revised architecture
The surviving one-factor problem is now:
```text
residual packet
  -> fixed N_-
  -> K_-=sqf(N_-)
  -> one-factor forces sqf(h)=K_-
  -> K_-|N_+(alpha,beta)
  -> genuine quartic root lines mod K_-
  -> equivalent cross-routing through
       gcd(W1,V2) and gcd(W2,V1).
```
Section98 already disposes of
```text
C_- <= B^(1/3-epsilon).
```
For the remaining region
```text
C_- >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
```
the meaningful second axis is now K_-, not C_- itself.
Define the new split:
```text
K_- large:
    exploit K_- cross-routing / quartic root-line cancellation;
K_- small:
    exploit the exact Gaussian reconstruction
    N_-=K_-*r^2
    and count residual packets from the small squarefree core.
```
This is a corrected version of the old
residue-rich / Gaussian-core-small architecture, now in coordinates that
are exact on the Section98 residual packets.
## 99.8 Exact state after Section99
```text
PMINUS_QMINUS_RESIDUAL_ONLY=true
NMINUS_RESIDUAL_PACKET_INVARIANT=true
NPLUS_ALPHA_BETA_MOVING=true
PMINUS2_MINUS_QMINUS2_EQUALS_GPLUS_CMINUS=true
C_MINUS_ROOT_LINE_INDEPENDENT_SIEVE=false
K_MINUS_SQUAREFREE_KERNEL_DEFINED=true
ONE_FACTOR_IFF_H_EQUALS_KMINUS_TIMES_SQUARE=true
K_MINUS_DIVIDES_H=true
K_MINUS_DIVIDES_NPLUS=true
GCD_K_MINUS_C_MINUS=true
GENUINE_K_MINUS_QUARTIC_ROOT_LINES=true
K_MINUS_CROSS_ROUTING=true
SMALL_C_MINUS_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
K_MINUS_LARGE_SAME_M3_SIEVE=false
K_MINUS_SMALL_GAUSSIAN_PACKET_COUNT=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=KminusLargeSmallDichotomy
```
The next task is to quantify both sides without assuming ordinary
squarefree-kernel statistics on M3:
1. large K_-: use the exact cross-routing congruence lattice on both source
   pairs and retain the physical M3 condition;
2. small K_-: use
   N_-=K_-*r^2
   to Gaussian-reconstruct the residual packet and determine the remaining
   source freedom under R<=B.

# SECTION100 — primitive norm rectangle and fixed-N_- anti-concentration
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact coordinate compression + new exact physical anti-concentration theorem
Section99 identified the genuine one-factor modulus as the squarefree kernel
of N_-.  This section compresses the four-cross-gcd coordinates back into
the Section90 face-divisor coordinates and obtains a cleaner two-norm model.
Let
```text
A0=P-Y,
C0=Q-Z.
```
Write
```text
d0=gcd(A0,C0),
A0=d0*a,
C0=d0*c,
gcd(a,c)=1.
```
Section90 defines
```text
k = X^2/(d0*a*c).
```
In the Section98 cross-gcd coordinates one has exactly
```text
d0 = beta^2*x0*s0,
a  = delta^2*x0*t0,
c  = gamma^2*y0*s0,
k  = alpha^2*y0*t0.
```
In particular
```text
gcd(a,c)=1,
gcd(d0,k)=1.
```
## 100.1 Exact physical rectangle
Because
```text
(P-Y)(P+Y)=X^2,
(Q-Z)(Q+Z)=X^2,
```
we obtain
```text
P-Y=d0*a,
Q-Z=d0*c,
Q+Z=a*k,
P+Y=c*k.
```
Hence
### Theorem 100-A — PrimitiveNormRectangle
```text
P=(d0*a+c*k)/2,
Y=(c*k-d0*a)/2,
Q=(d0*c+a*k)/2,
Z=(a*k-d0*c)/2.
```
Moreover
```text
X^2=a*c*d0*k.
```
Thus the physical odd edge is exactly the geometric mean of the four
positive odd rectangle factors.
The cross-gcd grid is
```text
gcd(a,d0)=x0,
gcd(a,k)=t0,
gcd(c,d0)=s0,
gcd(c,k)=y0.
```
Also
```text
gcd(a*c,d0*k)=R*S=x0*y0*s0*t0,
```
and
```text
(a*c)/(R*S)=g_+^2,
(d0*k)/(R*S)=g_-^2.
```
So the square identity X^2=a*c*d0*k is the compressed form of the
four-cross-gcd squareclass allocation.
## 100.2 The two primitive Gaussian norms
Define
```text
N_-=(a^2+c^2)/2,
N_+=(d0^2+k^2)/2.
```
Then the Section99 formulas become exactly
### Theorem 100-B — TwoPrimitiveNormDictionary
```text
N_- = P_-^2+Q_-^2,
N_+ = P_+^2+Q_+^2.
```
The terminal product identities simplify to
```text
PQ-YZ = d0*k*N_-,
PQ+YZ = a*c*N_+.
```
The physical third-face diagonal D satisfies
```text
D^2=Y^2+Z^2
   =N_-*N_+ - X^2.
```
Therefore the physical space-length square is
### Theorem 100-C — ExactPhysicalHeightProduct
```text
R_phys^2
 =X^2+Y^2+Z^2
 =N_-*N_+.
```
Hence the exact physical cutoff is simply
```text
N_-*N_+ <= B^2.
```
This is an exact same-measure height statement, not a parameter-box
replacement.
## 100.3 One-factor condition in the norm rectangle
Let
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+).
```
Since
```text
d0*k=(R*S)*g_-^2,
a*c =(R*S)*g_+^2,
```
the physical gcd is
```text
G=gcd(PQ,YZ)=R*S*h.
```
Consequently
```text
L_-=g_-^2*(N_-/h),
L_+=g_+^2*(N_+/h).
```
Thus
```text
L_- square
<=> N_-/h square,
```
and Section99-C becomes
```text
sqf(h)=sqf(N_-)=K_-.
```
The one-factor problem is therefore exactly:
> two primitive odd sum-of-two-squares norms N_-,N_+ under the product
> height N_-N_+<=B^2, with the squarefree kernel of N_- completely absorbed
> into gcd(N_-,N_+), together with the physical M3 equation
> D^2=N_-N_+-X^2 and the squareclass gluing X^2=a*c*d0*k.
This is the current minimal arithmetic receiver.
## 100.4 Fixed N_- has only divisor-many first pairs
Fix an odd integer n=N_-.
The equation
```text
a^2+c^2=2n,
gcd(a,c)=1,
a,c odd
```
has at most
```text
r_2(2n) <= n^{o(1)}
```
ordered solutions.
Thus fixing N_- leaves only divisor-many choices of the primitive first
pair (a,c).
## 100.5 Squareclass allocation for d0,k
For fixed coprime odd a,c, put
```text
sigma=sqf(a*c).
```
The condition
```text
a*c*d0*k=X^2,
gcd(d0,k)=1
```
forces
```text
sqf(d0*k)=sigma.
```
Choose a coprime orientation
```text
sigma=sigma_d*sigma_k.
```
There are at most
```text
2^omega(sigma)=B^{o(1)}
```
such orientations, and then
```text
d0=sigma_d*u^2,
k =sigma_k*v^2,
gcd(u,v)=1.
```
## 100.6 M3 becomes a smooth even quartic in u/v
Substitute the previous forms into
```text
4D^2
 =(c*k-d0*a)^2+(a*k-d0*c)^2.
```
For
```text
lambda=u/v
```
this becomes
```text
w^2
 = sigma_d^2*(a^2+c^2)*lambda^4
   -4*a*c*sigma_d*sigma_k*lambda^2
   +sigma_k^2*(a^2+c^2),
```
after harmless scaling of w.
Its quartic discriminant is nonzero whenever a!=c.  Indeed the relevant
quadratic-in-lambda^2 discriminant is
```text
-4*sigma_d^2*sigma_k^2*(a^2-c^2)^2.
```
The canonical physical setting has a!=c, so every such fiber is smooth.
The quartic is even in lambda, hence its Jacobian has a nonzero rational
2-torsion point.
The coefficients have polynomial height under R_phys<=B, and the rational
coordinate lambda=u/v has polynomial height because
```text
d0*a,
d0*c,
k*a,
k*c <= 2B.
```
Therefore the same Dujella/Bombieri-Zannier bounded-height theorem used in
Sections39 and 98 applies uniformly.
### Theorem 100-D — FixedFirstNormM3Multiplicity
For every fixed N_-=n,
```text
#{E in M3(B): N_-(E)=n}
 <= B^{o(1)}
```
uniformly in n<=B^2.
Proof structure:
```text
fixed n
 -> n^{o(1)} choices of (a,c)
 -> B^{o(1)} squareclass orientations of d0,k
 -> one smooth rational-2-torsion genus-one fiber
 -> B^{o(1)} bounded-height rational points.
```
The same argument is symmetric in the two norm pairs and gives
```text
#{E in M3(B): N_+(E)=n}
 <= B^{o(1)}.
```
## 100.7 New same-M3 anti-concentration consequences
Using
```text
M3(B) >> B^(1/3),
```
Theorem100-D implies that for every fixed epsilon>0,
```text
#{E in M3(B): N_-(E)<=B^(1/3-epsilon)}
 =o(M3(B)),
```
and symmetrically
```text
#{E in M3(B): N_+(E)<=B^(1/3-epsilon)}
 =o(M3(B)).
```
Thus, after deleting an M3-negligible population,
```text
N_- >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
N_+ >= B^(1/3-epsilon).
```
Combined with
```text
N_-*N_+<=B^2,
```
each surviving norm also satisfies
```text
N_-,N_+ <= B^(5/3+epsilon).
```
This is a third exact anti-concentration coordinate, after the rational
t-fiber and the physical odd edge.
## 100.8 What this does not yet close
The norm rectangle does not by itself prove one-factor thinness.
The remaining Class-3 statement has become more precise:
### Receiver 100-R1 — SameM3PrimitiveNormSquareKernelIncidence
On the exact physical M3 population in the product-height region
```text
N_-*N_+<=B^2,
N_-,N_+>=B^(1/3-epsilon),
```
prove that
```text
sqf(gcd(N_-,N_+))=sqf(N_-)
```
occurs with relative frequency o(1).
Equivalently, with
```text
K_-=sqf(N_-),
```
prove same-M3 decay for the requirement
```text
K_- | N_+
```
together with the exact parity condition
```text
gcd(N_-,N_+)/K_- is a square.
```
The external bounded-height results used above control one fixed genus-one
fiber, but they do not supply this moving-norm congruence cancellation
across the polynomially many occupied fibers.
That is the remaining local-to-global wall in its current smallest form.
## 100.9 Exact state after Section100
```text
PRIMITIVE_NORM_RECTANGLE=true
X2_EQUALS_A_C_D0_K=true
NMINUS_EQUALS_A2_PLUS_C2_OVER2=true
NPLUS_EQUALS_D02_PLUS_K2_OVER2=true
PHYSICAL_HEIGHT_SQUARED_EQUALS_NMINUS_NPLUS=true
PHYSICAL_HEIGHT_PRODUCT_CUTOFF_EXACT=true
ONE_FACTOR_SQUAREKERNEL_NORM_GCD_FORM=true
FIXED_NMINUS_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
FIXED_NPLUS_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
SMALL_NMINUS_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
SMALL_NPLUS_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
SAME_M3_PRIMITIVE_NORM_SQUAREKERNEL_INCIDENCE=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=PrimitiveNormSquareKernelIncidence
```
The next attack should work directly in the norm rectangle rather than
returning to the tautological C_- modulus.

# SECTION101 — dual prime-power Gaussian root packets at the common core h
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact local/lattice theorem + negative global summation result
Section100 reduced the one-factor condition to two primitive odd Gaussian norms
```text
N_-=(a^2+c^2)/2,
N_+=(d0^2+k^2)/2,
gcd(a,c)=gcd(d0,k)=1,
```
with
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+),
N_-=h*u^2
```
on the one-factor locus.
This section records the full prime-power congruence geometry carried by h.
## 101.1 Every prime power of h gives two root lines on each primitive pair
Let p^e||h.
Because p divides N_- and gcd(a,c)=1, neither a nor c is divisible by p.
Thus c is invertible modulo p^e and
```text
(a/c)^2 == -1 mod p^e.
```
Likewise, neither d0 nor k is divisible by p and
```text
(d0/k)^2 == -1 mod p^e.
```
Every prime divisor of h is therefore 1 mod 4.
For p==1 mod4, the two roots of T^2+1 modulo p are simple, so Hensel
lifting gives exactly two roots modulo every p^e.
By CRT:
### Theorem 101-A — DualGaussianRootPacket
For fixed h, the primitive projective pair (a:c) lies on exactly
```text
2^omega(h)
```
possible Gaussian root lines modulo h, and independently (d0:k) lies on
at most
```text
2^omega(h)
```
root lines modulo h.
Hence the pair of projective ratios lies in at most
```text
4^omega(h)=h^{o(1)}
```
dual root packets modulo h.
This uses the full prime powers in h, not merely rad(h).
## 101.2 Lattice determinant
Fix one root rho modulo h for the first pair:
```text
a == rho*c mod h.
```
The corresponding rank-2 lattice in Z^2 has determinant h.
Therefore in a box
```text
|a|<=A,
|c|<=C
```
the number of primitive points on that root line is
```text
O(1+A*C/h).
```
The same holds for the second pair.
Thus, for dyadic boxes with areas
```text
Area_- ~ A*C,
Area_+ ~ D*K,
```
### Theorem 101-B — DualRootPacketLatticeBound
The number of primitive norm rectangles satisfying h|N_- and h|N_+ in one
fixed pair of Gaussian root classes is
```text
O((1+Area_-/h)*(1+Area_+/h)).
```
After summing over all root packets,
```text
# rectangles
 << h^{o(1)}
    (1+Area_-/h)
    (1+Area_+/h).
```
This is an exact geometry-of-numbers bound.
## 101.3 Specialization to the one-factor locus
On the one-factor locus,
```text
N_-=h*u^2.
```
So the first primitive Gaussian pair is not merely on a root line; it is a
Gaussian square packet over a norm-h orientation.
Equivalently there is a Gaussian integer kappa of norm h and a primitive
Gaussian integer z such that
```text
a+i*c = epsilon*kappa*z^2.
```
The number of possible orientations kappa for fixed h is
```text
2^omega(h)=h^{o(1)}.
```
If N_-<=Y, then
```text
Norm(z)<=sqrt(Y/h).
```
Hence the total number of primitive first pairs on the one-factor locus
with fixed h and N_-<=Y is
```text
<< h^{o(1)}*sqrt(Y/h).
```
This square-root saving is stronger than the raw lattice-line count on the
first side.
## 101.4 Why the dual lattice theorem still does not close M3 thinness
The exact product-height cutoff is
```text
N_-*N_+<=B^2.
```
For fixed h and first Gaussian square parameter z, the second norm satisfies
```text
N_+<=B^2/N_-.
```
The root-line condition modulo h gives the ambient second-pair estimate
```text
#(d0,k)
 << h^{o(1)}*(1+B^2/(h*N_-)).
```
Since N_-=h*u^2,
```text
#(d0,k)
 << h^{o(1)}*(1+B^2/(h^2*u^2)).
```
Summing this ambient estimate over all primitive Gaussian z does not produce
an absolute count below the known M3 denominator scale B^(1/3).
The large family of moving h values destroys the gain.
Thus:
### Negative Result 101-C — DualRootPacketAloneInsufficient
The exact prime-power dual root-line geometry at h, even combined with the
Gaussian-square reconstruction of the first norm, is not by itself enough
to prove
```text
M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness.
```
A further use of the already-imposed M3 equation
```text
D^2=N_-*N_+-X^2
```
or a genuine same-M3 distribution theorem across the moving norm fibers is
still required.
This rules out a second tempting shortcut:
```text
"large h + determinant h^2 automatically gives density zero."
```
It does not.
## 101.5 Correct target after the failed raw-lattice summation
The useful exact structure now consists of three simultaneous facts:
```text
(1) N_-=h*u^2,
(2) both primitive Gaussian pairs lie on root lines mod h,
(3) D^2=N_-*N_+-X^2 and X^2=a*c*d0*k.
```
The next theorem must exploit (3) together with (1)-(2), not only count
root lines in the ambient rectangle.
A natural formulation is:
### Receiver 101-R1 — M3ConditionedDualGaussianIncidence
For the smooth genus-one M3 fibers from Section100, prove a uniform
congruence-counting estimate for rational points whose second Gaussian pair
lies in a prescribed root class modulo a moving divisor h of the first
norm.
Equivalently, prove a same-M3 large-sieve statement on the family of
Section100 even quartics with moduli h that are themselves common Gaussian
norm cores.
This is now a substantially narrower target than full seven-squareclass
equidistribution, but it is still a genuine moving-family theorem.
## 101.6 Exact state after Section101
```text
H_FULL_PRIME_POWER_ROOT_LINES=true
DUAL_GAUSSIAN_ROOT_PACKET_COUNT_4POWOMEGA=true
DUAL_ROOT_PACKET_LATTICE_DETERMINANT_H2=true
ONE_FACTOR_FIRST_GAUSSIAN_SQUARE_RECONSTRUCTION=true
RAW_DUAL_ROOT_PACKET_GLOBAL_SUMMATION_SUFFICIENT=false
M3_CONDITIONED_DUAL_GAUSSIAN_INCIDENCE=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=M3ConditionedDualGaussianIncidence
```

# SECTION102 — the physical M3 Gaussian root line modulo h^2
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact physical congruence theorem + reconstruction bound; global moving-h distribution still open
Section101 used the common Gaussian core
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+)
```
on the two primitive norm pairs.  The M3 equation itself gives a third,
stronger root line directly in physical coordinates.
Recall
```text
N_-=(a^2+c^2)/2,
N_+=(d0^2+k^2)/2,
R_phys^2=N_-*N_+,
D^2=Y^2+Z^2,
X^2=a*c*d0*k,
```
so
```text
D^2+X^2=N_-*N_+.
```
## 102.1 h is coprime to the physical odd edge X
Use the Master dictionary
```text
h=gcd(W1,V2)*gcd(W2,V1).
```
Let p divide gcd(W1,V2).
Since (U1,V1,W1) is a primitive Pythagorean triple,
```text
gcd(W1,U1)=1.
```
Since (U2,V2,W2) is primitive,
```text
gcd(V2,U2)=1.
```
Hence p divides neither U1 nor U2.
The same argument applies to every prime dividing gcd(W2,V1).
Because
```text
X=U1*U2/gcd(U1,U2),
```
we obtain:
### Theorem 102-A — CommonCoreOddEdgeCoprimality
```text
gcd(h,X)=1.
```
This is exact and pointwise.
## 102.2 The M3 pair (D:X) is a Gaussian root line modulo h^2
Since
```text
h|N_-,
h|N_+,
```
we have
```text
h^2 | N_-*N_+.
```
Using
```text
D^2+X^2=N_-*N_+,
```
gives
```text
D^2+X^2 == 0 mod h^2.
```
By Theorem102-A, X is a unit modulo h^2.  Therefore
```text
(D/X)^2 == -1 mod h^2.
```
Every prime divisor of h is 1 mod4, so T^2+1 has exactly two Hensel-stable
roots at each prime power.
Hence:
### Theorem 102-B — PhysicalM3RootLineModuloH2
For fixed h, every one-factor M3 point has
```text
D == rho*X mod h^2
```
for one of exactly
```text
2^omega(h)=h^{o(1)}
```
Gaussian roots rho of -1 modulo h^2.
Thus the already-imposed M3 condition creates a genuinely physical
projective root-line packet of modulus h^2.
This is stronger in modulus than the source-side root lines modulo h from
Section101.
## 102.3 Geometry of the physical root lattice
Fix one rho with
```text
rho^2 == -1 mod h^2.
```
The lattice
```text
Lambda_{h,rho}
 ={(X,D) in Z^2:
   D==rho*X mod h^2}
```
has determinant h^2.
Moreover every nonzero lattice vector satisfies
```text
h^2 | X^2+D^2.
```
Hence its Euclidean norm is at least h.
For the physical disk
```text
0<X<=B,
0<D<=B,
```
standard planar lattice counting gives
```text
#(Lambda_{h,rho} intersect disk_B)
 << 1+B/h+B^2/h^2.
```
After summing over the root packets:
### Theorem 102-C — PhysicalRootLineAmbientCount
For fixed h,
```text
#{(X,D):
   0<X,D<=B,
   gcd(X,h)=1,
   h^2|X^2+D^2}
 << h^{o(1)}
    (1+B/h+B^2/h^2).
```
This is an ambient physical-coordinate bound, not yet an M3 relative bound.
## 102.4 Fixed (X,D) has divisor-many brick multiplicity
Fix positive integers X,D.
Any compatible Euler brick must have
```text
Y^2+Z^2=D^2.
```
The number of positive integer representations of a fixed D as a
Pythagorean hypotenuse is bounded by a divisor function:
```text
#{(Y,Z):Y^2+Z^2=D^2}
 <= D^{o(1)}.
```
For each such pair, the adjacent-face conditions
```text
X^2+Y^2=P^2,
X^2+Z^2=Q^2
```
are deterministic checks.
After imposing primitive/canonical conventions only fewer points remain.
Therefore:
### Theorem 102-D — FixedPhysicalXDMultiplicity
Uniformly for X,D<=B,
```text
#{E in M3(B):
   odd edge X(E)=X,
   opposite face diagonal D(E)=D}
 <= B^{o(1)}.
```
Thus the moving-h problem can be formulated entirely on the physical
(X,D) plane.
## 102.5 Exact physical reformulation of the remaining wall
Combining Theorems102-B and 102-D, every one-factor M3 point determines a
triple
```text
(h,X,D)
```
such that
```text
h^2 | X^2+D^2,
gcd(h,X)=1,
D/X is a Gaussian root mod h^2,
```
and each fixed (X,D) supports only B^{o(1)} bricks.
Therefore the current one-factor thinness problem is equivalent, up to
B^{o(1)} multiplicity, to controlling on the occupied physical M3 image
the moving square divisor
```text
h^2 | X^2+D^2.
```
The key point is that h is not an arbitrary divisor:
it is exactly
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+)
```
and on the one-factor locus
```text
sqf(h)=sqf(N_-).
```
## 102.6 Negative result: ambient physical lattice summation is still too weak
If one ignores M3 occupancy and sums Theorem102-C over h, the resulting
bound is still far above the certified denominator scale
```text
M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
```
In particular, the determinant h^2 does not by itself compensate for the
polynomially many possible moving h values.
Therefore:
### Negative Result 102-E — AmbientPhysicalRootLineInsufficient
The physical congruence
```text
D^2 == -X^2 mod h^2
```
is a genuine M3-conditioned transverse constraint, but ambient lattice
counting on the (X,D) plane alone does not prove one-factor density zero.
The unresolved statement is now explicitly a same-M3 distribution theorem
for the moving common core h.
## 102.7 New minimal receiver
### Receiver 102-R1 — PhysicalM3MovingCommonCoreSieve
Prove on exact primitive canonical M3(B) that the subset for which
```text
h(E)^2 | X(E)^2+D(E)^2,
gcd(h(E),X(E))=1,
sqf(h(E))=sqf(N_-(E))
```
has relative frequency o(1), where h(E)=gcd(N_-(E),N_+(E)).
Equivalent formulations are:
1. same-M3 cancellation across the Gaussian root classes
   `D/X mod h^2`;
2. a square-divisor sieve for `X^2+D^2` restricted to the exact M3 image;
3. a congruence-counting theorem on the Section100 genus-one family with
   moving modulus h^2.
This is strictly narrower than the original seven-squareclass
local-to-global problem.
## 102.8 Exact state after Section102
```text
GCD_H_X_EQUALS_ONE=true
H2_DIVIDES_X2_PLUS_D2=true
PHYSICAL_D_OVER_X_GAUSSIAN_ROOT_MOD_H2=true
PHYSICAL_ROOT_LINE_MODULUS_H2=true
FIXED_X_D_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
AMBIENT_PHYSICAL_ROOT_LINE_SUMMATION_SUFFICIENT=false
PHYSICAL_M3_MOVING_COMMON_CORE_SIEVE=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=PhysicalM3MovingCommonCoreSieve
```
The next attack should search for an already available same-measure
congruence theorem on the K_c / Section32 odd-edge divisor model that can
see the physical pair (X,D) modulo a moving square modulus h^2.

# SECTION103 — physical recovery of h, four-corner routing, and Stage14 crosswalk
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact physical dictionary + exact routing; direct Reuss transfer blocked by split-product eliminant
Section102 left the moving common core
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+)
```
as the live modulus.  This section shows that h is already a canonical
physical cross-gcd and identifies the exact endpoint/root-line packet it
creates.
## 103.1 Exact physical recovery of the two coprime pieces of h
Use the unique-odd-edge Master notation
```text
X=U1*U2/g,
Y=V1*U2/g,
Z=U1*V2/g,
P=W1*U2/g,
Q=U1*W2/g,
g=gcd(U1,U2).
```
Write
```text
U1=g*a,
U2=g*b,
gcd(a,b)=1.
```
Then
```text
Y=V1*b,
Z=a*V2,
P=W1*b,
Q=a*W2.
```
Primitivity of the two Pythagorean triples gives
```text
gcd(V1,a)=1,
gcd(W2,b)=1,
gcd(W1,a)=1,
gcd(V2,b)=1.
```
Hence
```text
gcd(Y,Q)=gcd(V1,W2),
gcd(Z,P)=gcd(V2,W1).
```
Define
```text
d_phys:=gcd(Y,Q),
p_phys:=gcd(Z,P).
```
The Section89/86 common core is therefore
### Theorem 103-A — PhysicalCrossCoreRecovery
```text
h=d_phys*p_phys.
```
Moreover
```text
gcd(d_phys,p_phys)=1.
```
Indeed a common odd prime would divide both legs/hypotenuses inside one
primitive Pythagorean face, impossible.
Thus h is not a hidden source-coordinate modulus.  It is recovered directly
from the physical Euler brick by
```text
h=gcd(Y,Q)*gcd(Z,P).
```
## 103.2 Canonical routing of every prime power of h
Let p^e||h.
Because the two factors in Theorem103-A are coprime, the full prime power
p^e belongs to exactly one of the two physical cross gcds.
Therefore exactly one of the following holds:
```text
Type Y:
  p^e | Y,
  p^e | Q,
  p does not divide Z*P;
Type Z:
  p^e | Z,
  p^e | P,
  p does not divide Y*Q.
```
Equivalently, with
```text
h_Y:=gcd(h,Y)=gcd(h,Q),
h_Z:=gcd(h,Z)=gcd(h,P),
```
we have
### Theorem 103-B — PhysicalPrimePowerRouting
```text
h=h_Y*h_Z,
gcd(h_Y,h_Z)=1,
h_Y | gcd(Y,Q),
h_Z | gcd(Z,P),
```
and every prime power of h is routed wholly to one side.
In fact Theorem103-A gives equality:
```text
h_Y=gcd(Y,Q),
h_Z=gcd(Z,P).
```
after the fixed labeling of Y,Z/P,Q.
## 103.3 The same routing from the Gaussian root orientations
In Section100 coordinates,
```text
N_-=(a^2+c^2)/2,
N_+=(d0^2+k^2)/2.
```
For p^e||h choose roots of -1 modulo p^e and write
```text
a/c  == epsilon*i_p mod p^e,
d0/k == delta*i_p mod p^e,
epsilon,delta in {+1,-1}.
```
Using
```text
2Y=c*k-d0*a,
2Z=a*k-d0*c,
```
one gets:
```text
epsilon=delta
  => p^e|Z and p does not divide Y;
epsilon=-delta
  => p^e|Y and p does not divide Z.
```
Thus the Gaussian orientation packet and the physical cross-gcd routing are
the same local datum.
This gives an exact interpretation of the local orientation entropy:
the apparently independent 4 choices per prime are precisely the choice of
which physical cross edge receives the common-core prime power together
with the local square-root sign.
## 103.4 Endpoint square-modulus consequences
Suppose p^e|h_Y.  Then p^e|Y and gcd(p,X)=1.
Since
```text
P^2-X^2=Y^2,
```
we have
```text
P^2 == X^2 mod p^(2e).
```
Thus
```text
P/X
```
is a square root of +1 modulo p^(2e).
Similarly, if p^e|h_Z then
```text
Q^2 == X^2 mod p^(2e).
```
Consequently:
### Theorem 103-C — CrossCoreEndpointRootPacket
For every common-core packet,
```text
P/X is a root of +1 modulo h_Y^2,
Q/X is a root of +1 modulo h_Z^2,
D/X is a root of -1 modulo h^2.
```
All three statements are Hensel-stable at every odd prime power.
This is a fully physical triple-root packet:
```text
Gaussian/source side:
  roots of -1 mod h;
endpoint adjacent-face side:
  roots of +1 mod h_Y^2 and h_Z^2;
M3 opposite-face side:
  root of -1 mod h^2.
```
No modulus is to be charged twice; these are correlated images of the same
common core h.
## 103.5 Normalized physical eliminant
Write
```text
Y=d*y,
Q=d*q,
Z=p*z,
P=p*r,
```
where
```text
d=gcd(Y,Q),
p=gcd(Z,P),
gcd(p,d)=1,
h=p*d.
```
The two adjacent-face identities are
```text
p^2*r^2-X^2=d^2*y^2,
d^2*q^2-X^2=p^2*z^2.
```
Subtracting gives
```text
p^2*(r^2+z^2)
 =
d^2*(q^2+y^2).
```
Because gcd(p,d)=1, there is an integer M such that
```text
r^2+z^2=d^2*M,
q^2+y^2=p^2*M.
```
Also
```text
R_phys^2=h^2*M.
```
The reciprocal completion can be written as the two split products
```text
(p*r-d*y)(p*r+d*y)=X^2,
(d*q-p*z)(d*q+p*z)=X^2.
```
Therefore
### Theorem 103-D — PhysicalSplitProductEliminant
After fixing the physical common-core split (p,d), the exact completion
satisfies
```text
(p*r-d*y)(p*r+d*y)
 =
(d*q-p*z)(d*q+p*z)
 =
X^2.
```
This is the current exact eliminant between the correlated root-line blocks.
## 103.6 Crosswalk to the late Stage14 obstruction
The late Stage14 q10 packet consists of:
1. a primitive Gaussian root line with local root t^2=-1 over a moving
   common core;
2. a primitive endpoint root line with local root t^2=+1 over the same
   already-charged core;
3. an exact reciprocal physical completion;
4. only subpolynomial orientation entropy.
Theorems103-B/C show that the present M3 common-core packet is of exactly
that arithmetic species.
The Stage14 literature radar identifies Reuss's determinant-sensitive
bilinear/trilinear point-count theorem as the first deterministic transfer
test only if the reciprocal completion reduces to an irreducible bilinear
or trilinear eliminant with a genuinely large coefficient determinant.
Theorem103-D shows that the first M3 eliminant is instead a split product
equality.
Hence:
### Negative Result 103-E — DirectReussTransferNotYetAvailable
The present physical common-core packet does not yet satisfy the required
Reuss bridge merely from Theorem103-D.
The split equality has the same diagonal/proportional danger recorded in
Stage14-X3:
```text
fixed-value fibers may be divisor-small,
while the equal-value incidence can still carry a large diagonal family.
```
Therefore one must retain the physical cross-role locks and M3 completion
before claiming any determinant saving.
The Stage14 q10/X3 results are thus relevant methodology and obstruction
certificates, not a theorem that can be cross-promoted to M3 closure.
## 103.7 Current best exact formulation
The moving-core problem can now be stated without source coordinates.
For a primitive canonical M3 brick define
```text
d=gcd(Y,Q),
p=gcd(Z,P),
h=p*d.
```
A one-factor survivor must satisfy the Section99 square-kernel parity and
the correlated physical packet
```text
d | Y,Q,
p | Z,P,
P^2 == X^2 mod d^2,
Q^2 == X^2 mod p^2,
D^2 == -X^2 mod h^2,
(p*r-d*y)(p*r+d*y)
 =
(d*q-p*z)(d*q+p*z)
 =
X^2.
```
This is considerably narrower than an unrestricted moving-modulus
equidistribution problem.
### Receiver 103-R1 — PhysicalCrossCoreReciprocalIncidence
Prove a fixed-power saving for the exact physical incidence above after
all of the following are retained simultaneously:
```text
primitive/canonical M3,
physical R<=B,
coprime split h=p*d,
Gaussian root orientations,
endpoint +/- root orientations,
both split-product equalities,
square-kernel parity from Section99.
```
A valid proof may use a determinant-sensitive bilinear/trilinear theorem
only after deriving a non-split eliminant with a determinant not already
paid for by the common-core spacing.
## 103.8 Exact state after Section103
```text
H_PHYSICAL_RECOVERY_EQUALS_GCD_Y_Q_TIMES_GCD_Z_P=true
H_COPRIME_PHYSICAL_SPLIT=true
COMMON_CORE_PRIME_POWER_ROUTES_TO_EXACTLY_ONE_EVEN_EDGE=true
ENDPOINT_PLUSONE_ROOTS_MOD_SPLIT_CORE_SQUARED=true
M3_MINUSONE_ROOT_MOD_H_SQUARED=true
PHYSICAL_SPLIT_PRODUCT_ELIMINANT=true
STAGE14_Q10_ARITHMETIC_SPECIES_CROSSWALK=true
DIRECT_REUSS_TRANSFER_AVAILABLE=false
PHYSICAL_CROSSCORE_RECIPROCAL_INCIDENCE_POWER_SAVING=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=CrossCoreNonSplitEliminantOrDiagonalExclusion
```
The smallest next task is exactly the Stage14-q10 handoff specialized to
the simpler M3 physical variables:
1. classify the proportional/diagonal solutions of Theorem103-D under the
   full physical masks;
2. if that branch is negligible or impossible, derive the off-diagonal
   non-split eliminant and test Reuss immediately;
3. otherwise freeze the surviving diagonal family as the new exact
   obstruction rather than hiding it inside a generic large-sieve target.

# SECTION104 — matched Gaussian square extraction in the physical (X,D) plane
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact Gaussian factor extraction + new fixed-D anti-concentration; no global density closure yet
Section103 identified the physical common core
```text
h=gcd(Y,Q)*gcd(Z,P)
```
and proved
```text
gcd(h,X)=1,
h^2 | X^2+D^2.
```
This section extracts the full Gaussian square carried by h and records a
new exact M3 anti-concentration coordinate.
## 104.1 Correction about the raw split-product proportional branch
The raw identity
```text
A*B=C*E,
A=P-Y,
B=P+Y,
C=Q-Z,
E=Q+Z
```
is equivalently the rank-one condition
```text
(A,C) is proportional to (E,B).
```
Therefore this proportionality is not an exceptional diagonal branch: it
is the entire bilinear variety.
Hence one must not claim that proportionality alone forces a=c or Y=Z.
The only immediate degeneration is the stronger same-orientation equality
A=C and B=E, which indeed gives the already-excluded symmetric case.
This correction confirms that the raw bilinear determinant
`A*B-C*E` cannot by itself supply a Reuss saving.
## 104.2 Gaussian square extraction from h^2 | X^2+D^2
Let
```text
Z_phys := X+iD.
```
For every p^e||h, p==1 mod4 and p does not divide X*D.
Factor in Z[i]
```text
p=pi_p*conj(pi_p).
```
Because p does not divide both X and D, the two conjugate Gaussian primes
cannot both divide Z_phys.
Since
```text
p^(2e) | Norm(Z_phys),
```
exactly one of pi_p or conj(pi_p) divides Z_phys to exponent at least 2e.
Choose the oriented Gaussian factor
```text
kappa_h
 = product_{p^e||h} Pi_p^e,
```
where Pi_p is the unique conjugate orientation occurring in Z_phys.
Then
```text
Norm(kappa_h)=h
```
and
```text
kappa_h^2 | Z_phys
```
in Z[i].
Therefore:
### Theorem 104-A — PhysicalMatchedGaussianSquareExtraction
There exists a Gaussian integer zeta such that
```text
X+iD = epsilon*kappa_h^2*zeta,
Norm(kappa_h)=h,
```
with epsilon a Gaussian unit.
Moreover
```text
Norm(zeta)
 = (X^2+D^2)/h^2
 = (N_-/h)*(N_+/h).
```
On the one-factor locus, writing
```text
N_-/h=u^2,
N_+/h=v,
gcd(u,v)=1,
```
gives
```text
Norm(zeta)=u^2*v.
```
Thus the moving common core h is literally a matched Gaussian square factor
of the physical terminal pair (X,D).
## 104.3 Fixed (h,J) has subpolynomial physical multiplicity
Define
```text
J := (X^2+D^2)/h^2.
```
Fix h and J.
The number of Gaussian orientations kappa_h of norm h is
```text
<=2^omega(h)=B^{o(1)}.
```
The number of Gaussian integers zeta of norm J is
```text
r_2(J)=J^{o(1)}.
```
Therefore the number of physical pairs (X,D) satisfying
```text
X+iD=epsilon*kappa_h^2*zeta
```
is B^{o(1)}.
By Section102-D, each fixed (X,D) supports at most B^{o(1)} primitive
canonical M3 bricks.
Hence:
### Theorem 104-B — FixedMatchedCoreQuotientMultiplicity
For every fixed pair (h,J),
```text
#{E in M3(B):
   h(E)=h,
   (X(E)^2+D(E)^2)/h^2=J}
 <= B^{o(1)}.
```
On the one-factor locus J additionally has the coprime factorization
```text
J=u^2*v.
```
This is the physical analogue of the matched-common-core reconstruction
pattern seen in late Stage14.
It does not by itself compress the total number of moving (h,J) pairs
enough to prove density zero.
## 104.4 Fixed opposite-face diagonal D has subpolynomial M3 multiplicity
Fix D.
First solve
```text
Y^2+Z^2=D^2.
```
The number of positive integer representations is bounded by a divisor
function:
```text
#{(Y,Z):Y^2+Z^2=D^2}
 <= D^{o(1)}.
```
Now fix one such Y.
The adjacent-face equation
```text
X^2+Y^2=P^2
```
is equivalent to
```text
(P-X)(P+X)=Y^2.
```
Thus the number of positive integer pairs (X,P) is bounded by
```text
tau(Y^2)=D^{o(1)}.
```
The second adjacent-face condition
```text
X^2+Z^2=Q^2
```
only removes candidates.
Primitive/canonical conventions again only reduce the count.
Therefore:
### Theorem 104-C — FixedOppositeFaceDiagonalMultiplicity
Uniformly for D<=B,
```text
#{E in M3(B): D(E)=D}
 <= B^{o(1)}.
```
This is independent of the one-factor condition.
## 104.5 Small-D region is M3-negligible
Using
```text
M3(B)>>B^(1/3),
```
Theorem104-C implies that for every fixed epsilon>0,
```text
#{E in M3(B):
   D(E)<=B^(1/3-epsilon)}
 =o(M3(B)).
```
Hence, after deleting an M3-negligible population, every surviving
one-factor point simultaneously satisfies the previously proved lower
bounds
```text
X    >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
D    >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
N_-  >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
N_+  >= B^(1/3-epsilon),
C_-  >= B^(1/3-epsilon).
```
These are exact physical anti-concentration statements, not heuristic size
assumptions.
## 104.6 Why the matched (h,J) compression is still insufficient
The exact physical height gives
```text
h^2*J=X^2+D^2<=B^2.
```
Fixed (h,J) fibers are B^{o(1)}, but the ambient number of integer pairs
```text
(h,J): h^2*J<=B^2
```
is still polynomially too large compared with the certified denominator
scale B^(1/3).
Thus:
### Negative Result 104-D — MatchedCorePairCountAloneInsufficient
The Gaussian extraction
```text
X+iD=epsilon*kappa_h^2*zeta
```
and the fixed-(h,J) B^{o(1)} multiplicity do not by themselves prove
one-factor thinness.
A further correlation between
```text
J=u^2*v
```
and the physical face-completion data is required.
## 104.7 Revised exact obstruction
The current survivor packet can now be written as
```text
X+iD=epsilon*kappa_h^2*zeta,
Norm(kappa_h)=h,
Norm(zeta)=u^2*v,
N_-=h*u^2,
N_+=h*v,
gcd(u,v)=1,
h=gcd(Y,Q)*gcd(Z,P),
```
together with the two adjacent-face square conditions producing P,Q.
This makes the remaining obstruction a matched Gaussian-square /
reciprocal-completion incidence, rather than a generic moving-modulus
problem.
### Receiver 104-R1 — MatchedGaussianSquareReciprocalCompletion
Prove a fixed-power saving for the number of physical M3 completions of
```text
X+iD=epsilon*kappa^2*zeta
```
when
```text
Norm(kappa)=h,
Norm(zeta)=u^2*v,
gcd(u,v)=1,
```
and the exact adjacent-face square conditions and Section99 square-kernel
parity are retained.
The raw rank-one equation A*B=C*E is not sufficient; the saving must come
from the reciprocal completion beyond that identity.
## 104.8 Exact state after Section104
```text
RAW_SPLIT_PRODUCT_PROPORTIONALITY_IS_NOT_EXCEPTIONAL=true
PHYSICAL_MATCHED_GAUSSIAN_SQUARE_EXTRACTION=true
FIXED_H_J_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
FIXED_D_M3_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
SMALL_D_REGION_M3_NEGLIGIBLE=true
MATCHED_CORE_PAIR_COUNT_ALONE_SUFFICIENT=false
MATCHED_GAUSSIAN_SQUARE_RECIPROCAL_COMPLETION=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=MatchedGaussianSquareReciprocalCompletion
```
The next proof task is to insert the adjacent-face conditions directly into
```text
X+iD=epsilon*kappa_h^2*zeta
```
and determine whether the remaining completion becomes:
1. an irreducible bilinear/trilinear incidence with determinant carrying a
   fresh fixed power of h; or
2. a genuinely new low-dimensional curve family whose fixed-fiber
   multiplicity can be bounded uniformly.

# SECTION105 — canonical physical Gaussian squareclass equality
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact equivalence for the one-factor receiver + rational/integer square reformulations
Section104 extracted the moving common core as a Gaussian square factor of
the physical pair X+iD.  This section gives a different and sharper
reformulation: the one-factor condition itself is exactly equality of two
canonical physical Gaussian squareclasses.
Use the Section100 odd norm-rectangle coordinates
```text
A=(a+i*c)/(1+i),
B=(d0+i*k)/(1+i).
```
Because a,c,d0,k are odd and the two coordinate pairs are primitive,
```text
A,B in Z[i]
```
are primitive Gaussian integers and
```text
Norm(A)=N_-,
Norm(B)=N_+.
```
## 105.1 Physical Gaussian product identities
Direct multiplication gives
```text
A*B = Q+iY,
A*conj(B) = P-iZ,
conj(A)*B = P+iZ.
```
Indeed
```text
(a+i c)(d0+i k)
 =2i*(Q+iY),
```
while
```text
(a+i c)(d0-i k)
 =2*(P-iZ),
```
and the factors of 2 are exactly cancelled by the
`(1+i)` denominators.
Thus the two physical terminal Gaussian integers
```text
Q+iY,
P+iZ
```
are the two mixed products obtained by conjugating the first primitive norm
factor.
## 105.2 Gaussian gcd decomposition of the physical common core
Define Gaussian gcds, up to units,
```text
eta   = gcd_Z[i](A,B),
theta = gcd_Z[i](A,conj(B)).
```
Because A and B are primitive, for each rational prime dividing both norms
exactly one Gaussian orientation divides A, and the B orientation is either
the same or conjugate.
Hence the rational prime powers split uniquely between eta and theta.
Let
```text
p=gcd(P,Z),
d=gcd(Q,Y).
```
Using the product identities in 105.1:
- common Gaussian orientation between A and B produces a square Gaussian
  factor in P+iZ but no rational common factor in Q+iY;
- opposite Gaussian orientation produces a rational common factor in Q+iY.
Prime-by-prime one obtains:
### Theorem 105-A — GaussianGcdPhysicalCrossCoreDictionary
```text
Norm(eta)=p=gcd(P,Z),
Norm(theta)=d=gcd(Q,Y),
p*d=h=gcd(N_-,N_+).
```
The norms p,d are coprime.
Thus Section103's physical split of h is exactly the norm split of the two
Gaussian gcds.
## 105.3 Remove the common Gaussian core
Write
```text
A=epsilon*eta*theta*A0,
B=epsilon'*eta*conj(theta)*B0,
```
where all units are absorbed into epsilon,epsilon'.
Then
```text
Norm(A0)=N_-/h,
Norm(B0)=N_+/h,
gcd(Norm(A0),Norm(B0))=1.
```
The physical products become, up to Gaussian units,
```text
(Q+iY)/d
 = eta^2*A0*B0,
```
and
```text
(P+iZ)/p
 = conj(theta)^2*conj(A0)*B0.
```
Therefore
```text
[p*(Q+iY)]/[d*(P+iZ)]
 =
 square * [A0/conj(A0)].
```
## 105.4 Primitive Gaussian phase-square lemma
Let Z0 be a primitive Gaussian integer of odd norm.
Then
### Lemma 105-B — PrimitiveGaussianPhaseSquare
```text
Z0/conj(Z0) in Q(i)^{*2}
iff
Norm(Z0) in Z^2.
```
Proof:
factor Z0 in the UFD Z[i].
Primitivity means that for every split rational prime, at most one member
of a conjugate prime pair occurs in Z0.
The valuation of
```text
Z0/conj(Z0)
```
at a Gaussian prime is therefore plus or minus the corresponding valuation
of Z0.  The ratio is a square in Q(i) iff every such valuation is even.
That is equivalent to every rational-prime exponent in Norm(Z0) being even,
i.e. Norm(Z0) being an integer square.
The unit quotient is also a square because for a Gaussian unit epsilon,
```text
epsilon/conj(epsilon)=epsilon^2.
```
## 105.5 Canonical physical Gaussian-square criterion
Apply Lemma105-B to A0.
Since
```text
Norm(A0)=N_-/h,
```
and
```text
L_- square
iff
N_-/h square,
```
we obtain:
### Theorem 105-C — PhysicalGaussianSquareclassEquality
For every primitive canonical physical M3 brick,
```text
L_- is an integer square
```
iff
```text
Xi(E)
 :=
 p*(Q+iY) / [d*(P+iZ)]
```
is a square in Q(i), where
```text
p=gcd(P,Z),
d=gcd(Q,Y).
```
Equivalently,
```text
[(Q+iY)/d]
 =
[(P+iZ)/p]
```
in
```text
Q(i)^* / Q(i)^{*2}.
```
This is a fully canonical physical statement.
No Master tuple, Euclid source pair, or hidden Gaussian orientation appears
in the criterion.
It is exactly equivalent to the current one-factor receiver, not merely a
necessary condition.
## 105.6 Rational half-angle square criterion
Put
```text
T:=X^2+Y^2+Z^2=N_-*N_+,
S:=P*Q+Y*Z.
```
Because
```text
P^2+Z^2=Q^2+Y^2=T,
```
we have
```text
Xi
 =
 (p/d) *
 [(S+i*(Y*P-Q*Z))/T].
```
Its norm is
```text
Norm(Xi)=(p/d)^2.
```
For a rational complex number x+i y of positive square norm r^2,
```text
x+i y in Q(i)^{*2}
```
iff
```text
(r+x)/2 in Q^2
```
(equivalently `(r-x)/2 in Q^2`).
Therefore Theorem105-C gives:
### Theorem 105-D — CanonicalPhysicalRationalSquareReceiver
```text
L_- square
```
iff
```text
Psi(E)
 :=
 p*(T+P*Q+Y*Z)/(2*d*T)
```
is a rational square.
This criterion is again completely physical.
## 105.7 Exact factor check in norm-rectangle coordinates
Section100 gives
```text
P*Q+Y*Z=a*c*N_+,
T=N_-*N_+.
```
Hence
```text
T+(P*Q+Y*Z)
 = N_+*(N_-+a*c)
 = N_+*(a+c)^2/2.
```
Since
```text
h=p*d,
```
we get
```text
Psi(E)
 = (a+c)^2 /
   [4*d^2*(N_-/h)].
```
Thus Psi is a rational square exactly when N_-/h is an integer square,
independently confirming Theorem105-D.
Similarly,
```text
T-(P*Q+Y*Z)
 =N_+*(a-c)^2/2,
```
so the plus/minus half-angle versions are equivalent away from the already
excluded a=c degeneration.
## 105.8 Single integer square-sieve receiver
Because multiplication by a rational square does not change whether a
rational number is a square, Theorem105-D is equivalent to an integer
square condition.
Multiplying Psi by
```text
(2*d*T)^2
```
gives
```text
2*p*d*T*(T+P*Q+Y*Z).
```
Since p*d=h:
### Theorem 105-E — PhysicalIntegerSquareSieveReceiver
```text
L_- square
```
iff the positive integer
```text
S_-(E)
 :=
 2*h*T*(T+P*Q+Y*Z)
```
is an integer square.
Equivalently one may use the minus version
```text
2*h*T*(T-P*Q-Y*Z).
```
In Section100 coordinates,
```text
S_-(E)
 =
 [N_+*(a+c)]^2 * [h*N_-].
```
Hence its squarefree kernel is exactly the squarefree kernel of h*N_-.
Because h|N_-,
```text
h*N_- square
iff
N_-/h square.
```
Thus Theorem105-E is exact, not a relaxation.
This is the simplest current form for a possible Heath-Brown-style square
sieve on exact M3: one positive physical integer sequence, with no Gaussian
denominators.
## 105.9 Finite sanity check: the Euler brick (44,117,240)
Take the canonical labeling
```text
X=117,
Y=44,
Z=240,
P=125,
Q=267,
D=244.
```
Then
```text
d=gcd(Y,Q)=1,
p=gcd(Z,P)=5,
h=5.
```
The Section100 coordinates are
```text
d0=gcd(P-Y,Q-Z)=27,
a=3,
c=1,
k=169.
```
Therefore
```text
N_-=5,
N_+=14645,
N_-/h=1.
```
Indeed
```text
L_-=1521=39^2.
```
This brick is a one-factor survivor but not a perfect cuboid.
For the new rational receiver,
```text
Psi(E)
 =
 5*(T+P*Q+Y*Z)/(2*T)
 =4,
```
so the criterion detects the survivor exactly.
This is only a consistency check, not theorem evidence.
## 105.10 Literature transfer diagnostic
Theorem105-E puts the remaining problem into the formal shape required by a
square sieve:
```text
count E in exact M3(B)
such that
S_-(E) is a square.
```
Heath-Brown's square sieve and modern explicit quadratic large-sieve
inequalities can control squares in a weighted integer sequence once one has
suitable quadratic-character cancellation for that sequence.
Likewise quadratic large-sieve inequalities over number fields can detect
the Gaussian squareclass version in Theorem105-C.
However, none of those off-the-shelf inequalities supplies the missing
input automatically here.  The weight sequence is the exact primitive
canonical M3 population, and
```text
S_-(E)
```
contains the moving physical gcd
```text
h=gcd(Y,Q)*gcd(Z,P).
```
Thus the required character sums are still of same-M3 type:
```text
sum_{E in M3(B)} chi(S_-(E)),
```
or the Gaussian-Hecke analogue.
No theorem located so far proves the needed uniform cancellation with the
M3 condition, physical height, primitivity, canonical counting, and moving
gcd all retained.
The benefit of Theorem105-E is therefore reduction of the target, not
closure: the unresolved local-to-global theorem is now a single scalar
square-sieve problem rather than a multi-squareclass packet.
## 105.11 Exact state after Section105
```text
PHYSICAL_GAUSSIAN_PRODUCT_IDENTITIES=true
GAUSSIAN_GCD_PHYSICAL_CROSSCORE_DICTIONARY=true
PRIMITIVE_GAUSSIAN_PHASE_SQUARE_LEMMA=true
ONE_FACTOR_IFF_PHYSICAL_GAUSSIAN_RATIO_SQUARE=true
ONE_FACTOR_IFF_CANONICAL_RATIONAL_FUNCTION_SQUARE=true
ONE_FACTOR_IFF_SINGLE_PHYSICAL_INTEGER_SQUARE=true
OFF_THE_SHELF_QUADRATIC_LARGE_SIEVE_CLOSES_M3=false
SAME_M3_SINGLE_SEQUENCE_CHARACTER_CANCELLATION=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=SingleSequenceM3SquareSieve
```
The next attack should use
```text
S_-(E)=2*h*T*(T+P*Q+Y*Z)
```
as the sole square-sieve receiver and determine whether Section32's exact
odd-edge divisor coordinates make the character sums
```text
sum_{E in M3(B)} (S_-(E)/ell)
```
factor or reduce to the already-proved Section6 conditional character sums
without losing the exact physical M3 weight.

# SECTION106 — minimal physical square receiver and exact local factor law
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact receiver compression + rigorous one-factor local marginal; same-M3 transfer still open
Section105 reduced one-factor thinness to a single physical square sequence.
This section compresses that sequence further and derives the exact local
finite-field law for the individual minus factor.
## 106.1 Minimal physical integer square receiver
Recall
```text
G=gcd(PQ,YZ),
L_-=(PQ-YZ)/G.
```
Therefore
```text
G*(PQ-YZ)=G^2*L_-.
```
Hence:
### Theorem 106-A — MinimalPhysicalMinusSquareReceiver
```text
L_- is an integer square
```
iff
```text
F_-(E):=G*(PQ-YZ)
```
is an integer square.
Thus Section105's larger integer
```text
S_-(E)=2*h*T*(T+PQ+YZ)
```
has the same squareclass but is not the minimal square-sieve receiver.
The preferred scalar receiver is now
```text
F_-(E)=gcd(PQ,YZ)*(PQ-YZ).
```
Equivalently one may retain the normalized coprime factor itself
```text
L_-=(PQ-YZ)/gcd(PQ,YZ),
```
but F_- avoids denominators.
## 106.2 Section32 half-angle factorization
Use the exact odd-edge divisor coordinates
```text
u=(P-Y)/X,
v=(Q-Z)/X.
```
Then
```text
P/X=(u+u^{-1})/2,
Y/X=(u^{-1}-u)/2,
Q/X=(v+v^{-1})/2,
Z/X=(v^{-1}-v)/2.
```
Direct expansion gives
### Theorem 106-B — HalfAngleTerminalFactorization
```text
PQ-YZ
 = X^2*(u^2+v^2)/(2uv),
```
and
```text
PQ+YZ
 = X^2*(1+u^2*v^2)/(2uv).
```
Therefore the Section32 space numerator
```text
S(u,v)
 =(u^2+v^2)*(1+u^2*v^2)
```
is exactly the product of the two canonical terminal factors before their
common squareclass normalization.
Define
```text
A_-(u,v):=u^2+v^2,
A_+(u,v):=1+u^2*v^2.
```
The current one-factor problem is the global/physical version of selecting
the minus factor A_- after the common gcd has been removed.
## 106.3 Euler local polynomial
The Euler / third-face condition in the same coordinates is
```text
E(u,v)
 =v^2*(1-u^2)^2
  +u^2*(1-v^2)^2
```
or equivalently
```text
E(u,v)
 =u^2+v^2-4u^2v^2+u^4v^2+u^2v^4.
```
For a good odd prime p, work on the nonzero affine torus
```text
(u,v) in (F_p^*)^2
```
and remove the O(p) exceptional locus where any relevant factor vanishes.
The local Euler condition is
```text
chi_p(E)=+1.
```
## 106.4 Character sum for the individual minus factor
We need the conditional mean of
```text
chi_p(A_-)
```
on the Euler-square locus.
Using
```text
1_{chi(E)=+1}
 =(1+chi(E))/2
```
away from E=0, the numerator bias is controlled by
```text
sum chi(A_-)
```
and
```text
sum chi(A_-*E).
```
The first is O(p) after summing one quadratic character in one variable.
For the second, fix v.
As a polynomial in u,
```text
A_-(u,v)*E(u,v)
```
has degree 6.
Its exact u-discriminant is
```text
-2^14
 *v^22
 *(v-1)^4*(v+1)^4
 *(v^2-2v-1)^2
 *(v^2+2v-1)^2.
```
Hence for all but O(1) values of v the degree-6 polynomial is squarefree
and is not a square in F_p[u].
For every such v the Weil bound gives
```text
|sum_u chi_p(A_-*E)|
 <=5*sqrt(p).
```
The exceptional v-slices contribute only O(p).
Therefore
### Theorem 106-C — OneFactorLocalMarginal
For every good odd prime p,
```text
sum_{u,v in (F_p^*)^2}
  1_{E nonzero square}
  *chi_p(u^2+v^2)
 =O(p^(3/2)).
```
The number of Euler-square points is
```text
p^2/2+O(p^(3/2)).
```
Consequently
```text
Pr(
  chi_p(u^2+v^2)=+1
  |
  E(u,v) nonzero square
 )
 =
 1/2+O(p^(-1/2)).
```
This is the correct local marginal for the canonical minus factor.
It is not a consequence of the old product-character law alone.
## 106.5 The plus factor has the same marginal
The involution
```text
u -> 1/u
```
satisfies exactly
```text
u^4*E(1/u,v)=E(u,v),
```
and
```text
u^2*A_-(1/u,v)=A_+(u,v).
```
The prefactors are squares on F_p^*.
Hence the same conditional law holds for the plus factor:
### Theorem 106-D — PlusFactorLocalMarginal
```text
Pr(
  chi_p(1+u^2v^2)=+1
  |
  E(u,v) nonzero square
 )
 =
 1/2+O(p^(-1/2)).
```
## 106.6 Joint two-factor local law
Section6 already proves, in the same good-prime parameter measure, the
conditional product-character law
```text
Pr(
 chi_p(A_-*A_+)=+1
 |
 E square
)
=
1/2+O(p^(-1/2)).
```
Equivalently, the conditional expectation of
```text
chi_p(A_-)*chi_p(A_+)
```
is O(p^(-1/2)).
Sections106-C and 106-D give the two marginal expectations
O(p^(-1/2)).
For two {+1,-1}-valued variables X,Y,
```text
P(X=e1,Y=e2)
 =1/4*
  [1
   +e1 E(X)
   +e2 E(Y)
   +e1 e2 E(XY)].
```
Therefore:
### Theorem 106-E — LocalTerminalFactorPairUniformity
On the good-prime Euler-square parameter measure,
```text
Pr(
 chi_p(A_-)=epsilon_-,
 chi_p(A_+)=epsilon_+
 |
 E square
)
 =
1/4+O(p^(-1/2))
```
uniformly for
```text
epsilon_-,epsilon_+ in {+1,-1}.
```
Thus the two terminal factors are locally asymptotically unbiased and
pairwise independent at good odd primes, after conditioning on the Euler
square.
This is strictly more tailored to the current one-factor receiver than the
old Section6 product law.
## 106.7 Fixed finite prime-set consequence in parameter residue measure
For any fixed finite set of good odd primes
```text
S={p_1,...,p_r},
```
CRT gives the corresponding complete residue-space law:
```text
Pr(
 chi_p(A_-)=+1
 for every p in S
 |
 E square at every p in S
)
 =
2^(-r)*
prod_{p in S}(1+O(p^(-1/2))).
```
The same statement holds jointly for prescribed plus/minus factor signs.
This is an exact fixed-finite-prime local/parameter theorem.
It is not yet a theorem on physical M3(B).
## 106.8 Why Section6 still does not close the current receiver
The important distinction is now explicit.
Section6 and Theorem106-E control residue classes of
```text
u^2+v^2
```
and
```text
1+u^2v^2
```
in the complete local Master/half-angle parameter measure conditioned on
the Euler polynomial being a square.
The physical one-factor condition is instead
```text
L_-=(PQ-YZ)/gcd(PQ,YZ)
```
being a global integer square.
At primes not dividing the moving common gcd, the local one-factor bit is
exactly the A_- character from Theorem106-C.
At primes dividing the common gcd, valuation parity and the h/K_- routing
from Sections99-105 matter.
Therefore the remaining global theorem must transfer the fixed-prime local
bias
```text
1/2+O(p^(-1/2))
```
to the exact primitive canonical M3 population while simultaneously
controlling the moving common-gcd strata.
No currently imported theorem performs that transfer.
## 106.9 Revised minimal global receiver
The current local-to-global target can now be stated in a very small form.
### Receiver 106-R1 — SameM3MinusFactorCharacterTransfer
For a growing set of good odd primes p not absorbed by the moving common
gcd, prove on exact physical M3(B) that
```text
chi_p(
  (PQ-YZ)/gcd(PQ,YZ)
)
```
has average o(1), uniformly enough to run a square sieve.
Equivalently, using the integral receiver,
```text
chi_p(F_-(E))
```
should exhibit the same cancellation after the appropriate zero/gcd strata
are separated.
A fixed finite-prime version would already be a genuine same-M3
equidistribution theorem.
A growing-prime version with summable errors would prove
M3CanonicalMinusFactorThinness.
## 106.10 Exact state after Section106
```text
MINIMAL_PHYSICAL_INTEGER_RECEIVER_FMINUS=true
HALFANGLE_TERMINAL_TWO_FACTOR_DICTIONARY=true
ONE_FACTOR_LOCAL_MINUS_MARGINAL_HALF=true
ONE_FACTOR_LOCAL_PLUS_MARGINAL_HALF=true
LOCAL_TERMINAL_FACTOR_PAIR_UNIFORM_QUARTERS=true
FIXED_FINITE_PRIME_PARAMETER_CRT=true
SAME_M3_MINUS_FACTOR_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
GROWING_PRIME_M3_SQUARE_SIEVE=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=SameM3MinusFactorCharacterTransfer
```
The next step should not recompute local character sums again.
It should use one of the exact physical decompositions already available:
1. the Section32 odd-edge divisor one-object/one-count coordinates; or
2. the Section100 norm rectangle,
and prove a fixed-prime physical congruence count with an explicit error
term for the A_- / F_- character.
Only after such a fixed-prime same-M3 theorem is obtained should the prime
cutoff be allowed to grow.

# SECTION107 — theorem audit for the fixed-prime transfer wall
## 2026-09-27
## Status: literature applicability audit; no new closure theorem
Section106 isolates the remaining task as a fixed-prime same-M3 character
transfer for the individual terminal factor.
The relevant theorem families were re-audited against this exact target.
## 107.1 Uniform elliptic-fiber point counts do not give congruence transfer
Dujella's uniform bounded-height theorem for elliptic curves with rational
prime-order torsion, and the earlier Bombieri--Zannier line of results,
control the total number of rational points on one elliptic curve/fiber.
They are exactly the right input for Sections39, 98 and 100 fixed-fiber
anti-concentration.
They do not provide a theorem of the form
```text
sum_{P in E_t(Q), H(P)<=B}
  chi_p(f_t(P))
 = o(#points)
```
uniformly across a moving family of t.
Therefore they do not imply SameM3MinusFactorCharacterTransfer.
## 107.2 Toric adelic equidistribution remains ambient
Huang's equidistribution/geometric-sieve theorem for smooth projective split
toric varieties gives:
- effective counting in adelic neighbourhoods;
- explicit dependence on finite adelic level;
- geometric/Ekedahl sieve consequences for infinitely many local conditions.
This remains the strongest off-the-shelf modulus-uniform distribution
technology near the old toric host.
However the present population is already the degree-2 Euler/K3 lift:
```text
toric host point
 -> Euler-square lift
 -> physical primitive canonical M3 point.
```
Theorem106-C is conditional on the Euler-square locus, not ambient toric
measure.
No theorem located in this audit promotes Huang's ambient equidistribution
to the conditional exact M3 measure with the required relative error.
Thus the standing firewall remains valid:
```text
TORIC_ADELIC_EQUIDISTRIBUTION_AVAILABLE=true
TORIC_TO_EXACT_M3_CONDITIONAL_TRANSFER=false
```
## 107.3 Integral/elliptic large-sieve results are also fiber-local
Results combining repulsion, heights and the large sieve for integral or
rational points on elliptic curves give strong uniform point-count bounds
for individual curves and some families.
Their large sieve is used to bound the number of points themselves.
It is not a ready-made congruence equidistribution theorem for the exact
M3-weighted sequence across the moving K3 family.
Hence they do not fill Receiver106-R1 directly.
## 107.4 Denominator-scale check from the standard Euler-brick family
The classical Saunderson construction starts from a primitive Pythagorean
triple (u,v,w) and produces an Euler brick with edge coordinates of total
degree 3 in (u,v,w).
After writing the primitive Pythagorean triple in Euclid parameters
(m,n), the resulting Euler-brick edges have total degree 6 in (m,n).
Therefore a two-parameter box of Euclid parameters of size H naturally
produces bricks of height H^6 and contains H^2 parameter pairs.
The corresponding family-count scale is therefore
```text
B^(2/6)=B^(1/3).
```
This matches the exponent of the currently certified global lower corridor
```text
M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
```
This observation does not prove that B^(1/3) is the true order of M3(B).
It only shows that the standard two-parameter degree-6 family does not
offer an immediate route to a stronger denominator such as B^(1/2).
Thus the present Class-3 difficulty cannot be bypassed merely by replacing
the denominator lower bound with an obvious stronger Saunderson-family
count.
## 107.5 Consequence for strategy
The local side is now complete enough for the one-factor receiver:
```text
good-prime minus marginal = 1/2+O(p^-1/2),
good-prime plus marginal  = 1/2+O(p^-1/2),
joint pair law            = 1/4+O(p^-1/2).
```
The fixed-fiber side is also strong:
```text
fixed t      -> B^o(1),
fixed X      -> B^o(1),
fixed D      -> B^o(1),
fixed N_-    -> B^o(1),
fixed N_+    -> B^o(1),
fixed (h,J)  -> B^o(1).
```
What is missing is genuinely between these two regimes:
> distribution across polynomially many moving fibers under exact M3
> conditioning.
No audited external theorem removes that step.
Therefore the next research should stop searching for another local law or
another fixed-fiber theorem and instead build an explicit physical
fixed-prime counting statement.
## 107.6 Exact next target
### Receiver 107-R1 — FixedPrimePhysicalM3Count
For one fixed good odd prime p and sign epsilon in {+1,-1}, prove
```text
#{E in M3(B):
   p does not divide the relevant common-gcd stratum,
   chi_p(L_-(E))=epsilon}
 =
(1/2+O(p^-1/2))*M3_good,p(B)
 + error_p(B),
```
with
```text
error_p(B)=o(M3(B))
```
for fixed p.
An equivalent weighted character form is
```text
sum_{E in M3_good,p(B)}
  chi_p(L_-(E))
 =o(M3(B)).
```
Even one such fixed-prime theorem would be a genuine new same-M3
equidistribution result.
The first proof attempt should use the Section32 one-object/one-count
odd-edge divisor coordinates because they preserve the physical height
exactly and already expose the finite-field polynomials E(u,v), A_-(u,v).
## 107.7 Exact state after Section107
```text
ONE_FACTOR_LOCAL_THEORY_SUFFICIENT_FOR_SIEVE_INPUT=true
UNIFORM_FIXED_FIBER_POINT_COUNTS_AVAILABLE=true
TORIC_AMBIENT_ADELIC_EQUIDISTRIBUTION_AVAILABLE=true
TORIC_TO_EXACT_M3_CONDITIONAL_TRANSFER=false
OFF_THE_SHELF_FIXED_PRIME_M3_CHARACTER_THEOREM_FOUND=false
SAUNDERSON_STANDARD_FAMILY_SCALE_B_ONE_THIRD=true
FIXED_PRIME_PHYSICAL_M3_COUNT=false
SAME_M3_MINUS_FACTOR_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=FixedPrimePhysicalM3Count
```
The next calculation should write the Section32 exact physical count as a
sum over the odd edge X and divisor coordinates A,C, then insert the fixed
character chi_p(A_-) before the Euler-square indicator.
The key question is whether, after fixing X and the exact physical height
window, the divisor sums can be completed modulo p with an error that is
uniform enough to sum over X.
