# SECTION108 — correction of the Section106 marginal and common-divisor-sliced transfer
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact squareclass correction + exact divisor-convolution reduction; no same-M3 closure yet
Section106 correctly computed the finite-field marginal of the raw half-angle
factor
```text
A_-(u,v)=u^2+v^2,
```
but its interpretation as the local squareclass of the normalized canonical
factor L_- was too strong.
The moving common Gaussian core contributes a nontrivial twist.
This section records the exact correction and the resulting analytic
reduction.
## 108.1 Exact common twist between raw and normalized terminal factors
Use Section100 coordinates
```text
P-Y=d0*a,
Q-Z=d0*c,
Q+Z=a*k,
P+Y=c*k,
```
with
```text
N_-=(a^2+c^2)/2,
N_+=(d0^2+k^2)/2,
h=gcd(N_-,N_+).
```
The Section32 half-angle coordinates are
```text
u=(P-Y)/X=d0*a/X,
v=(Q-Z)/X=d0*c/X.
```
Hence
```text
A_-(u,v)
 =u^2+v^2
 =2*d0^2*N_-/X^2.
```
Section89/100 gives
```text
L_-=g_-^2*(N_-/h).
```
Therefore
```text
A_-/L_-
 =
2*h * (d0/(X*g_-))^2.
```
Thus in Q^*/Q^{*2},
### Theorem 108-A — CommonTwistMinusFactor
```text
[A_-]=[2*h*L_-].
```
Similarly,
```text
u*v=d0/k,
```
so
```text
A_+(u,v)
 :=1+u^2v^2
 =(d0^2+k^2)/k^2
 =2*N_+/k^2.
```
Since
```text
L_+=g_+^2*(N_+/h),
```
we obtain
### Theorem 108-B — CommonTwistPlusFactor
```text
[A_+]=[2*h*L_+].
```
The same moving squareclass 2h twists both terminal factors.
## 108.2 Why the old product law survives
Multiplying Theorems108-A/B,
```text
[A_-*A_+]
 =
[(2h)^2*L_-*L_+]
 =
[L_-*L_+].
```
Hence the common twist cancels in the product squareclass.
This explains retrospectively why the old Section6 character law for
```text
(u^2+v^2)(1+u^2v^2)
```
is genuinely adapted to the terminal PRODUCT / space condition, while an
individual marginal of A_- is not automatically a marginal of L_-.
Thus:
```text
SECTION6_PRODUCT_INTERPRETATION_VALID=true,
SECTION106_RAW_MINUS_MARGINAL_VALID=true,
SECTION106_RAW_PLUS_MARGINAL_VALID=true,
SECTION106_RAW_PAIR_UNIFORMITY_VALID=true,
```
but
```text
SECTION106_RAW_MINUS_EQUALS_NORMALIZED_LMINUS_LOCAL_BIT=false.
```
Theorem106-C remains a correct theorem about A_-.
Only its normalization interpretation is superseded.
## 108.3 Fixed-prime relation on the good stratum
Let ell be a good odd prime with
```text
ell does not divide 2*X*h.
```
Then all rational square factors in Theorem108-A are ell-adic units and
```text
chi_ell(L_-)
 =
chi_ell(A_-)*chi_ell(2h).
```
Equivalently,
```text
chi_ell(L_-)
 =
chi_ell(2)*chi_ell(A_-)*chi_ell(h).
```
Thus even away from primes dividing the common core, the value of h modulo
ell remains in the normalized one-factor character.
The obstruction is not merely the bad-divisibility stratum ell|h.
## 108.4 Finite sanity check on the standard brick
For the Euler brick
```text
(X,Y,Z)=(117,44,240)
```
with the corresponding labeling
```text
P=125,
Q=267,
h=5,
L_-=1521,
```
take ell=7.
Since L_- is a square,
```text
chi_7(L_-)=+1.
```
But
```text
u=(P-Y)/X=81/117=9/13,
v=(Q-Z)/X=27/117=3/13,
```
and
```text
chi_7(u^2+v^2)=-1.
```
Also
```text
chi_7(2h)=chi_7(10)=-1.
```
Therefore
```text
chi_7(A_-)
 =
chi_7(2h)*chi_7(L_-)
 =-1,
```
exactly as Theorem108-A predicts.
This is a consistency check only.
## 108.5 Divisor convolution for the moving twist
Fix an odd prime ell and let
```text
chi=chi_ell
```
be the quadratic Dirichlet character modulo ell.
Define the multiplicative function
```text
lambda_ell := mu * chi
```
under Dirichlet convolution.
Then for every positive integer n,
```text
chi(n)
 =
sum_{d|n} lambda_ell(d).
```
Hence on the good stratum ell∤h,
```text
chi(h)
 =
sum_{d|h} lambda_ell(d).
```
Since
```text
h=gcd(N_-,N_+),
```
the condition d|h is exactly
```text
d|N_-
and
d|N_+.
```
Therefore:
### Theorem 108-C — CommonDivisorSliceExpansion
On the stratum ell∤2Xh,
```text
chi_ell(L_-)
 =
chi_ell(2)
 *
sum_{d|N_-, d|N_+}
 lambda_ell(d)
 *
chi_ell(A_-).
```
Consequently the exact physical character sum satisfies
```text
sum_{E in M3_good,ell(B)}
 chi_ell(L_-(E))
 =
chi_ell(2)
 *
sum_{d>=1}
 lambda_ell(d)
 *
C_d(B;ell),
```
where
```text
C_d(B;ell)
 :=
sum_{E in M3_good,ell(B),
    d|N_-(E),
    d|N_+(E)}
 chi_ell(A_-(E)).
```
This is an exact re-expression of the moving-gcd twist.
## 108.6 Support of the convolution coefficient
For a prime q != ell,
```text
lambda_ell(q^e)
 =
chi(q)^e-chi(q)^(e-1)
 =
chi(q)^(e-1)*(chi(q)-1).
```
Hence:
if
```text
chi_ell(q)=+1,
```
then
```text
lambda_ell(q^e)=0
```
for every e>=1.
If
```text
chi_ell(q)=-1,
```
then
```text
lambda_ell(q^e)= +/- 2.
```
At q=ell,
```text
lambda_ell(ell)=-1,
lambda_ell(ell^e)=0  for e>=2.
```
Therefore, away from the separated ell|h stratum, the common-divisor
expansion is supported only on integers whose prime factors are quadratic
NONRESIDUES modulo ell.
### Theorem 108-D — NonresidueSupportedCommonDivisorExpansion
The normalized one-factor character at ell is a weighted sum of raw
A_- characters over common-norm-divisor slices d, and all nontrivial
prime factors of d are forced into the nonresidue class modulo ell.
This is the exact point where the earlier Eulerian residue-graph
architecture re-enters the current physical norm-rectangle route.
## 108.7 Exact unification with the old Eulerian graph viewpoint
Sections91-95 encoded one-factor necessary conditions by a bipartite graph
of quadratic nonresidue relations between prime supports.
Theorem108-D gives a new interpretation:
the graph is not an independent auxiliary obstruction.
It is the support structure of the Möbius/Dirichlet convolution required to
remove the moving common twist h from the raw local factor A_-.
Thus the two formerly separate routes
```text
Eulerian moving-prime sieve
```
and
```text
physical norm-rectangle character transfer
```
are the same analytic problem viewed from opposite sides.
The current exact bridge is
```text
raw local A_- cancellation
 + common-divisor slice expansion
 -> normalized L_- character.
```
## 108.8 Structural obstruction to a per-X proof
In the exact Section32 count, once X is fixed the allowed A,C are divisors
of X^2.
Thus the residue support modulo ell on a fixed X-fiber may contain only a
very sparse set of classes; no uniform complete-residue character
cancellation can hold fiber-by-fiber in X.
This is consistent with Section97:
```text
fixed X contributes only B^{o(1)} points,
```
but there are polynomially many occupied X values.
Therefore a proof of Receiver107-R1 must average across moving X (or an
equivalent moving norm/fiber coordinate).  It cannot be obtained by proving
a uniform cancellation theorem separately for every fixed X.
This rules out the naive plan
```text
fix X
 -> complete divisor set A,C mod ell
 -> apply Section106 Weil bound.
```
The divisor set is not a complete residue box.
## 108.9 Finite exact diagnostic
The Section32 one-object/one-count enumerator was extended with the
one-factor predicate.
It reproduces the already-certified checkpoint
```text
M3(10^6)=219.
```
Among those 219 physical bricks,
```text
8
```
satisfy L_-=square.
A further local scratch run gives
```text
M3(2*10^6)=298,
one-factor survivors=10.
```
The second line is a finite computational diagnostic from the present
research session, not a hostile-audited theorem or asymptotic claim.
The finite survivor fraction is therefore visibly small in this range, but
no trend is promoted to proof.
## 108.10 Corrected next receiver
The fixed-prime problem should now be formulated with the common-divisor
slices.
### Receiver 108-R1 — CommonDivisorSlicedM3CharacterTransfer
For fixed good ell, prove a summable estimate for
```text
C_d(B;ell)
 =
sum_{E in M3(B),
    d|N_-(E),
    d|N_+(E)}
 chi_ell(A_-(E))
```
uniformly over the nonresidue-supported d that occur in Theorem108-C,
with enough control to sum against
```text
lambda_ell(d).
```
The physical masks must remain:
```text
primitive,
canonical,
R<=B,
Euler third-face square,
one object one count.
```
A successful theorem would combine:
1. the Section106 Weil cancellation on the raw A_- bit;
2. the Section101/102 root-line geometry forced by d|N_-,N_+;
3. the nonresidue support of lambda_ell(d);
4. averaging over the genuinely moving X / norm fibers.
This is more precise than Receiver107-R1 and explicitly includes the
moving-gcd normalization.
## 108.11 Exact state after Section108
```text
RAW_TERMINAL_FACTOR_LOCAL_MARGINALS_VALID=true
RAW_AND_NORMALIZED_TERMINAL_FACTORS_HAVE_COMMON_TWIST_2H=true
SECTION106_NORMALIZED_MINUS_INTERPRETATION_SUPERSEDED=true
PRODUCT_TERMINAL_LOCAL_LAW_UNAFFECTED=true
COMMON_DIVISOR_SLICE_EXPANSION=true
CONVOLUTION_SUPPORT_ONLY_ON_ELL_NONRESIDUE_PRIMES=true
EULERIAN_GRAPH_NORM_RECTANGLE_UNIFIED=true
PER_X_COMPLETE_RESIDUE_STRATEGY_INVALID=true
COMMON_DIVISOR_SLICED_M3_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
SAME_M3_MINUS_FACTOR_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=CommonDivisorSlicedM3CharacterTransfer
```
The next proof attempt should split the d-sum into ranges and use the exact
physical root-line condition
```text
d^2 | X^2+D^2
```
for d|h together with Section100's genus-one fiber structure, instead of
trying to complete sparse divisor sets at fixed X.

# SECTION109 — product-route re-evaluation and exact divisor-coordinate terminal character
## 2026-09-27
## Status: strategic correction + exact product-character formula + finite counterexample to the u=1 shortcut
Section108 shows that the normalized one-factor character carries a moving
common twist 2h.  This changes the strategic comparison between the
one-factor route and the original terminal-product route.
The one-factor route remains valuable for structural reconstruction and
absolute counting, but it is not automatically the cleaner route for a
local square sieve.
## 109.1 The terminal product has no moving-gcd twist
Let
```text
T:=X^2+Y^2+Z^2.
```
Perfect endpoint means
```text
T is an integer square.
```
Section32 gives
```text
R_phys^2=T
 =X^2*
  (u^2+v^2)*(1+u^2v^2)/(4u^2v^2).
```
Therefore, in Q^*/Q^{*2},
### Theorem 109-A — TerminalProductRawSquareclass
```text
[T]
 =
[(u^2+v^2)*(1+u^2v^2)].
```
The common twist from Section108 appears twice and cancels:
```text
[A_-*A_+]
 =[L_-*L_+].
```
Thus the old Section6 product-character law is exactly aligned with the
physical terminal square sequence T.
No moving gcd h needs to be normalized away.
## 109.2 Integral Section32 formula for the terminal character
Use the exact divisor coordinates
```text
A=P-Y,
C=Q-Z,
A,C|X^2,
0<A<C<X.
```
Section32 proves
```text
4*A^2*C^2*T
 =
(A^2+C^2)*(X^4+A^2*C^2).
```
Hence for every odd prime ell with
```text
ell does not divide A*C,
```
the square denominator disappears in the Legendre symbol:
### Theorem 109-B — ExactDivisorCoordinateTerminalCharacter
```text
chi_ell(T)
 =
chi_ell(
 (A^2+C^2)*(X^4+A^2*C^2)
).
```
The Euler/M3 condition in the same coordinates is
```text
C^2*(X^2-A^2)^2
+
A^2*(X^2-C^2)^2
 = square.
```
Therefore the fixed-prime product-transfer problem has an entirely integral
form on the exact one-object/one-count physical coordinates:
```text
sum_{X odd}
sum_{A<C<X, A,C|X^2}
 primitive_mask
 height_mask
 Euler_square_mask
 *
 chi_ell(
  (A^2+C^2)*(X^4+A^2*C^2)
 ).
```
There is no hidden normalization factor analogous to h.
## 109.3 Exact comparison of the two analytic routes
The current situation is:
### Route OF — one-factor
Target:
```text
L_- square.
```
Advantage:
- larger necessary-condition set;
- Gaussian reconstruction;
- root-line/common-core structure;
- fixed-norm/fixed-core anti-concentration.
Disadvantage:
```text
[A_-]=[2hL_-]
```
so individual local characters require the moving common core h.
### Route TP — terminal product
Target:
```text
T square.
```
Advantage:
- exactly the perfect endpoint predicate on M3;
- local observable is polynomial/rational before primitive gcd
  normalization;
- Section6 product-character theorem applies to the correct raw
  squareclass;
- no h-convolution is required.
Disadvantage:
- this is the original Class-3 same-M3 transfer wall;
- no weaker one-factor overcount is available.
Hence:
### Strategic conclusion 109-C
For local/large-sieve work, Route TP is currently cleaner.
For absolute counting, Gaussian reconstruction and structural exclusions,
Route OF remains useful.
The two routes should be kept in parallel rather than forcing the
one-factor route to carry the entire local-to-global transfer.
## 109.4 The u=1 finite shortcut is false
On the one-factor locus write
```text
N_-=h*u^2.
```
In the finite census through B=2*10^6, all observed one-factor survivors
had u=1.
This suggested the possible shortcut
```text
N_-=h.
```
The exact Section32 enumerator was pushed to B=5*10^6.
A counterexample occurs with
```text
X=1,792,317,
Y=2,974,356,
Z=1,532,960,
```
with adjacent face diagonals
```text
P=3,472,635,
Q=2,358,467,
```
and opposite-face diagonal
```text
D=3,346,156.
```
Its Section100 coordinates are
```text
d0=7437,
a=67,
c=111,
k=58081.
```
Hence
```text
N_-=8405=5*41^2,
N_+=1,714,355,765,
h=5.
```
Therefore
```text
u=41>1.
```
Also
```text
L_-=97,634,161
```
is an integer square.
Thus:
### Negative Result 109-D — NminusEqualsHNotUniversal
The implication
```text
L_- square
 => N_-=h
```
is false.
The general Gaussian-square quotient u must be retained.
This finite counterexample prevents an invalid simplification of the
one-factor route.
## 109.5 Finite one-factor census extension
The same exact Section32 enumerator gives
```text
B=5*10^6:
M3(B)=480,
one-factor survivors=15.
```
Among these 15:
```text
14 have u=1,
1 has u=41.
```
This is only a finite diagnostic.
No asymptotic claim is made.
It does suggest that the u=1 branch is numerically prominent at moderate
height, but it is not exhaustive.
## 109.6 Exact fixed-prime target for the product route
Define
```text
S_ell(B)
 :=
sum_{E in M3(B)}
 chi_ell(T(E)),
```
with the usual convention that chi_ell(T)=0 when ell|T.
A fixed-prime same-M3 theorem would be
### Receiver 109-R1 — FixedPrimeTerminalProductTransfer
For every fixed good odd prime ell,
```text
S_ell(B)=o(M3(B)).
```
More quantitatively, the expected local main term from Section6 is
```text
Pr(
 chi_ell(T)=+1
 |
 Euler condition
)
 =
1/2+O(ell^-1/2)
```
on the complete local parameter measure.
A modulus-uniform version would feed directly into a square sieve on T and
therefore attack P(B)/M3(B) without the h-convolution.
## 109.7 Why fixed-X completion still fails on the product route
The simplification in Theorem109-B does not remove the sparse-divisor issue.
For fixed X, the allowed A,C are divisors of X^2.
Their residue classes modulo ell can occupy an arbitrarily sparse
multiplicative subset.
Therefore the local Weil sum from Section6 still cannot be applied
fiber-by-fiber in X.
The transfer must average across moving X or use an equivalent global
universal-torsor / divisor-convolution coordinate system.
Thus both routes share the same fundamental global barrier:
```text
complete local residue cancellation
  -> exact sparse physical M3 population.
```
The difference is that Route TP no longer has the additional moving-gcd
normalization.
## 109.8 Updated research priority
For the next global analytic attempt, prioritize:
### Primary
```text
FixedPrimeTerminalProductTransfer
```
using Theorem109-B in exact Section32 coordinates.
### Secondary
```text
CommonDivisorSlicedM3CharacterTransfer
```
for the one-factor route, because it may still yield stronger absolute
structure or a fallback upper bound.
No existing theorem credit is revoked from Sections89-105; only the
analytic priority is changed.
## 109.9 Exact state after Section109
```text
TERMINAL_PRODUCT_HAS_NO_MOVING_GCD_TWIST=true
TERMINAL_PRODUCT_DIVISOR_COORDINATE_CHARACTER_EXACT=true
ONE_FACTOR_LOCAL_ROUTE_HAS_COMMON_TWIST_2H=true
NMINUS_EQUALS_H_UNIVERSAL=false
FINITE_U_GREATER_THAN_ONE_COUNTEREXAMPLE=true
FIXED_PRIME_TERMINAL_PRODUCT_TRANSFER=false
COMMON_DIVISOR_SLICED_M3_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
PERFECT_CUBOID_NONEXISTENCE_CLAIM=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=FixedPrimeTerminalProductTransfer
```
The next proof attempt should rewrite the exact Section32 divisor sum through
```text
A=d*a^2,
X/A=d*b^2,
C=e*c^2,
X^2/C=e*f^2,
```
with
```text
gcd(a,b)=gcd(c,f)=gcd(d,e)=1,
d*a*b=e*c*f=X,
```
so that the sparse divisor sets are replaced by primitive Pythagorean-face
variables before testing any global character-sum or large-sieve theorem.

# SECTION110 — exact four-variable primitive-face model for M3 and terminal square
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact one-object/one-count reparametrization of the Section32 divisor model
Section109 proposed replacing the sparse divisor variables A,C|X^2 by the
primitive Pythagorean-face variables hidden inside each divisor.
This section carries that replacement out exactly.
## 110.1 One primitive face
For the first face write
```text
A=d*a^2,
X^2/A=d*b^2,
```
with
```text
a<b,
gcd(a,b)=1,
a,b odd.
```
Then
```text
X=d*a*b,
Y=d*(b^2-a^2)/2,
P=d*(a^2+b^2)/2.
```
Similarly the second face has
```text
C=e*c^2,
X^2/C=e*f^2,
```
with
```text
c<f,
gcd(c,f)=1,
c,f odd,
```
and
```text
X=e*c*f,
Z=e*(f^2-c^2)/2,
Q=e*(c^2+f^2)/2.
```
The Section32 primitivity condition is exactly
```text
gcd(d,e)=1.
```
## 110.2 The face scales are uniquely determined by four primitive variables
Since
```text
d*a*b=e*c*f
```
and gcd(d,e)=1, put
```text
g:=gcd(a*b,c*f).
```
Then the reduced ratio is unique:
### Theorem 110-A — UniqueFaceScaleRecovery
```text
d=(c*f)/g,
e=(a*b)/g.
```
Consequently
```text
X=a*b*c*f/g.
```
Thus d,e are not extra free variables.
A primitive physical Euler-brick candidate is encoded by four positive odd
integers
```text
(a,b,c,f)
```
satisfying
```text
gcd(a,b)=1,
gcd(c,f)=1,
a<b,
c<f,
```
with the scales reconstructed by Theorem110-A.
## 110.3 Canonical face ordering becomes one bilinear inequality
Section32 uses A<C to remove the Y/Z face swap.
Substituting
```text
A=(c*f/g)*a^2,
C=(a*b/g)*c^2
```
gives
```text
A<C
iff
a*f<b*c.
```
Hence the one-object/one-count orientation is simply
```text
a*f<b*c.
```
No divisor comparison remains.
## 110.4 Exact four-variable physical coordinates
Let
```text
g=gcd(ab,cf).
```
Then
### Theorem 110-B — FourVariablePhysicalDictionary
```text
X=abcf/g,
Y=cf*(b^2-a^2)/(2g),
Z=ab*(f^2-c^2)/(2g),
P=cf*(a^2+b^2)/(2g),
Q=ab*(c^2+f^2)/(2g).
```
Every primitive canonical Euler-brick candidate occurs exactly once under
```text
a<b,
c<f,
af<bc.
```
This is the Section32 one-object/one-count model with the divisor variables
fully resolved.
## 110.5 Exact M3 square polynomial
Define
```text
E4(a,b,c,f)
 :=
c^2*f^2*(b^2-a^2)^2
+
a^2*b^2*(f^2-c^2)^2.
```
Using Theorem110-B,
```text
4*g^2*(Y^2+Z^2)=E4.
```
Therefore:
### Theorem 110-C — FourVariableM3Predicate
The physical candidate is in M3 iff
```text
E4(a,b,c,f)
```
is an integer square.
If
```text
E4=W^2,
```
then
```text
W=2*g*D.
```
Thus the Euler/M3 square predicate is independent of any additional moving
gcd normalization.
## 110.6 Exact terminal/space square polynomial
Define
```text
H4(a,b,c,f)
 :=
(a^2*f^2+b^2*c^2)
*
(a^2*c^2+b^2*f^2).
```
The half-angle identity gives
```text
4*g^2*T=H4,
```
where
```text
T=X^2+Y^2+Z^2.
```
Hence:
### Theorem 110-D — FourVariableTerminalPredicate
On M3,
```text
perfect endpoint
iff
H4(a,b,c,f) is an integer square.
```
The exact physical height cutoff R_phys<=B becomes
```text
H4(a,b,c,f)<=4*g^2*B^2.
```
Thus both relevant square predicates are polynomial in the same four
primitive variables.
## 110.7 Exact square-difference identity
A direct expansion gives
### Theorem 110-E — DoubleSquareDifferenceIdentity
```text
H4-E4=(2*a*b*c*f)^2.
```
Equivalently,
```text
4*g^2*T-(2*g*D)^2
 =(2*a*b*c*f)^2.
```
Dividing by 4g^2 recovers the physical identity
```text
T-D^2=X^2.
```
The importance of Theorem110-E is not the identity itself, which is
physically expected, but the fact that the entire Class-3 problem is now
written as the polynomial double-square problem
```text
E4 = square,
H4 = square,
H4-E4 = an explicit square
```
on four primitive variables.
## 110.8 Exact local character in the four-variable model
For a good odd prime ell away from the coordinate-zero locus,
```text
chi_ell(T)
 =
chi_ell(H4).
```
Since
```text
u=a/b,
v=c/f,
```
we have
```text
H4/(b^4*f^4)
 =
(u^2+v^2)*(1+u^2v^2).
```
Therefore the Section6 conditional product-character law is exactly the
finite-field law for H4 on the E4-square locus.
No common-core twist appears.
## 110.9 Third Pythagorean triple interpretation of the M3 condition
Define
```text
U3:=c*f*(b^2-a^2),
V3:=a*b*(f^2-c^2).
```
Then
```text
E4=U3^2+V3^2.
```
Thus M3 is exactly the requirement that
```text
(U3,V3,W)
```
form an integral Pythagorean triple, with
```text
W=2*g*D.
```
The two adjacent faces are already Pythagorean by construction, so the
Euler-brick condition is literally the compatibility condition that these
two primitive-face variables generate a third Pythagorean triple.
This opens a second exact route:
```text
do not leave 1_{E4=square} as a square indicator;
Euclid-parameterize the third triple as well.
```
Whether that produces a useful multiplicative torsor rather than simply
repackaging the K3 wall remains to be tested.
## 110.10 Fixed product anti-concentration
Put
```text
n:=a*b*c*f.
```
For fixed n, the number of ordered positive quadruples (a,b,c,f) with
product n is at most
```text
tau_4(n)=n^{o(1)}.
```
The coprimality, ordering, M3 and canonical masks only reduce this number.
Hence:
### Theorem 110-F — FixedPrimitiveFaceProductMultiplicity
Uniformly for n occurring below physical height B,
```text
#{E in M3(B): a*b*c*f=n}
 <=B^{o(1)}.
```
Also
```text
g<=sqrt(n),
X=n/g<=B
```
imply
```text
n<=B^2.
```
This is another exact anti-concentration coordinate, although its natural
range B^2 is too large for it alone to solve the density problem.
## 110.11 Exact counting formulation
The exact M3 population can now be written as
```text
M3(B)
 =
#{
 (a,b,c,f):
 a,b,c,f positive odd,
 gcd(a,b)=gcd(c,f)=1,
 a<b,
 c<f,
 af<bc,
 E4(a,b,c,f) is square,
 H4(a,b,c,f)
   <=4*gcd(ab,cf)^2*B^2
 }.
```
Perfect endpoints are exactly the same set with the extra condition
```text
H4(a,b,c,f) is square.
```
This is currently the cleanest exact global character-sum model.
## 110.12 Revised fixed-prime receiver
### Receiver 110-R1 — FourVariableConditionalCharacterTransfer
For each fixed good odd prime ell, prove
```text
sum_{primitive canonical quadruples,
    E4 square,
    physical height<=B}
 chi_ell(H4)
 =
o(M3(B)).
```
The local complete-residue analogue is already Section6 and has
O(ell^-1/2) bias.
The remaining issue is entirely global:
```text
primitive lattice + gcd-dependent height + exact E4-square population
```
must inherit enough of that local cancellation.
### Alternative Receiver 110-R2 — ThirdTripleTorsorTransfer
Euclid-parameterize
```text
U3^2+V3^2=W^2
```
and determine whether the resulting all-three-faces torsor has a
multiplicative structure on which a standard large sieve or geometry-of-
numbers theorem applies with the physical height retained.
## 110.13 Exact state after Section110
```text
FOUR_VARIABLE_PRIMITIVE_FACE_MODEL=true
FACE_SCALES_UNIQUELY_RECOVERED=true
CANONICAL_FACE_ORDER_EQUALS_AF_LT_BC=true
M3_IFF_E4_SQUARE=true
PERFECT_ENDPOINT_IFF_H4_SQUARE_ON_M3=true
PHYSICAL_HEIGHT_EQUALS_H4_OVER_4G2=true
H4_MINUS_E4_EQUALS_4A2B2C2F2=true
THIRD_PYTHAGOREAN_TRIPLE_MODEL=true
FIXED_FACE_PRODUCT_MULTIPLICITY_BO1=true
FOUR_VARIABLE_CONDITIONAL_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
THIRD_TRIPLE_TORSOR_TRANSFER=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
PERFECT_CUBOID_NONEXISTENCE_CLAIM=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=ThirdTripleTorsorTransfer
```
The next calculation should parameterize the primitive reduction of
```text
U3=c*f*(b^2-a^2),
V3=a*b*(f^2-c^2)
```
as a third Euclid triple, keeping track of its exact common gcd and 2-adic
orientation, and test whether the compatibility equations reduce to
bilinear/multiplicative relations with genuinely fewer free dimensions.

# SECTION111 — cross-gcd resolution of the four-variable model
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact unification of Sections100/108/110; third-triple parametrization gives no automatic dimension drop
Section110 gives the exact primitive-face variables
```text
(a,b,c,f),
gcd(a,b)=gcd(c,f)=1.
```
This section resolves their cross gcds and shows that the earlier primitive
norm rectangle appears canonically inside the four-variable model.
## 111.1 Four cross gcds
Define
```text
r:=gcd(a,c),
s:=gcd(a,f),
t:=gcd(b,c),
u:=gcd(b,f).
```
Because gcd(a,b)=gcd(c,f)=1, the four integers
```text
r,s,t,u
```
are pairwise coprime.
Write
```text
a=r*s*a0,
b=t*u*b0,
c=r*t*c0,
f=s*u*f0.
```
The residual variables are coprime to all cross factors in the evident
opposite positions, and
```text
gcd(a0*b0,c0*f0)=1.
```
Moreover
### Theorem 111-A — CrossGcdProduct
```text
gcd(a*b,c*f)=r*s*t*u.
```
Thus the Section110 quantity
```text
g=gcd(ab,cf)
```
is exactly
```text
g=r*s*t*u.
```
## 111.2 Face scales become the residual products
Section110 gives
```text
d=cf/g,
e=ab/g.
```
Substituting the cross-gcd resolution yields
### Theorem 111-B — ResidualFaceScales
```text
d=c0*f0,
e=a0*b0.
```
Hence the two face scales are precisely the parts not shared across the two
primitive face pairs.
## 111.3 Raw terminal factors
Define the two odd integers
```text
M_-:=(a^2*f^2+b^2*c^2)/2,
M_+:=(a^2*c^2+b^2*f^2)/2.
```
They are integral because all four variables are odd.
Using the cross-gcd factorization,
```text
a*f = r*u*s^2*a0*f0,
b*c = r*u*t^2*b0*c0,
```
so
```text
M_-
 =(r*u)^2
  *[(s^2*a0*f0)^2+(t^2*b0*c0)^2]/2.
```
Likewise
```text
a*c = s*t*r^2*a0*c0,
b*f = s*t*u^2*b0*f0,
```
so
```text
M_+
 =(s*t)^2
  *[(r^2*a0*c0)^2+(u^2*b0*f0)^2]/2.
```
Define
```text
N_-:=((s^2*a0*f0)^2+(t^2*b0*c0)^2)/2,
N_+:=((r^2*a0*c0)^2+(u^2*b0*f0)^2)/2.
```
Then:
### Theorem 111-C — RawToPrimitiveNormFactorization
```text
M_-=(r*u)^2*N_-,
M_+=(s*t)^2*N_+.
```
These N_-,N_+ are exactly the primitive norm factors of Section100 after
renaming coordinates.
## 111.4 Exact recovery of the common core h
Because r,s,t,u are pairwise coprime, the square multipliers
```text
(r*u)^2,
(s*t)^2
```
are coprime.
Moreover every prime dividing r*u divides exactly one coordinate inside the
first primitive norm pair defining N_+ and not the other, so it cannot divide
N_+; symmetrically s*t is coprime to N_-.
Therefore:
### Theorem 111-D — RawTerminalGcdEqualsCommonCore
```text
gcd(M_-,M_+)
 =gcd(N_-,N_+)
 =h.
```
Thus the moving common core h is already visible directly in the two raw
four-variable terminal factors.
No Master coordinates are required.
## 111.5 Terminal height and one-factor conditions
Since
```text
H4=4*M_-*M_+
```
and
```text
g=r*s*t*u,
```
Theorem111-C gives
```text
H4
 =4*g^2*N_-*N_+.
```
Hence the Section110 height identity
```text
4*g^2*T=H4
```
becomes exactly
```text
T=N_-*N_+.
```
This independently recovers Section100-C.
Similarly, because M_-/h differs from N_-/h by a square,
### Theorem 111-E — RawOneFactorCriterion
```text
L_- square
iff
M_-/h square,
```
where
```text
h=gcd(M_-,M_+).
```
Likewise
```text
L_+ square
iff
M_+/h square.
```
Thus the complete terminal factorization is visible from the raw four-
variable polynomials alone.
## 111.6 Transparent explanation of the Section108 twist
The raw half-angle minus factor is
```text
A_-=u_half^2+v_half^2
```
with
```text
u_half=a/b,
v_half=c/f.
```
Therefore
```text
A_-
 =(a^2*f^2+b^2*c^2)/(b^2*f^2)
 =2*M_-/(b^2*f^2).
```
Hence
```text
[A_-]=[2*M_-]
       =[2*h*(M_-/h)]
       =[2*h*L_-].
```
This recovers Theorem108-A without any hidden-coordinate calculation.
Similarly
```text
[A_+]=[2*M_+]=[2*h*L_+].
```
So the common twist 2h is structurally unavoidable for each individual raw
factor and structurally cancels in their product.
## 111.7 Product route in primitive norms
For every good odd prime ell away from zeros,
```text
chi_ell(H4)
 =chi_ell(M_-*M_+)
 =chi_ell(N_-*N_+),
```
because all omitted multipliers are squares.
Thus the terminal-product route can be stated equivalently as character
cancellation for the product of the two primitive Gaussian norms.
The perfect endpoint condition is
```text
M_-*M_+ square
```
or equivalently
```text
N_-*N_+ square.
```
Since h=gcd(N_-,N_+), this is equivalent to both
```text
N_-/h square,
N_+/h square.
```
The product route and the two-factor route are therefore not different
arithmetic conditions; they differ only in which squareclass normalization
is used analytically.
## 111.8 Third-face triple and 2-adic orientation
Recall
```text
U3=c*f*(b^2-a^2),
V3=a*b*(f^2-c^2).
```
Let
```text
q3:=gcd(U3,V3).
```
Since
```text
U3=2*g*Y,
V3=2*g*Z,
```
we have
### Theorem 111-F — ThirdTripleScale
```text
q3=2*g*gcd(Y,Z).
```
If E4 is square, then
```text
(U3/q3, V3/q3, sqrt(E4)/q3)
```
is a primitive Pythagorean triple.
Because a,b,c,f are odd,
```text
v2(b^2-a^2)>=3,
v2(f^2-c^2)>=3.
```
For the primitive reduction to have one odd and one even leg, necessarily
### Theorem 111-G — ThirdFace2AdicMismatch
```text
v2(b^2-a^2)
 !=
v2(f^2-c^2).
```
This is an exact M3 mask in the four-variable coordinates.
## 111.9 Euclid-parametrizing the third triple
After dividing by q3, there are unique coprime opposite-parity integers
```text
m>n>0
```
such that, according to which residual leg is even,
```text
U3/q3=m^2-n^2,
V3/q3=2mn,
```
or the two legs are swapped.
Substitution gives two compatibility equations between
```text
(a,b,c,f)
```
and
```text
(m,n,q3).
```
Eliminating q3 leaves exactly the ratio equation encoding
```text
Y/Z
```
as the Euclid slope of the third face.
Therefore:
### Negative Result 111-H — RawThirdTripleParametrizationDoesNotLowerDimension
Simply Euclid-parametrizing the third Pythagorean face does not by itself
reduce the dimension or remove the M3 wall.
It is an exact reparametrization of the already-imposed condition
```text
E4 square.
```
Any gain from the third-triple route would require an additional step:
for example, a gcd allocation producing a nontrivial bilinear/trilinear
torsor or a counting theorem adapted to the resulting compatibility
relations.
Thus ThirdTripleTorsorTransfer remains open but is not automatically better
than Receiver110-R1.
## 111.10 Strategic consequence
The current cleanest exact architecture is now:
```text
four primitive face variables
 -> raw norms M_-,M_+
 -> cross-gcd square factors
 -> primitive norms N_-,N_+
 -> h=gcd(M_-,M_+)=gcd(N_-,N_+)
 -> exact height T=N_-N_+.
```
For local transfer:
```text
product character:
  chi(H4)=chi(N_-N_+)
  no moving-gcd twist;
individual one-factor character:
  chi(L_-)
  requires normalization by h.
```
This confirms the Section109 priority:
use the product route for the first global fixed-prime transfer theorem,
while retaining the one-factor route for structural/absolute estimates.
## 111.11 Exact state after Section111
```text
FOUR_VARIABLE_CROSS_GCD_RESOLUTION=true
RAW_TERMINAL_FACTORS_MMINUS_MPLUS=true
MMINUS_EQUALS_SQUARE_TIMES_NMINUS=true
MPLUS_EQUALS_SQUARE_TIMES_NPLUS=true
GCD_MMINUS_MPLUS_EQUALS_H=true
SECTION108_COMMON_TWIST_REDERIVED=true
THIRD_FACE_2ADIC_MISMATCH=true
RAW_THIRD_TRIPLE_PARAMETRIZATION_LOWERS_DIMENSION=false
FIXED_PRIME_TERMINAL_PRODUCT_TRANSFER=false
FOUR_VARIABLE_CONDITIONAL_CHARACTER_TRANSFER=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
PERFECT_CUBOID_NONEXISTENCE_CLAIM=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=GlobalProductCharacterViaCrossGcdStrata
```
The next analytic attempt should stratify by the four pairwise-coprime cross
gcds r,s,t,u and test whether, after fixing their dyadic sizes, the residual
variables a0,b0,c0,f0 range in sufficiently regular boxes for a completion
modulo a fixed ell.
Unlike fixed-X divisor sets, these residual variables are not restricted to
divisors of one fixed integer; this is the first coordinate system in which
standard lattice/character-sum completion may plausibly apply without
changing the exact physical height.

# SECTION112 — simultaneous square-sieve route in the ambient four-variable model
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact mixed local sums + literature applicability check; global physical incomplete-sum estimate open
Sections110-111 show that the exact perfect-endpoint population is the
simultaneous-square locus
```text
E4(a,b,c,f) is square,
H4(a,b,c,f) is square,
```
inside the primitive/canonical physical region.
This suggests a different analytic route:
> avoid conditioning on the exact M3/K3 population first; instead apply
> square-sieve machinery simultaneously to E4 and H4 in the ambient
> four-variable lattice.
If successful, this would bypass the missing same-M3 equidistribution
theorem.
## 112.1 Ratio coordinates remove two redundant scales modulo p
For a good odd prime p and nonzero b,f set
```text
u=a/b,
v=c/f.
```
Then exactly
```text
E4=b^4*f^4*E(u,v),
H4=b^4*f^4*H(u,v),
```
where
```text
E(u,v)
 =v^2*(1-u^2)^2
  +u^2*(1-v^2)^2,
```
and
```text
H(u,v)
 =(u^2+v^2)*(1+u^2*v^2).
```
Therefore all quadratic characters of E4 and H4 depend only on (u,v).
The complete four-variable character sums are simply (p-1)^2 times the
corresponding two-variable sums.
## 112.2 Complete local sums for E, H and EH
For fixed v, the u-discriminants are:
```text
Disc_u(H)
 =
16*(v-1)^4*v^4*(v+1)^4*(1+v^2)^4.
```
For E:
```text
Disc_u(E)
 =
16*(v-1)^4*v^4*(v+1)^4
*(v^2-2v-1)^2
*(v^2+2v-1)^2.
```
For the product E*H:
```text
Disc_u(E*H)
 =
2^24
*(v-1)^8*v^40*(v+1)^8*(1+v^2)^4
*(v^2-2v-1)^2
*(v^2+2v-1)^2.
```
Thus, outside O(1) exceptional v-values, each relevant polynomial is
squarefree and non-square.
Weil's bound on each good v-slice gives O(sqrt(p)) cancellation, and the
exceptional slices contribute O(p).
Hence:
### Theorem 112-A — CompleteTwoVariableMixedCharacterBounds
For every good odd prime p,
```text
sum_{u,v in (F_p^*)^2} chi_p(E(u,v))
 =O(p^(3/2)),
```
```text
sum_{u,v in (F_p^*)^2} chi_p(H(u,v))
 =O(p^(3/2)),
```
and
```text
sum_{u,v in (F_p^*)^2} chi_p(E(u,v)H(u,v))
 =O(p^(3/2)).
```
The implied constants are absolute.
Multiplying by the free nonzero choices of b,f gives:
### Theorem 112-B — CompleteFourVariableMixedCharacterBounds
```text
sum_{a,b,c,f in (F_p^*)}
 chi_p(E4)
 =O(p^(7/2)),
```
```text
sum chi_p(H4)
 =O(p^(7/2)),
```
and
```text
sum chi_p(E4*H4)
 =O(p^(7/2)).
```
Thus the ambient four-variable family has square-root-quality saving
relative to its p^4 volume for all three nontrivial squareclass moments.
## 112.3 Local joint squareclass law without conditioning
For two {+1,-1}-valued quadratic characters away from the zero divisors,
Theorems112-A/B imply
```text
Pr(
 chi_p(E4)=epsilon_1,
 chi_p(H4)=epsilon_2
)
 =
1/4+O(p^-1/2)
```
uniformly in epsilon_1,epsilon_2.
Therefore the simultaneous-square problem has the correct local
independence input before any exact global square condition is imposed.
This is stronger for a double square sieve than merely knowing the
conditional law of H4 on the E4-square residue locus.
## 112.4 Why a simultaneous square sieve is analytically attractive
The former Route TP was
```text
first restrict to E4 = integer square,
then show H4 is rarely an integer square.
```
That requires same-M3/K3 equidistribution.
The simultaneous route instead counts directly
```text
#{ambient physical quadruples:
  E4 square,
  H4 square}.
```
A two-stage square sieve expands this count into ambient sums of the form
```text
sum w(a,b,c,f)
 chi_m(E4)
 chi_n(H4),
```
for squarefree moduli m,n built from sieve primes.
For coprime m,n, CRT separates the complete local analysis into the
single-modulus character sums supplied by Theorem112-B.
Thus the missing input moves from
```text
equidistribution of rational points on a K3 surface
```
to
```text
incomplete character-sum bounds over the exact physical four-variable
region.
```
This is a genuine simplification in the type of theorem required.
## 112.5 Physical dyadic boxes
Let a,b,c,f lie in dyadic ranges
```text
a~A,
b~B1,
c~C,
f~F,
```
with
```text
A<=B1,
C<=F.
```
Let
```text
G~gcd(ab,cf).
```
The exact physical height is
```text
sqrt(H4)/(2g)<=B_phys.
```
In a dyadic box,
```text
sqrt(H4)
asymp
max(A*F,B1*C)
*
max(A*C,B1*F).
```
Hence every occupied physical box satisfies
### Theorem 112-C — DyadicPhysicalProductConstraint
```text
max(AF,B1*C)
*
max(A*C,B1*F)
 ll G*B_phys.
```
In particular,
```text
A*B1*C*F
 ll G*B_phys.
```
Since
```text
G<=sqrt(A*B1*C*F),
```
this recovers the global bound
```text
A*B1*C*F ll B_phys^2.
```
The dyadic form is the correct region for incomplete-sum analysis.
## 112.6 Literature applicability: Bonolis--Browning
Bonolis and Browning prove uniform rational-point bounds for smooth
hyperelliptic fibrations of the form
```text
Y^2=X^n+X*f(U1,U2)+g(U1,U2),
```
with n>=3 odd, using a variant of the square sieve and q-van der Corput
differencing.
Their methodology is highly relevant because it retains an exact global
square condition while estimating ambient weighted character sums.
However their theorem does not apply directly here.
For fixed (a,c,f), the equation E4=W^2 becomes, with x=b^2,
```text
W^2
 =
c^2*f^2*x^2
+
a^2*(c^4+f^4-4c^2*f^2)*x
+
a^4*c^2*f^2.
```
Thus the natural x-degree is 2, while in b it is an even quartic.
The Bonolis--Browning main theorem assumes odd n>=3; their paper explicitly
notes that the parity restriction comes from an oscillatory-sum estimate
which may fail for even n.
Therefore:
### Negative Result 112-D — BonolisBrowningNotDirectlyApplicable
The published hyperelliptic-fibration theorem cannot be cited as an
off-the-shelf bound for the M3 surface E4=W^2.
Its square-sieve architecture is reusable, but the crucial incomplete
oscillatory sums must be re-proved for the present even-quartic / two-square
geometry.
## 112.7 Conic-in-b^2 structure
For fixed a,c,f and x=b^2,
```text
E4=W^2
```
is a conic in (x,W).
Its x-discriminant is
```text
a^4
*(c-f)^2*(c+f)^2
*(c^2-2cf-f^2)
*(c^2+2cf-f^2).
```
The conic has the rational point
```text
x=0,
W=a^2*c*f.
```
The genus-one nature of the physical fiber enters only when the extra
condition
```text
x=b^2
```
is imposed.
This gives a useful conceptual decomposition:
```text
M3 square condition
 =
rational conic
+
one additional square-coordinate condition.
```
It explains both why fixed fibers are elliptic and why a two-layer square
sieve is a natural analytic model.
## 112.8 What a successful double square sieve would need to prove
Let R(B) be the exact primitive/canonical four-variable physical region.
Define
```text
P_end(B)
 =
#{x in R(B):
 E4(x) square,
 H4(x) square}.
```
A direct endpoint proof would follow from
```text
P_end(B)=o(B^(1/3)),
```
because the certified denominator gives
```text
M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
```
Theorem112-B supplies the complete local cancellation.
The unresolved estimate is an incomplete version in each dyadic physical
box, uniformly in the cross-gcd strata and in squarefree moduli generated by
the sieve.
The needed theorem is therefore:
### Receiver 112-R1 — PhysicalDoubleSquareSieve
Prove a bound for
```text
sum_{(a,b,c,f) in physical dyadic box}
 chi_m(E4)
 chi_n(H4)
```
which combines:
1. square-root-quality complete sums from Theorem112-B;
2. completion or q-van der Corput saving in short/anisotropic boxes;
3. the exact gcd-dependent physical constraint from Theorem112-C;
4. primitive/canonical masks via Mobius inversion;
5. enough uniformity in m,n to optimize a two-stage square sieve.
The quantitative target is strong:
```text
endpoint upper bound < B^(1/3-o(1)).
```
A weaker power saving that remains above B^(1/3) would not yet prove the
density theorem, but could still be combined with one-factor or
anti-concentration inputs.
## 112.9 Strategic comparison after the theorem audit
Three analytic routes now remain:
### A. Same-M3 fixed-prime transfer
Needs distribution of exact K3/M3 rational points.
No suitable general theorem is known.
### B. One-factor common-divisor convolution
Uses stronger structural information but carries the moving h-normalization.
### C. Ambient simultaneous double square sieve
Avoids K3 equidistribution and moving h, at the cost of requiring stronger
absolute incomplete character-sum estimates.
Among these, Route C is the first route whose local complete sums and exact
ambient parameter region are both fully explicit.
Therefore it should now receive the main analytic effort.
## 112.10 Exact state after Section112
```text
COMPLETE_E4_CHARACTER_SUM_O_P_7_OVER_2=true
COMPLETE_H4_CHARACTER_SUM_O_P_7_OVER_2=true
COMPLETE_E4H4_CHARACTER_SUM_O_P_7_OVER_2=true
LOCAL_E4_H4_SQUARECLASSES_UNIFORM_QUARTERS=true
DYADIC_PHYSICAL_PRODUCT_CONSTRAINT=true
E4_CONIC_IN_B2=true
BONOLIS_BROWNING_DIRECT_APPLICATION=false
PHYSICAL_DOUBLE_SQUARE_SIEVE=false
ENDPOINT_ABSOLUTE_BOUND_BELOW_B_ONE_THIRD=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
PERFECT_CUBOID_NONEXISTENCE_CLAIM=false
```
### Next research item
```text
NEXT_ITEM=PhysicalDoubleSquareSieveBoxEstimate
```
The next calculation should derive an explicit incomplete completion bound
for one dyadic box and one squarefree modulus, first without coprimality
masks, and determine the best exponent obtainable by elementary completion.
If that exponent cannot cross the B^(1/3) threshold, the failure should be
recorded quantitatively before attempting q-van der Corput differencing.

# SECTION113 — explicit Fourier transform and the elementary double-sieve exponent ceiling
## 2026-09-27
## Status: exact prime-modulus Fourier decomposition + quantitative negative result for elementary completion
Section112 left the simultaneous square-sieve route at the incomplete four-variable character sums.
The special ratio structure of E4 and H4 makes their additive Fourier transforms explicit.
## 113.1 Prime-modulus Fourier reduction
Let F(u,v) denote any one of E(u,v), H(u,v), or E(u,v)H(u,v).
For a good odd prime p define
S_p(alpha,beta,gamma,delta;F)
 = sum_{a,b,c,f in F_p^*}
   chi_p(F(a/b,c/f))
   e_p(alpha*a+beta*b+gamma*c+delta*f).
Substitute a=u*b and c=v*f. Then
S_p
 = sum_{u,v in F_p^*}
   chi_p(F(u,v))
   B_p(alpha*u+beta)
   B_p(gamma*v+delta),
where
B_p(L)=sum_{x in F_p^*} e_p(Lx)
      =-1+p*1_{L=0}.
Thus the full four-dimensional Fourier transform reduces exactly to the two-dimensional character sum plus at most two line restrictions and one point restriction.
## 113.2 Fourier hierarchy
Expanding
B_p(L1)B_p(L2)
 =1-p*1_{L1=0}-p*1_{L2=0}+p^2*1_{L1=L2=0},
and using the Section112 Weil bounds gives:
### Theorem 113-A — ExplicitPrimeFourierBounds
Uniformly in the additive frequency:
1. Zero frequency gives S_p=O(p^(7/2)).
2. If exactly one variable pair (alpha,beta) or (gamma,delta) vanishes identically, while the other pair produces a genuine affine restriction, then S_p=O(p^(5/2)).
3. For generic nonzero frequency, where both affine restrictions are proper, S_p=O(p^2).
The O(p^2) generic bound is genuine four-dimensional square-root cancellation.
The same hierarchy holds for F=E, H and EH away from the finite bad-prime set.
## 113.3 Incomplete rectangular box bound
Let I_a,I_b,I_c,I_f be intervals of lengths A,B1,C,F and put V=A*B1*C*F.
Fourier completion modulo p, together with the standard L1 bound for an interval Fourier transform, gives:
### Theorem 113-B — PrimeIncompleteBoxCharacterBound
For any of chi_p(E4), chi_p(H4), chi_p(E4H4),
|sum_box chi_p(...)|
 << V*p^(-1/2)
    +p^(1/2)*(A*B1+C*F)*(log p)^2
    +p^2*(log p)^4.
The first term is the zero Fourier mode; the middle terms are the one-pair-degenerate modes; the last term is the generic dual contribution.
## 113.4 Squarefree composite moduli
For squarefree q supported on good primes, CRT multiplicativity gives, for every epsilon>0,
### Theorem 113-C — SquarefreeIncompleteBoxCharacterBound
|sum_box chi_q(...)|
 << q^epsilon * [
      V*q^(-1/2)
      +q^(1/2)*(A*B1+C*F)
      +q^2
    ].
The theorem applies separately to E4, H4 and to mixed characters obtained from disjoint squarefree moduli by CRT.
Primitive/coprime masks can be inserted later by Mobius inversion; they do not improve the exponent ceiling below.
## 113.5 Balanced-box test
Consider the generic balanced physical box
A~B1~C~F~R,
g~1.
Then V~R^4 and B_phys~R^4.
A standard square sieve with primes of size P creates correlation moduli q~P^2.
Theorem113-C gives the characteristic correlation scale
R^4/P + R^2*P + P^4.
The square-sieve diagonal term is also of size R^4/P.
Balancing R^4/P with P^4 gives
P~R^(4/5),
and therefore
### Theorem 113-D — ElementarySingleSquareCeiling
best elementary-completion scale ~ R^(16/5).
The middle term is then only R^(14/5).
## 113.6 Simultaneous two-square sieve ceiling
A symmetric two-square sieve for E4 square and H4 square uses two prime sets of comparable size P.
The fully off-diagonal terms have composite modulus q~P^4.
The generic incomplete Fourier term then contains q^2~P^8.
The double-diagonal main term is R^4/P^2.
Balancing
R^4/P^2 = P^8
gives
P~R^(2/5),
and endpoint scale
R^(16/5).
Partially diagonal terms do not improve this exponent.
### Negative Result 113-E — ElementaryDoubleSquareSieveCeiling
Even with the exact generic Fourier bound O(q^2), a standard two-stage square sieve plus elementary completion cannot beat
R^(16/5+epsilon)
in a balanced generic box.
Since B_phys~R^4, this is
B_phys^(4/5+epsilon).
The required absolute endpoint bound for the current denominator strategy is o(B_phys^(1/3)).
Therefore elementary completion misses the target by a very large exponent gap.
## 113.7 Interpretation
The failure is not caused by weak local complete sums.
Generic additive Fourier modes already attain the natural four-dimensional square-root scale O(q^2).
The obstruction is global: the sieve modulus needed to force integer squares must grow much faster than the side lengths of the physical parameter box.
Thus merely improving complete finite-field character sums cannot close the problem.
A successful ambient double-square method would require an additional structural mechanism such as:
1. q-van der Corput differencing lowering the effective dual dimension;
2. determinant/spacing information from gcd strata;
3. a low-dimensional exceptional decomposition with much smaller effective volume;
4. a relative argument against M3(B), rather than an absolute endpoint count.
## 113.8 Consequence for Bonolis–Browning style methods
Bonolis–Browning obtain extra saving beyond elementary completion by q-analogue van der Corput differencing and a nontrivial oscillatory-sum estimate.
For the present family, such technology would be necessary even to improve substantially on B^(4/5).
However the final B^(1/3) target is far beyond the gain produced by a single mild differencing step in analogous hyperelliptic-fibration problems.
Thus the ambient double-square route remains useful as a secondary route and as a source of quantitative upper bounds, but should not presently replace the structural one-factor/norm-core route as the sole strategy.
## 113.9 Revised priority
Structural primary:
return to the primitive norm/common-core architecture and seek a relative-to-M3 power saving, where only o(M3(B)) is required.
Analytic secondary:
develop q-van der Corput for the four-variable E4/H4 family only if it yields an explicit exponent materially below 4/5.
## 113.10 Exact state after Section113
EXPLICIT_FOURIER_REDUCTION_TO_RATIO_VARIABLES=true
GENERIC_ADDITIVE_FOURIER_BOUND_O_P2=true
ONE_PAIR_DEGENERATE_FOURIER_BOUND_O_P5_OVER2=true
ZERO_FREQUENCY_BOUND_O_P7_OVER2=true
SQUAREFREE_INCOMPLETE_BOX_BOUND=true
ELEMENTARY_SINGLE_SQUARE_CEILING_R16_OVER5=true
ELEMENTARY_DOUBLE_SQUARE_CEILING_R16_OVER5=true
ELEMENTARY_DOUBLE_SQUARE_ENDPOINT_TARGET_SUFFICIENT=false
PHYSICAL_DOUBLE_SQUARE_SIEVE=false
M3_CANONICAL_MINUS_FACTOR_THINNESS=false
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
PERFECT_CUBOID_NONEXISTENCE_CLAIM=false
### Next research item
NEXT_ITEM=RelativeNormCorePowerSavingOrQVanDerCorput
The structural branch should next test whether large/small h or K_- can be split at a B-dependent threshold so that one side is negligible by existing anti-concentration and the other gains directly from the h^2 physical root modulus.
The analytic branch should proceed only if q-van der Corput gives a materially smaller exponent than 4/5.

# SECTION114 — top-down audit and anti-loop reset
## 2026-09-27
## Status: strategic audit; no new theorem credit
## 114.1 M3 research goal
M3 research is scoped only to the exact primitive canonical Euler-brick population M3(B).
Primary goal:
G1 = prove P(B)/M3(B) -> 0, where P(B) is the subset satisfying the additional endpoint/space-square condition.
A sufficient weaker receiver remains:
G2 = #{E in M3(B): L_-(E) square} = o(M3(B)).
No broader nonexistence objective is part of the current M3 research scope.
## 114.2 Loop diagnosis
Sections89-113 produced real exact assets, but the same unresolved step repeatedly reappeared:
local or fixed-fiber information -> exact moving physical M3 population.
The coordinate sequence
Eulerian graph -> Gaussian core -> C_- -> K_- -> h -> physical root lines -> Gaussian ratio -> half-angle factors -> four-variable E4/H4
mostly changed the representation of the same transfer wall.
The two persistent walls are:
WALL-A = same-M3 moving-family transfer;
WALL-B = an absolute endpoint bound strong enough to beat the certified M3(B) lower scale B^(1/3).
Section113 quantifies WALL-B: elementary ambient double-square completion only reaches about B^(4/5), far from B^(1/3).
Therefore deriving another equivalent local coordinate system without a new global mechanism is now an anti-loop violation.
## 114.3 What the recent work genuinely added
Reusable exact assets include:
- canonical factors L_-,L_+;
- primitive norms N_-,N_+ and h=gcd(N_-,N_+);
- fixed-coordinate B^o(1) anti-concentration for several physical coordinates;
- exact four-variable model E4,H4;
- exact local finite-field character laws;
- explicit Fourier bounds;
- quantitative failure certificate for elementary double square sieve.
These are an arsenal. They are not a reason to keep descending into equivalent coordinates.
## 114.4 Two valid M3 routes
Route D — relative density inside M3:
prove G1 or G2. A valid next result must cross WALL-A, not merely add local congruences.
Route A — absolute endpoint count inside the ambient exact model:
bound P(B) strongly enough that P(B)=o(M3(B)). With only the certified denominator lower bound, an absolute estimate below B^(1/3) would suffice.
## 114.5 Better question: image size, not another local sieve
Existing anti-concentration results say that fixing many canonical coordinates gives only B^o(1) M3 points.
Therefore a density proof can come from an IMAGE-SIZE theorem:
if endpoint points occupy at most B^(1/3-epsilon) values of a canonical coordinate C(E),
and each fixed C-fiber contributes B^o(1),
then P(B)=o(M3(B)).
This may be much easier than proving full same-M3 character equidistribution.
Candidate coordinates already available:
t, X, D, N_-, N_+, h, K_-, (h,J), and the physical split (p,d).
## 114.6 Main anti-loop reset: use the FULL endpoint condition
Recent work mostly exploited only one-factor information:
N_-=h*u^2 while N_+/h remained unrestricted.
At an endpoint BOTH factors are squares, so exact algebra gives
N_-=h*u^2,
N_+=h*v^2,
with gcd(u,v)=1.
This simultaneous-square symmetry has not been exploited top-down in the recent M3 work.
It is strictly stronger than the one-factor receiver and is the first thing to test before returning to any local sieve.
## 114.7 Preferred next audit
For each canonical coordinate C(E), test only:
Does the full endpoint condition force
# {C(E): endpoint E, R<=B} <= B^(1/3-epsilon) ?
If yes, combine with the existing fixed-fiber B^o(1) theorem and close G1.
If no exact image-size mechanism appears, stop that coordinate immediately.
Priority:
1. common core h and squarefree kernel K_-;
2. Gaussian quotient J=(X^2+D^2)/h^2;
3. norm ratio N_-/N_+;
4. original K3 fibration coordinate t.
## 114.8 What to stop
Do not spend primary effort on:
- another equivalent Legendre-symbol graph;
- another coordinate transform of L_-;
- another fixed-fiber genus/rank computation;
- elementary square-sieve completion;
- raw ambient lattice counts for moving h;
- third-triple parametrization without an image-size or dimension gain.
All of these have already looped back to WALL-A or missed the exponent target.
## 114.9 Revised current research item
NEXT_ITEM=EndpointCanonicalImageSizeAudit
First test the full endpoint norm system
N_-=h*u^2,
N_+=h*v^2,
gcd(u,v)=1,
together with
T=N_-N_+=h^2*u^2*v^2.
This is the strongest unused top-down information produced by the recent M3 algebra.
---
# SECTION115 — completed strategic audit: primary route reset
## 2026-09-27
## Status: audit complete; active-route decision
## 115.1 Audit question
Given the exact M3 goal P(B)/M3(B)->0, and the results through Section114, determine which current viewpoint has a credible closure path and which routes are looping.
## 115.2 Existing canonical image-size audit
The Section114 proposal was tested on the currently available canonical coordinates.
### X and D
Fixed X and fixed D fibers are B^o(1), but the endpoint condition does not force X or D into fewer than B^(1/3-epsilon) possible values by any known exact mechanism.
Endpoint only adds X^2+D^2=R^2, i.e. a fourth Pythagorean relation.
The ambient number of possible Pythagorean (X,D) pairs below height B is far too large for the certified M3(B)>>B^(1/3) denominator.
### N_- and N_+
Fixed N_- and fixed N_+ fibers are B^o(1).
At endpoint
N_-=h*u^2,
N_+=h*v^2,
gcd(u,v)=1,
and h*u*v<=B.
This does not by itself restrict either norm to B^(1/3-epsilon) possible values.
### h and K_-
The common core is highly structured, but endpoint does not force h into a sufficiently small image.
The relation h*u*v<=B still allows polynomially many h-values.
Likewise the squarefree-kernel information alone does not produce the required image-size bound.
### (h,J)
At endpoint
J=(X^2+D^2)/h^2=(u*v)^2
is a square.
Fixed (h,J) has B^o(1) multiplicity, but the number of admissible pairs
(h,w), J=w^2, h*w<=B
is of order at least the ambient divisor-sum scale B^(1+o(1)), not B^(1/3-epsilon).
Therefore fixed-(h,J) anti-concentration does not close the M3 ratio.
### norm ratio N_-/N_+
At endpoint
N_-/N_+=(u/v)^2.
This square-ratio condition still admits polynomially many coprime ratios under h*u*v<=B.
No current image-size compression follows.
### original fibration coordinate t
Fixed t fibers are uniformly B^o(1), which is strong.
But no full-endpoint identity currently confines endpoint points to only B^(1/3-epsilon) t-values.
Using t again therefore returns directly to the already-known moving-base transfer wall.
### Audit result 115-A
None of the currently defined canonical coordinates gives an immediate image-size closure from the full endpoint equations alone.
Thus EndpointCanonicalImageSizeAudit is complete with a negative result for the existing coordinate list.
## 115.3 One-factor route audit
The one-factor receiver
L_- square
remains a correct sufficient condition route.
However the recent work shows that using it as the primary global strategy introduces extra normalization by the moving common core h.
The raw local factor differs by the common twist 2h, and every attempted global average returns to the same-M3 moving-family transfer problem.
Therefore:
### Audit result 115-B
The one-factor route remains an auxiliary structural tool, but it should no longer be the default primary route for global M3 closure.
Its strongest retained uses are:
- exact Gaussian reconstruction;
- small-coordinate anti-concentration;
- common-core/root-line structure;
- possible decomposition of exceptional subfamilies.
It should not receive further local-symbol refinements unless they directly yield a new global counting theorem.
## 115.4 Ambient double-square route audit
The four-variable E4/H4 model is exact and analytically clean.
Complete character sums and Fourier transforms are strong.
But Section113 proves that elementary completion plus a standard double square sieve reaches only about
B^(4/5+epsilon),
while an absolute numerator estimate below B^(1/3) is needed from the currently certified denominator alone.
### Audit result 115-C
The ambient double-square route is not a credible primary closure route without a genuinely stronger analytic mechanism such as major q-van der Corput or determinant-method input.
It remains a secondary quantitative route only.
## 115.5 The top-down viewpoint that survives the audit
The most faithful formulation of the M3 goal is geometric:
K_c = the Euler-brick/M3 K3 surface,
S -> K_c = the degree-2 endpoint cover obtained by adjoining the space-diagonal square root.
The numerator P(B) is the physical rational-point population on S.
The denominator M3(B) is the corresponding population on K_c.
This uses the full endpoint condition from the start and avoids weakening to one factor.
Section37 already gives a genuine theorem in exactly this language:
- on every integral degree-6 curve C in K_c, the restricted endpoint cover is non-split;
- a fixed such curve contributes at most B^(1/6+o(1)) endpoint points;
- hence any fixed finite collection of critical degree-6 curves is negligible against M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
This is the only current route that has already converted the full endpoint cover geometry into a numerator exponent strictly smaller than the denominator exponent on a nontrivial class of subvarieties.
### Audit result 115-D
The primary viewpoint should move upward to terminal-cover geometry and accumulating-curve decomposition.
## 115.6 New primary closure architecture
Decompose the physical M3 population on K_c into:
A. low-degree / accumulating curves;
B. the Zariski-dense residual population.
For A:
use the endpoint double cover S->K_c and low-degree curve classification.
Section37 already closes every fixed degree-6 curve at exponent 1/6.
The next task is to determine whether all potentially B^(1/3)-scale accumulating curves can be classified into a controlled family, and whether the number of such curves of relevant height is small enough to preserve negligibility.
For B:
seek a generic rational-point upper bound or congruence theorem only on the residual population, after the accumulating curves are removed.
This is potentially easier than forcing one same-M3 equidistribution theorem to handle both exceptional curves and generic K3 points.
## 115.7 What is now frozen / deprioritized
Primary work is stopped on:
- new L_- coordinate transforms;
- new Eulerian/Legendre graphs;
- fixed h root-line counting without a new relative theorem;
- fixed-fiber genus computations already covered by B^o(1) bounds;
- elementary double square sieve;
- canonical image-size tests on h,J,N_-/N_+,X,D,t unless a new independent structural theorem appears.
These results remain available as auxiliary tools.
## 115.8 Current single research question
NEXT_ITEM=TerminalCoverAccumulatingCurveDecomposition
Concrete questions:
1. What low-degree curves on K_c can carry polynomially many M3 points under the physical height?
2. Which of those curves are already classified by Testa-Stoll or by the existing K_c geometry?
3. For each such curve family, what is the degree/genus of its pullback to S under the endpoint double cover?
4. Can the total endpoint contribution of all accumulating curves be bounded by o(B^(1/3))?
5. After removing them, which general rational-point counting theorem gives the strongest bound on the residual endpoint surface population?
This is the current top-down route selected by the audit.
## 115.9 Final audit state
STRATEGIC_AUDIT_COMPLETE=true
RECENT_COORDINATE_LOOP_CONFIRMED=true
EXISTING_CANONICAL_IMAGE_SIZE_ROUTE_CLOSES=false
ONE_FACTOR_PRIMARY_ROUTE=false
AMBIENT_DOUBLE_SQUARE_PRIMARY_ROUTE=false
TERMINAL_COVER_GEOMETRY_PRIMARY_ROUTE=true
NEXT_ITEM=TerminalCoverAccumulatingCurveDecomposition
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false


# END PRE-COMPACTION MAIN SNAPSHOT

# POST-COMPACTION PRE-ROUTE-BREADTH MAIN SNAPSHOT


# K16-C3-M3-LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL — MAIN
## Current research authority / active route
> このファイルだけ読めば通常の M3 研究を再開できる状態を保つ。
> 旧主ルート・詳細証明・negative result・監査前の MAIN 全文は K16-C3-M3-LOCAL-TO-GLOBAL-ARCHIVE に保存済み。

## 0. M3研究の現在のゴール
対象は primitive canonical Euler brick の exact physical population M3(B)。
必須条件:
- primitive
- canonical ordering
- Euclidean height R<=B
- one object = one count
- 3 face diagonals integral
endpoint subset を P(B) とする。
現在の唯一の主ゴール:
P(B)/M3(B) -> 0.
完全直方体の非存在そのものは、現在の M3 研究のゴールに含めない。
既知の certified denominator scale:
M3(B) >> B^(1/3).
したがって absolute endpoint count で閉じるなら
P(B)=o(B^(1/3))
で十分。ただし relative M3 argument なら absolute B^(1/3) barrier を直接越える必要はない。
## 0.5 Research credit / routing firewall
- 研究 credit は厳格に分離する。有限計算 ≠ global theorem、numerical / receiver / theorem / effectivity / endpoint credit は別物。
- population、measure、field、quotient、model、height、multiplicity 等が変わるなら exact adapter 必須。
- 同じ restriction/saving を二重に credit しない。独立性を勝手に仮定しない。
- formal class / orbit / cohomology があるだけでは、必要な geometric object の存在証明にならない。
- blocked route や有限範囲で hit=0 は impossibility ではない。
- perfect-cuboid の existence/nonexistence は explicit audited full-endpoint certificate がない限り絶対に claim しない。
- Stage-local controller はこの firewall を強化してよいが、弱めてはいけない。
- 既存武器を探すときだけ repository-asset-discovery、route を広げる・park・reopen・exhausted 判定をするときだけ cycle-exploration-safety-protocol を開く。
---
## 1. Exact physical endpoint structure
Canonical labeling:
- X = unique odd edge
- Y,Z = even edges
- P^2=X^2+Y^2
- Q^2=X^2+Z^2
- D^2=Y^2+Z^2
- R^2=X^2+Y^2+Z^2
M3 conditionは D integer。
endpoint conditionはさらに R integer。
Exact terminal factorization:
(PQ-YZ)(PQ+YZ)=X^2 R^2.
G=gcd(PQ,YZ),
L_-=(PQ-YZ)/G,
L_+=(PQ+YZ)/G.
L_-,L_+ は positive odd coprime。
Endpoint iff
L_- and L_+ are both integer squares.
one-factor condition L_-=square は endpoint の canonical necessary conditionだが、監査後は primary global route ではない。
---
## 2. Primitive norm architecture — auxiliary load-bearing arsenal
Exact primitive Gaussian/norm decomposition:
T:=R^2=N_- N_+,
h:=gcd(N_-,N_+).
Endpoint では exact に
N_-=h*u^2,
N_+=h*v^2,
gcd(u,v)=1,
したがって
R=h*u*v.
one-factor では N_-/h square のみを要求する。
Current retained physical consequences:
- gcd(h,X)=1
- h^2 | X^2+D^2
- D/X is a Gaussian root of -1 mod h^2
- fixed (X,D) physical multiplicity is B^o(1)
これらは auxiliary structure として保持するが、moving-h root-line counting 単独では relative M3 closure を与えないことは監査済み。
---
## 3. Accepted anti-concentration arsenal
以下は再証明しない。
### 3.1 Fixed t fiber
K_c の rational fibration coordinate t について、各 fixed occupied t-fiber は
N_t(B)<=exp(O(log B/log log B))=B^o(1)
uniformly。
したがって occupied t-values は少なくとも
B^(1/3-o(1))
個必要。
B^(1/3-epsilon) 個以下の t-fibers の集合は M3-negligible。
### 3.2 Other fixed-coordinate fibers
既存 exact results により fixed:
- X
- D
- N_-
- N_+
- (h,J), J=(X^2+D^2)/h^2
はいずれも relevant M3 fiber multiplicity B^o(1)。
small-X, small-D, small-N_-, small-N_+, small-C_- については B^(1/3-epsilon) threshold 以下の領域が M3-negligible になる exact anti-concentration results を保持。
これらは今後 accumulating-curve decomposition の補助武器として使用する。
---
## 4. Terminal cover geometry — current primary viewpoint
K_c = Euler-brick / M3 K3 surface。
S -> K_c = space-diagonal square rootを adjoining する endpoint degree-2 cover。
P(B) は S 側の exact physical rational-point population、
M3(B) は K_c 側の exact physical rational-point populationとして考える。
この formulation は full endpoint condition を最初から使用し、one-factor normalization h を primary global variable にしない。
### Existing load-bearing terminal-cover result
既存 Section37 result:
任意の integral degree-6 curve C subset K_c について、restricted endpoint cover
pi^{-1}(C) -> C
は generically split しない。
Testa–Stoll の full endpoint surface S に integral degree-6 curves が存在しない結果から従う。
そのため fixed degree-6 M3 curve C の endpoint lifts は
O(B^(1/6+o(1)))
以下。
certified denominator M3(B)>>B^(1/3) と比較して、
任意の fixed finite collection of degree-6 M3 curves
は endpoint numerator 上で negligible。
これは現在の arsenal の中で、full endpoint geometry から numerator exponent 1/6 < denominator exponent 1/3 を実際に得ている重要な result。
---
## 5. Strategic audit — completed
STRATEGIC_AUDIT_COMPLETE=true.
監査結論:
### 5.1 Recent coordinate loop
Sections89–113 では有用な exact algebra が多数得られたが、
local / fixed-fiber information
  -> exact moving physical M3 population
という同じ transfer wall を別座標で繰り返し発見する傾向があった。
新しい equivalent coordinate system を作るだけの研究は停止。
### 5.2 Existing canonical image-size audit
endpoint full conditionを使い、既存 coordinates
X, D, N_-, N_+, h, K_-, (h,J), N_-/N_+, t
を査定したが、現時点の exact identities だけでは endpoint image を B^(1/3-epsilon) values 以下へ押し込む mechanism は得られなかった。
EXISTING_CANONICAL_IMAGE_SIZE_ROUTE_CLOSES=false.
### 5.3 One-factor route
L_-=square は正しい sufficient receiver。
ただし raw local factor と normalized L_- の間に moving common twist 2h が入り、global character averaging は same-M3 moving-family transfer に戻る。
ONE_FACTOR_PRIMARY_ROUTE=false.
Retain as auxiliary:
- Gaussian reconstruction
- common-core/root-line structure
- exceptional subfamily decomposition
- fixed-coordinate anti-concentration
### 5.4 Ambient double-square route
Exact four-variable model E4/H4、complete character sums、Fourier transforms は成立。
しかし elementary double square sieve + completion の balanced-box ceiling は約
B^(4/5+epsilon),
必要な absolute B^(1/3) scale から遠い。
AMBIENT_DOUBLE_SQUARE_PRIMARY_ROUTE=false.
q-van der Corput / determinant-method 級の新しい強い analytic input が出ない限り secondary route。

## 6. Current primary route
TERMINAL_COVER_GEOMETRY_PRIMARY_ROUTE=true.
NEXT_ITEM=TerminalCoverAccumulatingCurveDecomposition.
M3 population on K_c を
A. low-degree / accumulating curves
B. Zariski-dense residual population
に分ける。
### A. Accumulating curves
最初に解くべき問題:
1. physical height で polynomially many M3 points を持ち得る low-degree curves は何か。
2. Testa–Stoll / 既存 K_c geometry でどこまで分類済みか。
3. 各 curve family の endpoint pullback under S->K_c の degree/genus は何か。
4. fixed curve ではなく、該当する curve family 全体を足しても endpoint contribution が o(B^(1/3)) になるか。
5. exceptional low-degree curves が B^(1/3) scale の M3 denominator を担い得るなら、その family count を exact physical height とともに評価できるか。
### B. Residual population
accumulating curves を切り離した後だけ、generic residual に対して:
- determinant method
- uniform congruence counting
- geometric sieve
- rational-point height theorem
のどれが最も強いかを査定する。
最初から M3 全体へ一発 equidistribution theorem を要求しない。

- 新しい L_- equivalent coordinate transform
- 新しい Eulerian / Legendre-symbol graph

新しい route を開始する前に必ず:
「この結果が TerminalCoverAccumulatingCurveDecomposition のどの closure step を前進させるか」
を明示する。
答えられない場合は loop 候補として停止。

## 4. Top-down closure map — route inventory after ARCHIVE replay
