## 14. R2 reentry audit — delta descent is a fixed-base genus-one twist family
Purpose: determine whether the late Section34–35 divisor/delta work created a genuinely new global route, or only refines the old moving-fibration wall.
### 14.1 Exact fixed-delta quartic
Recall the Section34 base data
P=L^2-M^2,
Q=2LM,
W=L^2+M^2,
A=P-Q,
B=P+Q,
together with the transverse quotient coordinate
t2=C/D.
Section35 fixes a signed squarefree delta through
C^2-D^2=delta*r^2
and, after primitive cross-gcd removal,
c^2*a^2-d^2*b^2=delta*s^2.
Because
c*a/(d*b)=C*A/(D*B),
put
x=C/D,
k=A/B.
Then the two delta conditions are
x^2-1=delta*y^2,
k^2*x^2-1=delta*z^2.
Parameterize the first conic by
x=(1+delta*t^2)/(1-delta*t^2).
Substitution into the second gives the even quartic
delta*zeta^2
=
(k^2-1)
+2*delta*(k^2+1)*t^2
+delta^2*(k^2-1)*t^4.
Using
k=(P-Q)/(P+Q)=A/B,
and multiplying by the harmless rational square B^2/4, this becomes
### Theorem 14-A — FixedBaseDeltaQuartic
y^2
=
-delta*P*Q*t^4
+W^2*t^2
-(P*Q)/delta.
This is smooth for a genuine primitive Pythagorean base P,Q>0 with P!=Q.
### 14.2 Delta does not create new elliptic moduli
For an even quartic
y^2=a*t^4+b*t^2+c,
the binary-quartic invariants are functions of
ac
and
b.
In Theorem14-A,
a=-delta*P*Q,
b=W^2,
c=-(P*Q)/delta,
so
ac=(P*Q)^2,
b=W^2,
both independent of delta.
Therefore the binary-quartic invariants I,J, its geometric discriminant up to the standard scalar normalization, and the geometric Jacobian j-invariant are independent of delta.
### Theorem 14-B — DeltaTwistNoNewModuli
For a fixed primitive Pythagorean base (P,Q,W), all signed-squarefree delta slices from Section35 lie in one geometric genus-one/Jacobian isomorphism class.
Changing delta changes the arithmetic twist / torsor / descent class, not the underlying elliptic moduli.
This is an exact theorem about the Section35 descent family. It does not assert that every delta slice has a rational point.
### 14.3 Relation to the old two-fibration architecture
Section31 proves that
t1=a2/a3=P/Q
and
t2=C/D
are transverse rational fibrations, with t2 a degree-two quotient coordinate on a fixed t1-fiber.
Also
k=A/B=(t1-1)/(t1+1).
Hence Theorem14-A is precisely a descent decomposition performed on the transverse degree-two quotient of a fixed t1-fiber.
The late Section35 squareclass decomposition therefore does not create a new independent global parameter direction.
It refines each moving t1-fiber into only
(PQ)^o(1)
arithmetic twist/descent classes.
### Audit Result 14-C — R2R3StructuralMerge
The late R2 divisor/delta route and the R3 moving-fibration route share the same unresolved global quantifier:
uniform control while the rational Pythagorean base t1=P/Q moves through the exact physical M3 population.
R2 contributes a valuable arithmetic decomposition of each moving fiber.
R3 contributes the exact elliptic-fibration height and fixed-fiber anti-concentration theorem.
They should no longer be counted as two logically independent closure mechanisms.
They are two implementations of one broader method family:
METHOD_BC = MOVING_FIBRATION_WITH_DESCENT.
Subroutes:
BC1 = moving-base distribution / energy on t1;
BC2 = fixed-base squareclass/twist decomposition by delta, followed by a uniform sum over moving t1.
### 14.4 What the merge buys and what it does not
It buys:
- exact physical one-object coordinates;
- only subpolynomially many delta classes per base;
- a fixed-base genus-one twist family with no new delta-dependent moduli;
- uniform fixed-fiber point bound B^o(1) from Section39;
- a precise statement that any remaining obstruction must be spread over polynomially many moving base values.
It does NOT buy:
- equidistribution of occupied t1 values modulo q;
- a uniform average over the twist classes as t1 moves;
- a summable fixed-q error over the full physical population;
- endpoint density zero.
### 14.5 Revised theorem gate
The narrowest remaining METHOD_BC gate is no longer best described as fixed-q count on every delta slice separately.
Because delta does not add a new moduli dimension, the natural gate is:
### Receiver BC-MIN — MovingBaseTwistUniformity
For each fixed good modulus q, prove a physical-height weighted congruence / character estimate on the moving t1-family, uniform over the rationally soluble delta/twist classes, with total error
o(M3(B))
or strong enough to support a slowly growing prime sieve.
Equivalent acceptable outputs include:
- an M3-weighted second-moment theorem across t1;
- a uniform average over the relevant 2-covering/twist classes;
- a moving-base large sieve after exact physical-height and primitive adapters.
### 14.6 Anti-loop disposition
Further algebraic refinement of the fixed-delta quartic is not primary work unless it creates a genuinely uniform moving-base estimate.
Do not:
- derive another equivalent quartic model;
- classify individual delta fibers one by one;
- promote fixed-base rank or Selmer information to a moving-family count;
- treat the (PQ)^o(1) delta count itself as density saving.
R2_REENTRY_CHECK_COMPLETE=true
DELTA_SLICE_GENUS1_EXPLICIT=true
DELTA_GEOMETRIC_MODULI_INDEPENDENT=true
R2_R3_STRUCTURAL_MERGE=true
METHOD_BC_STATUS=THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED
Under the route-rotation rule, the next materially distinct route is METHOD_D terminal-cover/accumulating-subvariety geometry.

## 14. R2 reentry audit — old semantic wall reduced to a conditional sparse-lattice theorem
### 14.1 What changed since the old Master-Hit wall
The old R2 failure had two layers:
1. semantic/measure mismatch: moving gcd normalization, physical R<=B, one-object multiplicity and canonicalization were not yet controlled cleanly;
2. analytic conditional-counting wall: after imposing the M3 square condition, no same-measure character/sieve theorem was available for the space-square predicate.
Sections32,35,110,111 materially repair layer 1.
Section32 gives an exact one-object/one-count odd-edge divisor model with exact primitivity and exact physical height.
Section35 reduces squareclass complexity to only subpolynomially many signed delta allocations, but the coupled slice retains a genus-1 core.
Section110 replaces sparse divisors by four primitive face variables (a,b,c,f) with
E4(a,b,c,f)=square  iff  M3,
H4(a,b,c,f)=square  iff  endpoint on M3,
and exact height
H4 <= 4*gcd(ab,cf)^2*B^2.
Section111 resolves all cross gcds and shows that the residual variables are no longer divisors of one fixed integer. This is the first exact coordinate system in which ordinary lattice/character-sum completion is structurally plausible without changing the physical measure.
Therefore the original objection
"Master-Hit parameter measure != exact physical M3 measure"
is no longer the correct first blocker.
### 14.2 What did not change
The M3 condition itself is still sparse:
E4(a,b,c,f)=integer square.
Theorems112-A/B prove excellent complete finite-field cancellation for E4, H4 and E4*H4 in the ambient four-variable family, but this does not imply cancellation after restricting to the global integer-square locus E4=square.
Theorems113-B/C give incomplete ambient box bounds, but the elementary simultaneous square-sieve ceiling is only B^(4/5+epsilon) in physical height. That route cannot close the current B^(1/3) denominator strategy.
Hence the remaining R2 wall is no longer a coordinate or multiplicity problem. It is a conditional sparse-lattice theorem.
### 14.3 Exact R2 theorem ladder
A single fixed-prime theorem is useful but does not by itself prove P/M3->0.
#### R2-L1 — FixedPrimeConditionalCharacterTransfer
For each fixed good odd prime ell, prove
sum_{E in M3(B)} chi_ell(H4(E)) = o(M3(B)),
with exact primitive/canonical/R<=B semantics.
This would establish genuine same-M3 local transfer.
#### R2-L2 — UniformFiniteSetTransfer
Extend R2-L1 uniformly to squarefree moduli or finite good-prime sets with an explicit modulus-dependent error.
#### R2-L3 — GrowingLocalSquareUpperSieve
Choose a good-prime set S(B) with #S(B)->infinity and prove
#{E in M3(B): H4(E) is a local square at every p in S(B)}
 = o(M3(B)).
Every endpoint lies in this set, so R2-L3 implies P/M3->0.
Thus R2 closure still requires growing-prime uniformity, but the immediate first missing lemma is now R2-L1 rather than a vague physical-height adapter.
### 14.4 Cross-gcd-strata branch is genuinely unfinished, not previously disproved
Section111 proposed
GlobalProductCharacterViaCrossGcdStrata
as the next item.
The idea is to fix dyadic ranges for the four pairwise-coprime cross gcds r,s,t,u and work in residual variables a0,b0,c0,f0. In these variables:
- exact height is retained;
- the terminal product character has no moving-h twist;
- the residual variables are ordinary lattice variables rather than divisors of a fixed X;
- no automatic dimension drop occurs from simply Euclid-parametrizing the third face.
The later Sections112-113 switched instead to an ambient double-square sieve. They did not prove or disprove the conditional cross-gcd-strata transfer R2-L1.
Therefore this branch has not been exhausted.
### 14.5 Reentry decision
R2_REENTRY_JUSTIFIED=true.
But the permitted restart point is narrow:
NEXT_R2_ITEM=CrossGcdStrataFixedPrimeConditionalTransfer.
The bounded research question is:
After fixing one good odd prime ell and dyadic cross-gcd strata, can the exact E4=square population be decomposed into enough regular residual lattice pieces to prove
sum chi_ell(H4)=o(count)
uniformly over the strata, with summable errors?
Do not reopen:
- raw Master-Hit box equidistribution;
- moving-gcd heuristics;
- another equivalent one-factor coordinate;
- ambient complete character sums already proved in Section112.
If the fixed-prime conditional transfer cannot be obtained from the cross-gcd/delta structure without invoking a new theorem, freeze R2 at R2-L1 and rotate to R3.
R2_SEMANTIC_ADAPTER_WALL_REDUCED=true
R2_CONDITIONAL_SPARSE_LATTICE_WALL=true

R2_GROWING_PRIME_TRANSFER_PROVED=false

## 15. Method D reentry audit — low-degree spectrum and the Saunderson denominator-scale curve
### 15.1 Audited Stage28 low-degree assets
Stage28 checkpoint60-r3 / checkpoint70 fresh audit gives theorem-level reusable facts under the common physical quasi-polarization normalization:
Stage19 space-completion K3:
- physical degree <4 rational curves impossible;
- physical degree 4 stratum empty;
- every odd physical degree absent;
- hence physical degree 5 absent;
- physical degree 6 presence/absence remains open.
Stage20 third-face / M3 K3:
- the generalized Saunderson image is a rational curve C_S;
- its physical M-degree is exactly 6;
- on the audited positive physical cone it contributes
Theta(B^(1/3))
M3 points.
The common physical polarization adapter has zero height-power loss.
These facts are already fresh-audit PASS in Stage28 and must not be downgraded to heuristic evidence.
### 15.2 Combine with the terminal-cover degree-six theorem
ARCHIVE Section37 proves:
for every fixed integral degree-6 curve C subset K_c, the endpoint double cover
pi^{-1}(C)->C
is generically non-split, because a split component would give an integral degree-6 curve on the full endpoint surface S, contradicting the Testa-Stoll low-degree theorem used there.
Consequently every fixed degree-6 M3 curve contributes at most
O(B^(1/6+o(1)))
endpoint points.
Apply this to the audited Saunderson curve C_S.
### Theorem 15-A — SaundersonFamilyEndpointPowerSaving
Let
M3_S(B)=# {physical M3 points on the fixed Saunderson degree-6 curve C_S with R<=B},
P_S(B)=# {endpoint points among them}.
Then
M3_S(B)=Theta(B^(1/3)),
while
P_S(B)=O(B^(1/6+o(1))).
Hence
P_S(B)/M3_S(B)
 = O(B^(-1/6+o(1)))
 ->0.
This is a genuine relative endpoint-thinning theorem on a denominator-scale explicit M3 subpopulation.
It does not imply the global P/M3 limit, because M3 mass outside C_S is uncontrolled from below and endpoint mass outside C_S is uncontrolled from above.
### 15.3 Meaning for Method D
The terminal-cover mechanism demonstrably works on the known construction that supplies the certified global denominator exponent 1/3.
Therefore Method D is not merely aesthetic geometry: it kills the endpoint condition by a fixed power on the strongest explicit denominator-scale family currently known.
However Stage28 checkpoint70 also proves the precise limitation:
PhysicalLowDegreeRootSpectrumM6
is only an optional finite refinement.
Even a complete degree-6 spectrum does not control the moving/collective complement.
The genuine global Method D gate remains:
### Receiver D-MIN — AccumulatingPlusResidualEndpointCount
Decompose K_c(Q) under physical height into
A = accumulating low-degree contribution,
G = moving/Zariski-dense residual contribution.
Prove
P_A(B)+P_G(B)=o(B^(1/3)).
Current status:
- one critical denominator-scale curve C_S is closed with endpoint ratio B^(-1/6+o(1));
- every fixed finite set of degree-6 curves is harmless;
- a complete summable classification/count of all relevant accumulating families is not available;
- no residual endpoint count below B^(1/3) is available.
### 15.4 Optional finite receiver disposition
Stage28's remaining finite receiver
PhysicalLowDegreeRootSpectrumM6
can sharpen the causal low-degree spectrum but cannot by itself close Method D.
Under the anti-loop rule it is not the next primary task unless:
- a global residual theorem becomes available whose hypotheses require complete M6 removal; or
- the M6 classification itself produces a new global decomposition theorem.

## 16. R3 moving-fibration reentry audit
### 16.1 What is already strong
For the exact M3 fibration coordinate t=a2/a3:
- the rational/genus-one/elliptic fiber model is explicit;
- the physical height comparison is explicit;
- every fixed occupied t-fiber contributes only B^o(1) M3 points;
- consequently the occupied base image satisfies
#T_M3(B) >= B^(1/3-o(1)).
Thus no bounded, polylogarithmic or B^o(1) collection of exceptional fibers can carry a positive proportion of M3.
### 16.2 Exact sufficient receiver
Let
T_P(B)={t : the M3 fiber t contains at least one endpoint of height <=B}.
A sufficient theorem is
#T_P(B) <= B^(1/3-delta)
for some fixed delta>0.
Then fixed-fiber B^o(1) gives
P(B)<=B^(1/3-delta+o(1))=o(M3(B)).
A more flexible sufficient statement is any weighted moving-base theorem implying
sum_t P_t(B)=o(sum_t M3_t(B)).
### 16.3 Archive/Stage35 compatibility audit
The later Stage35 direct endpoint fibration does not automatically improve this receiver.
Its selected endpoint parameter uses endpoint-only information involving the space diagonal, so it is not an exact fibration of the whole M3 denominator population.
Therefore Stage35 fixed-fiber or moving-fiber results cannot be promoted to the present T_M3/T_P comparison without an exact denominator-side adapter.
Likewise SR-STR-223 concerns a different moving compatible-small-point/fiber-product receiver. It is structurally adjacent but does not currently imply an endpoint-active t-image bound for exact physical M3.
### 16.4 First genuinely missing theorem
R3_MOVING_BASE_UNIFORMITY:
prove one of:
1. endpoint-active base image power saving;
2. M3-weighted exceptional-base mass o(M3);
3. a two-fibration incidence/energy estimate implying either of the above;
4. a specialization theorem with quantitative height uniformity strong enough to sum over the B^(1/3-o(1)) moving base values.
Fixed-fiber Mordell-Weil/Chabauty calculations do not satisfy this quantifier.
### 16.5 Anti-loop disposition
R3 has reached a clean theorem gate.

## 17. METHOD C — four-linear class identification: branched cover, not standard 2-isogeny Kummer
Purpose: resolve the current item FourLinearKummerClassIdentification without re-crediting Stage36 structural weapons.
Recall
E_t: y^2=x(x+4)(x+4t^2),
with genuine M3 base
r^2=1+t^2.
For a half Q=(x,y) with 2Q=P_t, the endpoint condition is
G_t(x)=q_-(x)q_+(x) in Q^{*2},
where
q_-(x)=x^2+8(t^2-t+1)x+16t^2,
q_+(x)=x^2+8(t^2+t+1)x+16t^2.
### 17.1 Exact splitting on the genuine base
The discriminants are
Disc_x(q_-)=64(t-1)^2(1+t^2),
Disc_x(q_+)=64(t+1)^2(1+t^2).
Since r^2=1+t^2, both are rational squares on the genuine M3 base.
Using r^2-t^2=1 one obtains exactly
q_-(x)
 = [x+2(r-t+1)^2]
   [x+2(r+t-1)^2],
q_+(x)
 = [x+2(r-t-1)^2]
   [x+2(r+t+1)^2].
Hence
### Theorem 17-A — GenuineBaseFourLinearSplit
On every nondegenerate genuine base value t with r^2=1+t^2,
G_t(x)
 = product over epsilon,delta in {+1,-1} of
   [x+2(r+epsilon*t+delta)^2]
with the four sign choices corresponding to the displayed factors.
This is equivalent to the earlier s-parameter factorization but exposes the intrinsic Pythagorean-base meaning.
### 17.2 Not a standard 2-isogeny / E_t[2] Kummer class
The standard full-2 Kummer functions on E_t are generated by
x,
x+4,
x+4t^2.
Their divisors are even: each is twice a rational 2-torsion divisor minus twice the origin divisor. Therefore every squareclass generated by the standard E_t[2] Kummer functions has even valuation at every non-2-torsion geometric point.
For generic nondegenerate t, each of the four roots
-2(r-t+1)^2,
-2(r+t-1)^2,
-2(r-t-1)^2,
-2(r+t+1)^2
is distinct and avoids 0,-4,-4t^2.
Above each root there are generically two points of E_t, and G_t has valuation 1 at each such point.
Therefore the divisor of G_t contains eight generic odd-valuation branch points.
### Theorem 17-B — FourLinearClassIsBranchedNonKummer
Generically,
[G_t] in Q(E_t)^*/Q(E_t)^{*2}
is not contained in the subgroup generated by the standard E_t[2] Kummer functions x, x+4, x+4t^2.
Equivalently, the quadratic extension
Q(E_t)(sqrt(G_t))/Q(E_t)
is branched at eight generic points and is not the unramified 2-isogeny/Kummer cover supplied by S36-PW05/PW06.
Thus:
S36_PW05_DIRECT_RECEIVER_IDENTIFICATION=false
S36_PW06_DIRECT_RECEIVER_IDENTIFICATION=false.
PW05/PW06 remain useful for the denominator point P_t and its halving baseline, but they do not identify the terminal squareclass itself.
### 17.3 Genus check
A degree-two cover of a genus-one curve branched at eight simple points has
2g(C)-2 = 2*(2*1-2)+8 = 8,
so
g(C)=5.
Thus the halving receiver returns exactly to a genuine moving genus-5 cover, consistent with the earlier endpoint-fiber geometry rather than collapsing to an elliptic 2-isogeny problem.
### 17.4 Special branch geometry
Parameterize the genuine base by
t=2s/(1-s^2),
r=(1+s^2)/(1-s^2).
The four x-branch values become
-8/(1+s)^2,
-8s^2/(1-s)^2,
-8s^2/(1+s)^2,
-8/(1-s)^2.
For the ordering
a=-8/(1+s)^2,
b=-8s^2/(1-s)^2,
c=-8s^2/(1+s)^2,
d=-8/(1-s)^2,
the cross-ratio is
((a-c)(b-d))/((a-d)(b-c))
 = (1-s^2)^4/(16s^4)
 = 1/t^4.
Hence the branch family is not isotrivial, but its four x-values have a square-valued cross-ratio.
This is genuine structure, but no counting credit is assigned from it alone.
### 17.5 Anti-loop consequence
The next METHOD C step is NOT another 2-isogeny normalization or fixed-fiber rank calculation.
The remaining possible leverage is a moving-family theorem for this special branched genus-5 family, exploiting one of:
- square-valued branch cross-ratio;
- the V4/Jacobian quotient decomposition;
- a uniform large-sieve / Selmer / rational-point frequency theorem over the Pythagorean base.
No such theorem is currently retained in Arsenal.

## 18. METHOD C moving-family theorem search — near-matches do not close the physical frequency problem
### 18.1 Literature screen
The exact Section17 receiver is a non-isotrivial one-parameter genus-5 family, presented as a degree-two cover of E_t branched at eight generic points, with V4/Jacobian quotient structure available.
A targeted literature search found the following near-match theorem species.
1. Dimitrov--Gao--Habegger, Uniform Bound for the Number of Rational Points on a Pencil of Curves (IMRN 2021):
for a fixed one-parameter family of genus >=2 curves, the number of rational points on a fiber is bounded in terms of the family and the Mordell--Weil rank of that fiber's Jacobian.
2. Silverman, A Uniform Bound for Rational Points on Twists of a Given Curve (1993):
uniform bounds for twists in terms of Jacobian rank; primarily useful for geometrically constant/twist families.
3. modern arithmetic-statistics results for 2-Selmer groups of structured curve families and hyperelliptic Jacobians: potentially relevant only after an exact representation/family adapter.
### 18.2 Why the strongest near-match is insufficient
The M3 goal is not merely a uniform per-fiber point bound.
Even if one had
#C_t(Q) <= F(rank Jac(C_t))
uniformly, the denominator occupies at least B^(1/3-o(1)) moving t-values.
A bounded or subpolynomial number of endpoint points on EACH active fiber can still sum to the full B^(1/3) denominator scale.
To close P/M3 one still needs one of:
- endpoint-active base values form an o(1) weighted fraction of M3 base mass;
- a rank/Selmer statistic forcing such exceptional mass decay;
- a two-fibration/quotient correlation theorem that directly controls the weighted endpoint frequency.
The current literature screen did not produce such a theorem on this exact Pythagorean-base family.
### 18.3 Twist-family theorem mismatch
Section17 proves that the branch cross-ratio varies as 1/t^4.
Therefore the genus-5 family is not geometrically constant.
Consequently uniform twist-family theorems do not apply by simply declaring the fibers twists of one fixed genus-5 curve.
SILVERMAN_TWIST_DIRECT_MATCH=false.
### 18.4 V4 / Selmer statistics mismatch
The V4 decomposition can reduce Jacobian arithmetic to lower-genus quotient factors, but this is only a structural adapter.
Existing arithmetic-statistics theorems require a specific coregular representation / hyperelliptic / twist-family parameterization and a compatible height/ordering.
No exact adapter currently identifies the M3 Pythagorean-base physical measure with such a published family ordering.
Therefore no Selmer-density credit is imported.
### Audit Result 18-A — MethodCMovingFamilySearchNegative
The special genus-5 structure narrows the theorem species but does not currently match an off-the-shelf moving-family rational-point or Selmer-frequency theorem strong enough for P/M3.
METHOD_C_DGH_NEARMATCH=true
METHOD_C_DGH_DIRECT_CLOSURE=false
METHOD_C_TWIST_FAMILY_DIRECT_MATCH=false
METHOD_C_ARITHMETIC_STATISTICS_ADAPTER=false
METHOD_C_STATUS=LIVE_NARROWED_THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED
Reopen METHOD C only for:
- a theorem controlling rank/Selmer exceptional mass on this exact Pythagorean-base family;
- an exact representation adapter to an arithmetic-statistics family;
- a direct two-fibration incidence theorem.

## 18. Cross-route new mechanism search — R1 Poisson/trace-function candidate
### 18.1 Why R1 deserves one bounded reopen
The directional formulation places the terminal interaction problem on
H_j(B)=M2,j(B)+M3(B),
not on the sparse M3 measure.
Exact cell counts are
F=0,S=0 : M2,j-N2,j
F=0,S=1 : N2,j
F=1,S=0 : M3-P
F=1,S=1 : P.
The target remains
Cov_j(S,F)_+ = o(Pr_j(F=1)),
or equivalently any
T_j
=
(P/M3)/(N2,j/M2,j)
<< B^(1/2-delta)
for fixed delta>0.
H_j is the Stage18 directional shared-edge toric host with
M2,j(B)~C_j B(log B)^5,
exact physical height, and exact Pythagorean parametrization.
### 18.2 New external theorem species found
Recent 2025-2026 inputs are relevant as method species only.
A. Bonolis-Pierce-Woo 2025:
affine type-II thin-set counting by polynomial sieve and uniform Katz-Laumon/Fouvry-Katz stratification.
Direct R1/R4a transfer is not available because their ambient integer-box measure does not preserve the exact directional toric physical population.
B. Bonolis-Kowalski-Woo 2025:
uniform stratification theorems for exponential sums in families.
Potentially relevant after Poisson completion if R1 yields a bounded-complexity family of finite-field trace functions with controlled exceptional loci.
C. Fouvry-Kowalski-Michel-Sawin 2025:
bilinear trace-function bounds below the Polya-Vinogradov range under robust monodromy hypotheses.
Potentially relevant only after exact coefficient separation, conductor cleanup, and monodromy verification.
D. Bo He 2026:
a linked simultaneous-square problem is solved via exact parametrization, sieve, Poisson summation, finite-field cancellation, monodromy/exceptional-locus control, and switching.
The arithmetic target differs from R1, so this is method evidence, not theorem transfer.
DIRECT_THEOREM_TRANSFER_FOUND=false.
NEW_METHOD_SPECIES_FOUND=true.
### 18.3 New bounded R1 implementation
R1-TF = DirectionalToricSelectorPoissonTrace.
On one exact shared-edge direction:
1. parameterize H_j by the two primitive Pythagorean pairs;
2. write the centered F/S covariance before imposing the sparse M3 conditioning;
3. insert square-sieve / character weights without recharging Stage24 or Stage26 savings;
4. smooth the exact physical-height region;
5. apply Poisson summation in the genuine free lattice variables;
6. identify complete mixed sums as a trace-function family;
7. classify coincidence/symmetry loci;
8. apply uniform stratification and, if separation is available, bilinear trace-function bounds;
9. seek a power deficit strong enough for T_j << B^(1/2-delta).
### 18.4 First missing adapter
R1_DIRECTIONAL_TORIC_SELECTOR_POISSON_TRACE_ADAPTER:
produce an exact smoothed integer-parameter formula for the centered two-selector correlation on H_j such that
- primitive/canonical multiplicity is exact;
- R<=B is retained with no height-power loss;
- the zero frequency is the correct product-of-marginals term;
- nonzero frequencies form a bounded-complexity trace-function family;
- exceptional coincidence loci are explicit;
- coefficient energies fit a uniform stratification/bilinear theorem.
This is an adapter task, not another renaming of a correlation theorem.
### 18.5 Why this differs from the frozen SR-STR-169 loop
SR-STR-169 worked on a later sparse Stage27 survivor measure.
R1-TF starts earlier on H_j, whose toric/Pythagorean host and asymptotic count are already exact.
The key change is quantifier order:
center the two completion selectors on the easy two-face host BEFORE conditioning on third-face completion.
If successful, the covariance can be attacked by ambient toric cancellation instead of same-M3 transfer.

## 19. R1 quantitative narrowing — only a 1/12 correlation-power gain is sufficient
### 19.1 Rare-selector normalization
On the exact directional host
H_j=M2,j+M3,
write
alpha_j=Pr_j(F=1)=M3/H_j,
beta_j=Pr_j(S=1)=(N2,j+P)/H_j.
Certified global inputs give
H_j ~ C_j B(log B)^5,
M3 >> B^(1/3),
N2,j+P <<_epsilon B^(1/2+epsilon).
Hence
alpha_j >> B^(-2/3)(log B)^(-5),
beta_j <<_epsilon B^(-1/2+epsilon)(log B)^(-5).
Therefore
sqrt(beta_j/alpha_j)
 <<_epsilon
 B^(1/12+epsilon).
Let rho_j(B) be the Pearson correlation coefficient of the two Boolean selectors F,S on H_j:
rho_j
 =
Cov_j(S,F)
/ sqrt(Var_j(S)Var_j(F)).
Since alpha_j,beta_j->0,
Var(F)<=alpha_j,
Var(S)<=beta_j,
and thus
Cov_j(S,F)_+ / alpha_j
 <=
|rho_j| * sqrt(beta_j/alpha_j)
 <<_epsilon
 |rho_j| B^(1/12+epsilon).
But Section13 shows
P/M3
 =
N2,j/M2,j
 +
Cov_j(S,F)/[Pr_j(F=1)Pr_j(F=0)],
and Pr_j(F=0)->1.
Therefore:
### Theorem 19-A — OneTwelfthCorrelationReceiver
If for some fixed delta>0
|rho_j(B)| << B^(-1/12-delta)
for one fixed direction j, then
P(B)/M3(B)->0.
More generally any estimate
rho_j(B)=o(B^(-1/12))
with sufficient control of the epsilon/log factors closes the target.
This is a sufficient receiver, not an assertion that the true correlation has this scale.
### 19.2 Interpretation
R1 does NOT require:
- asymptotic independence;
- T_j->1;
- a full asymptotic for M3;
- a fixed-power upper for P below B^(1/3).
It only requires a fixed power improvement of slightly more than 1/12 beyond the rare-selector Cauchy-Schwarz normalization.
This is materially weaker than the earlier absolute double-square target.
### 19.3 Existing Section112-113 finite-field assets already match the selector pair
The four-variable primitive-face model defines exact squareclass representatives
E4 = third-face square condition,
H4 = space square condition.
Section112 proves complete mixed character bounds for
chi(E4),
chi(H4),
chi(E4 H4),
with square-root-quality complete cancellation.
Section113 proves the additive Fourier hierarchy:
- zero frequency O(p^(7/2));
- one-pair-degenerate frequency O(p^(5/2));
- generic nonzero frequency O(p^2);
and squarefree incomplete-box bounds.
Thus the finite-field cancellation component needed by R1-TF is not new.
What failed in Section113 was the ABSOLUTE simultaneous-square count:
elementary double square sieve only reached about B^(4/5).
R1 is different:
it asks for a CENTERED covariance on the easy two-face host, normalized by the rare third-face selector.
Therefore the old B^(4/5) absolute ceiling does not by itself rule out R1-TF.
### 19.4 Updated R1-TF first missing step
The first missing input is no longer generic finite-field cancellation.
It is:
R1_CENTERED_PHYSICAL_SQUARE_SELECTOR_EXPANSION
Construct a physical-height-preserving expansion of the global square selectors F,S on H_j such that:
1. the zero-frequency / principal contribution is exactly the product of the directional marginals;
2. the remaining centered term can use the Section113 nonzero-frequency Fourier bounds;
3. primitive/canonical/gcd masks are retained without changing measure;
4. no existing Stage24 or Stage26 saving is charged twice;
5. the final correlation coefficient gains >B^(-1/12) over the rare-selector Cauchy scale.
### 19.5 New literature rematch
Recent sources materially improve the available method species but do not directly prove the receiver:
- Bonolis-Pierce-Woo 2025: type-II thin-set sieve + uniform stratification; direct exact-host transfer absent.
- Bonolis-Kowalski-Woo 2025: uniform stratification in exponential-sum families; relevant to the nonzero-frequency family.
- Fouvry-Kowalski-Michel-Sawin 2025: bilinear trace-function estimates below Polya-Vinogradov; relevant after exact separation/monodromy.
- Bo He 2026: linked-square problem solved with parametrization + sieve + Poisson + finite-field correlation + switching; method analogue only.
- Buggenhout-Cluckers-Santens-Vermeulen 2025: strong projective thin-set degree>=4 bounds; no exact toric anticanonical-height adapter.
No direct theorem transfer is certified.
### 19.6 Current disposition
R1_TF_FINITE_FIELD_INPUT_ALREADY_AVAILABLE=true
R1_TF_ABSOLUTE_DOUBLE_SIEVE_CEILING_NOT_DECISIVE_FOR_CENTERED_ROUTE=true

## 14. R2 reentry check — old transfer wall materially narrowed, not closed
### 14.1 Historical R2 wall
The old Class-3 wall was:
local / parameter-side squareclass information
  -> exact primitive canonical physical M3(B) under R<=B.
ARCHIVE Section38 states the missing ingredient explicitly:
"The local law itself is already available. The missing ingredient is the global physical weighting."
The operational form was:
- fix modulus q;
- fix signed squarefree delta / cross-gcd labels;
- count exact physical M3 points in the slice;
- require an error uniform enough in delta and cross-gcd labels;
- sum the slice estimates without losing the saving.
### 14.2 What later work actually repaired
The later physical divisor / delta / norm work substantially reduced the semantic part of this wall.
Already exact:
- one-object / one-count physical coordinates;
- primitive/canonical normalization;
- exact physical height;
- delta_odd | rad(PQ);
- only B^o(1) squareclass allocations per physical packet;
- fixed-delta individual equations genus 0;
- coupled fixed-delta slice retains one genus-1 core;
- four-cross-gcd packetization;
- fixed residual packet M3 multiplicity B^o(1);
- small C_- region M3-negligible;
- exact primitive norm rectangle;
- physical recovery h=gcd(Y,Q)*gcd(Z,P);
- fixed N_-, fixed D, fixed (h,J), fixed X and related fibers have B^o(1) multiplicity.
Thus the old "parameter-to-object transfer" is no longer the correct description of the blocker.
The object semantics and height are now physically exact.
### 14.3 What remains genuinely missing
What is still absent is cancellation across a polynomially large collection of moving physical packets.
A fixed packet may contain only B^o(1) points, but there are polynomially many packets and their arithmetic states may be biased.
Therefore packet anti-concentration alone does not imply same-M3 equidistribution.
The remaining wall is not:
"recover the physical M3 measure."
It is now:
"prove a nontrivial average cancellation theorem across the exact moving packet family."
### Receiver 14-A — PhysicalPacketAverageCharacterCancellation
Let Pi range over any exact packetization retaining:
- primitive/canonical M3;
- R<=B;
- one-object/one-count;
- all cross-gcd / delta / common-core labels needed to reconstruct the physical point.
For a fixed good odd prime ell, let chi_ell(E) denote the nontrivial squareclass character relevant to the terminal condition on a physical M3 point E.
A sufficient fixed-prime theorem is
sum_{Pi} sum_{E in Pi(B)} chi_ell(E)
  = o(M3(B)).
A stronger quantitative form uniform for squarefree q is
sum_{Pi} sum_{E in Pi(B)} chi_q(E)
  << M3(B)*rho(q) + Err(B,q),
with rho(q) decaying multiplicatively and an error summable for q in a growing family.
This formulation preserves the exact physical measure and does not require ambient packet density assumptions.
### 14.4 Why fixed-packet B^o(1) is not enough
Suppose every packet has at most B^o(1) points.
This only implies that M3(B) is spread over at least B^(1/3-o(1)) packets.
It does not prevent every occupied packet from carrying the same terminal squareclass sign.
Hence:
FIXED_PACKET_ANTICONCENTRATION != PACKET_AVERAGE_EQUIDISTRIBUTION.
The missing theorem must see correlations between packet labels and terminal characters.
### 14.5 Later one-factor/common-core work does not close R2
Sections99–104 identify genuine moving moduli K_- and h and exact root packets.
These are valuable arithmetic decompositions, but the large-modulus savings remain conditional on averaging over the same physical M3 packet family.
Section103 explicitly shows that the first split-product eliminant is too diagonal for a direct Reuss transfer.
Section104 corrects the proportional-branch interpretation and confirms that raw bilinear determinant savings are not available automatically.
Therefore no later result supplies the missing packet-average theorem.
### 14.6 R2 current theorem gate
R2 has materially improved from a broad semantic transfer problem to a precise same-measure averaging problem.
Current minimal theorem species:
PhysicalPacketAverageCharacterCancellation
or equivalently
ExactM3GrowingLocalSquareUpperSieve.
Required features:
- exact physical M3 measure;
- primitive/canonical/R<=B preserved;
- no double charge of common-core/root-line savings;
- uniformity over the moving packet labels;
- enough modulus range to make the terminal survival factor tend to zero.
Existing packet/fiber B^o(1) theorems are supporting inputs only.
### 14.7 R2 anti-loop disposition
R2_REENTRY_MATERIALLY_JUSTIFIED=true because the later exact physical work changed the blocker from a parameter-transfer problem to a packet-average theorem gate.
However no current retained theorem closes that gate.
R2_STATUS=LIVE_THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED.
Reopen R2 only on one of:
- a same-measure character/large-sieve theorem on the exact packet family;
- an exact low-rank separation of the packet character making SR-STR-161/165 applicable;
- a determinant/incidence theorem with a nondegenerate eliminant after all physical masks are retained;
- a new packet image-size theorem that reduces the number of bad packets below B^(1/3-epsilon).
Further coordinate rewrites alone do not count as progress.
### 14.8 Comparison with R1
R1 and R2 now end at related but distinct gates:
R1:
  one normalized terminal covariance on the exact directional two-face host.
R2:
  character cancellation across the exact M3 moving packet family.
R1 is logically closer to P/M3, but its selector covariance theorem is absent.
R2 has much richer local arithmetic input, but requires broader global averaging.
Neither dominates the other.

## 15. R3 reentry check — moving-fibration gate is exact and still open
### 15.1 What is already proved
For the rational fibration
t1=a2/a3,
ARCHIVE proves uniformly for every occupied physical fiber:
N_t(B)=B^o(1).
Since M3(B)>>B^(1/3), the occupied base image satisfies
#T_M3(B) >= B^(1/3-o(1)).
Hence no finite, polylogarithmic, or B^o(1) collection of fibers can carry a positive proportion of M3.
This permanently rules out fixed-fiber Mordell-Weil enumeration as a whole-population strategy.
### 15.2 Transverse fibration progress
A second rational fibration was constructed after the earlier Q(i)-valued attempt.
The physically useful pair is:
t1=a2/a3,
t2=(a2+a3)/(b2+b3).
On a fixed generic t1-fiber, t2 is nonconstant and the map
E_{t1} -> P1_{t2}
has exact degree 2.
Therefore the two fibrations are genuinely transverse, not a base reparametrization of one another.
This is real geometric progress and supplies an exact two-fibration incidence architecture.
### 15.3 What it does not prove
The transverse degree-2 map does not count which rational base specializations are occupied under the exact physical height.
ARCHIVE explicitly records that geometric MW information and transverse-fibration structure do not by themselves give a physical M3 asymptotic or endpoint density theorem.
The unresolved quantifier is moving-base uniformity.
### Receiver 15-A — EndpointActiveBaseImagePowerSaving
Let
T_P(B)={t1 : the t1-fiber contains at least one endpoint point of height <=B}.
A sufficient theorem is:
#T_P(B) <= B^(1/3-delta)
for some fixed delta>0.
Because every fixed t1-fiber contributes only B^o(1) points,
P(B) <= B^(1/3-delta+o(1))=o(M3(B)).
A weaker weighted theorem
sum_t P_t(B)=o(sum_t N_t(B))
is also sufficient.
### Receiver 15-B — TwoFibrationIncidenceEnergySaving
Using the transverse pair (t1,t2), another sufficient theorem is a same-height incidence/energy estimate showing that endpoint-active rational points occupy an o(1) proportion of the physical M3 incidence graph.
The degree-2 map is the exact geometric input; the missing piece is a uniform rational-point/specialization theorem over the moving base.
### 15.4 R3 theorem boundary
R3 exact assets:
- physical rational t1-map;
- uniform fixed-fiber B^o(1) count;
- polynomially many occupied t1-values forced;
- second transverse rational fibration;
- exact degree-2 map between fibrations.
Missing:
- moving-base distribution / specialization theorem;
- or a two-fibration incidence-energy estimate with exact physical height.
No retained theorem currently supplies either.
Therefore:
R3_STATUS=LIVE_THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED.
Reopen only on:
- a uniform moving-specialization theorem;
- a quantitative two-fibration incidence/energy estimate;
- an endpoint-active base-image compression theorem;
- an exact external theorem matching the physical height and rational base quantifiers.
Further fixed-fiber rank/MW computations do not count as progress.

## 16. R4a reentry check — terminal-cover geometry remains a two-layer route
### 16.1 Exact assets retained
Let pi:S->K_c be the endpoint degree-two cover from the Euler K3 to the full endpoint surface.
For every fixed integral degree-6 curve C subset K_c, the restricted cover is non-split.
The dominating lift has endpoint contribution at most
O(B^(1/6+o(1))).
Thus every fixed finite collection of such curves is negligible against
M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
This is a genuine numerator exponent improvement on a nontrivial geometric subpopulation.
### 16.2 What Stage29/Testa-Stoll adds
On the full endpoint surface Sbar, Testa-Stoll completely classifies all integral curves of canonical/projective degree <=6.
In the positive nondegenerate physical chamber:
- degree <=6 positive-dimensional endpoint carrier families are absent;
- there are no integral degree-6 curves on the endpoint surface;
- the first possible physical endpoint curve-family degree is >=8.
This strengthens the fixed-curve terminal-cover picture.
However it is a theorem on the endpoint surface, not a classification of all accumulating curves on the Euler K3 denominator.
### 16.3 Higher low-genus carrier work is still incomplete
The frozen Stage29/Stage32 low-genus Picard receivers remain:
R29-LG2,
R29-LG2-EFF,
R29-LG2-MB.
Their audited scope includes unibranch genus-0 / genus-1 numerical windows through degree 176/192 plus effectivity and multibranch corrections.
Current literature and bounded computation strengthen filters but do not discharge the full receivers.
Therefore higher-degree low-genus endpoint carrier census is not complete.
### 16.4 The two distinct layers that remain
Even if every low-genus endpoint carrier receiver were eventually closed, the M3 density target would still require a whole-population statement.
Layer A — accumulating/carrier contribution:
classify and sum every curve/family capable of carrying denominator-scale or numerator-relevant mass, with exact physical height.
Layer B — residual population:
prove that the endpoint lift on the Zariski-dense residual M3 population contributes o(B^(1/3)) or o(M3(B)).
Layer B is not supplied by low-degree curve classification alone.
Hence:
LOW_DEGREE_CARRIER_CLASSIFICATION != WHOLE_ENDPOINT_COUNT.
### Receiver 16-A — AccumulatingCarrierTotalBound
A sufficient Layer-A theorem is
P_acc(B)=o(B^(1/3)).
The fixed degree-6 theorem proves this only for every fixed finite subcollection, not for a moving family of curves.
### Receiver 16-B — ResidualEndpointCount
After removing all certified accumulating carriers, prove
P_res(B)=o(B^(1/3)).
Any relative version
P_res(B)=o(M3_res(B))
is also sufficient when the removed denominator mass is separately controlled.
### 16.5 R4a theorem boundary
R4a exact assets:
- global endpoint cover S->K_c;
- fixed degree-6 nonsplitting;
- B^(1/6+o(1)) fixed-curve endpoint count;
- complete endpoint degree<=6 carrier classification;
- positive physical degree<=6 carrier absence;
- extensive but incomplete higher low-genus Picard machinery.
Missing:
- total accumulating-family count under physical height;
- residual endpoint count.
Therefore:
R4A_STATUS=LIVE_TWO_LAYER_THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED.
Reopen on:
- complete effective carrier classification with a summable family count;
- a residual rational-point theorem on the terminal cover;
- a determinant/geometric-sieve theorem after carrier removal;
- a theorem proving all denominator-scale accumulation is captured by a controlled explicit family.
Do not promote completion of the finite Picard census itself to P/M3 credit.

## 17. R5 reentry check — denominator upgrade remains mechanism-discovery stage
### 17.1 Exact closure implication
The current whole-endpoint upper is
P(B) <<_epsilon B^(1/2+epsilon).
Therefore any theorem
M3(B) >> B^(1/2+delta)
for one fixed delta>0 would immediately imply
P(B)/M3(B)->0
by choosing epsilon<delta.
This implication is exact and uses no interaction assumption.
### 17.2 Where the present B^(1/3) lower came from
The current explicit lower arose by exposing the hidden two-dimensional freedom in the generalized Saunderson construction.
The historical improvement
B^(1/6) -> B^(1/3)
came from replacing an effectively one-parameter view by a genuine two-parameter cone with bounded multiplicity and exact physical control.
Thus the denominator route is fundamentally a parameter-dimension / physical-height problem.
### 17.3 What was already tried
ARCHIVE explicitly investigated a possible next dimension gain through the empirical divisibility pattern
U1 | U2.
One concrete ansatz
m-n=r+s,
m+n=k(r-s)
matched a few examples but failed as a universal identity.
The induced transform had an involutive/scaling structure but did not create an additional free parameter family.
Therefore that branch did not improve the lower exponent.
### 17.4 Current minimal receiver
### Receiver 17-A — SuperHalfPowerM3Family
Construct an exact primitive/canonical M3 subfamily F(B) satisfying
#F(B) >> B^(1/2+delta)
for some fixed delta>0,
with:
- exact R<=B;
- bounded/subpolynomial multiplicity;
- primitive/canonical recovery;
- no endpoint assumption.
Any such family would close the M3 density target when combined with the existing endpoint upper.
### 17.5 Current theorem/mechanism status
No retained construction currently approaches exponent >1/2.
No theorem rules out the existence of such a family.
But unlike R1/R2/R3, there is not yet a precise one-theorem gate with an identified input architecture.
The missing object is first a new mechanism:
- an additional independent parameter freedom;
- a new dominant M3 component/family;
- or a direct lower-count theorem for a broad M3 region.
Hence:
R5_STATUS=UNTESTED_MECHANISM_DISCOVERY.
This is not a blocked theorem gate; it is earlier-stage discovery.
Reopen when:
- a new exact M3 parametrized family with apparent exponent ratio >1/2 is found;
- a broad K3/rational-point lower-count theorem becomes applicable to exact physical M3;
- or a new structural decomposition shows that an already-known family has hidden additional free dimension.
Do not spend primary effort on blind ansatz enumeration without a dimension-gain certificate.

## 17. R5 reentry check — denominator upgrade now has an exact family-growth threshold
Purpose: turn the vague idea “make M3 much larger” into a quantitative construction receiver.
Known globally:
M3(B) >> B^(1/3),
P(B) <<_epsilon B^(1/2+epsilon).
Hence any fixed delta>0 theorem
M3(B) >> B^(1/2+delta)
would imply P(B)/M3(B)->0 by choosing epsilon<delta.
### 17.1 General family exponent bookkeeping
Suppose an explicit exact primitive/canonical M3 family is parametrized by integer data of effective count
T^(k+o(1))
inside a positive-density admissible parameter region, has physical height
R << T^d,
and parameter-to-object multiplicity T^o(1).
Then it contributes
M3(B) >> B^(k/d-o(1)).
Therefore a family-construction proof of the denominator route requires
k/d > 1/2,
equivalently
2k>d.
This is a receiver condition, not a claim that every possible M3 lower-bound proof must arise from such a polynomial family.
### 17.2 Standard Saunderson scale
The classical Saunderson construction starts from a primitive Pythagorean triple, hence from two Euclid integer parameters.
After substitution, the physical Euler-brick edges have total degree 6 in those parameters.
Thus
k=2,
d=6,
and the natural count scale is
B^(2/6)=B^(1/3).
This exactly matches the certified lower exponent.
### 17.3 Previous attempted upgrade
ARCHIVE Phase1.1 investigated the observed U1|U2 subpopulation and the ansatz
m-n=r+s,
m+n=k(r-s).
It produced only sparse hits and an involutive pairing under iteration; it did not create a new genuinely free parameter dimension or lower the physical height degree.
Therefore that branch does not satisfy 2k>d and gives no denominator upgrade.
### Receiver 17-A — SuperHalfPowerM3Family
To reopen R5 by explicit construction, produce an exact primitive/canonical M3 family with:
1. effective parameter count T^(k+o(1));
2. physical height O(T^d);
3. multiplicity T^o(1);
4. 2k>d.
Any equivalent counting mechanism proving M3(B)>>B^(1/2+delta) also reopens R5.
### 17.4 R5 disposition
No retained family currently meets Receiver17-A.
No theorem rules out such a family or a non-parametric denominator lower-bound mechanism.
Hence:
R5_STATUS=UNTESTED_MECHANISM_FREE.
Do not spend primary effort on random algebraic ansatz search unless it comes with a credible k/d improvement certificate.
R5_REENTRY_CHECK_COMPLETE=true
R5_SUPERHALF_THRESHOLD=2k>d

## 18. R10 preflight — direct physical occupied-pair incidence
Let X be the unique odd edge and D=sqrt(Y^2+Z^2) on a canonical M3 object.
Define
W_B={(X,D): some E in M3(B) has these coordinates},
E_B={(X,D) in W_B: X^2+D^2 is an integer square}.
The endpoint condition is exactly the fourth Pythagorean relation
X^2+D^2=R^2.
The retained fixed-(X,D) theorem gives
mu_B(X,D)=#{E in M3(B):(X(E),D(E))=(X,D)} <= B^o(1).
Hence
|W_B| <= M3(B) <= B^o(1)|W_B|,
P(B) <= B^o(1)|E_B|.
### Receiver 18-A — OccupiedPhysicalPairIncidenceDeficit
Any fixed delta>0 theorem
|E_B| <= B^(-delta+o(1))*|W_B|
implies
P(B)/M3(B)->0.
This route is distinct from the one-factor/common-core route because it uses the full endpoint relation directly.
Ambient Pythagorean counting alone is insufficient: for fixed X the endpoint condition gives divisor-many D, but the occupied M3 image above fixed X is itself already only B^o(1). Separate fixed-X and fixed-D anti-concentration therefore gives no relative saving on W_B.
A genuine R10 theorem would need one of:
- expansion of the occupied M3 pair image;
- incidence-energy deficit against the Pythagorean graph;
- determinant-method counting retaining the M3 witness equations;
- two-coordinate equidistribution on W_B.
No retained theorem currently supplies such an estimate.
R10_STATUS=UNTESTED_THEOREM_GATE.

## 20. Cross-route mechanism search — McKinnon big-nef adapter tested and rejected in the naive form
Status: DERIVED_RESEARCH_RESULT_PENDING_HOSTILE_AUDIT.
External theorem checked:
David McKinnon, Counting Rational Points on K3 Surfaces, JNT 84 (2000), Theorem 2.1 / Corollary 2.2.
McKinnon treats a smooth K3 surface with a generically degree-two map to P1xP1 branched over a singular (4,4) curve. If the singular points give simple irreducible one-dimensional fibers E_i, write F1,F2 for the two ruling-fiber classes and
A_i=F1+F2-E_i.
For an ample divisor
D=sum_i a_i A_i+c1 F1+c2 F2,
a=sum_i a_i,
his residual-point exponent contains
alpha=max{4/(a+c1+c2), 2/(a+c1), 2/(a+c2)}.
### 20.1 Exact physical specialization
The Stage14/20 Euler K3 blow-down has four ordinary double points on the (4,4) branch, at the four toric corners.
The resolved physical polarization is
M=pi^*(-K_Y),
Y=Bl_4(P1xP1),
-K_Y=2H1+2H2-E1-E2-E3-E4.
Under the direct map to P1xP1 this gives the corresponding K3 divisor class
M=2F1+2F2-(E1+E2+E3+E4).
Using McKinnon's A_i basis:
sum_{i=1}^4 A_i
 =4F1+4F2-(E1+E2+E3+E4),
hence
M=sum_i A_i-2F1-2F2.
Therefore the physical coefficients are
a_i=1,
a=4,
c1=c2=-2.
Thus
a+c1+c2=0.
### Theorem 20-A — McKinnonPhysicalBoundaryDegeneracy
The exact physical quasi-polarization M lies on the boundary at which the first McKinnon exponent denominator vanishes.
Consequently McKinnon Theorem 2.1 does not merely fail because its statement says ample: its explicit counting exponent degenerates at the physical M-ray.
A naive ample perturbation M_epsilon approaching M makes the corresponding first exponent term blow up rather than converge to a useful physical-height exponent.
The fact that the M-null curves lie outside the primitive positive physical open does not by itself repair this exponent degeneration.
### 20.2 Strategic consequence
The candidate
McKinnonBigNefPhysicalOpenAdapter
is NOT obtained by a routine ample perturbation or by deleting the null boundary curves.
Reopening this route requires a genuinely new proof that exploits additional arithmetic/physical restrictions in the diagonal-growth region responsible for a+c1+c2=0.
This is stronger than the old statement MCKINNON_DIRECT_PHYSICAL_HEIGHT_TRANSFER=false.
R4A_MCKINNON_NAIVE_BIG_NEF_EXTENSION=false
R4A_MCKINNON_BOUNDARY_DENOMINATOR_ZERO=true
R4A_NEW_RESIDUAL_THEOREM_FOUND=false
### 20.3 Literature screen note
Recent quasi-polarized K3 work of Rams--Schuett controls low-degree rational curves for big-and-nef polarizations but does not provide the required bounded-height residual rational-point theorem at physical degree M^2=8.
The 2026 degree-two K3 rational-point work located in the screen concerns infinitude/field extensions, not a physical-height residual counting theorem.
Therefore R4a remains
R4A_STATUS=LIVE_TWO_LAYER_THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED.
NEXT_SEARCH_TARGET=R3_MOVING_FIBER_ENERGY_OR_R1_SELECTOR_COVARIANCE
PRIMARY_ROUTE=UNDECIDED
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false

## 21. R1 blocker-level joint local interaction — corrected exact support statement
Status: DERIVED_AFTER_HOSTILE_SOURCE_RECONCILIATION; GLOBAL_CREDIT_NOT_GRANTED.
The previous draft first overclaimed a codimension-two statement from geometric branch divisors, then correctly withdrew it after noticing that the Stage20 blocker lives on toric exceptional divisors rather than directly on the third-face branch divisor.
A second source-level reconciliation with the exact Stage15 split-prime decomposition now identifies the relevant supports correctly.
### 21.1 Stage19 first-order blocker support
For good split primes p=1 mod 4,
A=(mr+i ns)(mr-i ns),
B=(ms+i nr)(ms-i nr).
On the smooth toric resolution Y=Bl_4(P1xP1), the four squareclass-odd divisors are the strict transforms
D_A,+, D_A,-, D_B,+, D_B,-.
The four exceptional divisors occur in div(A) or div(B) with multiplicity 2 and are therefore invisible to valuation parity.
Accordingly the Stage19 rejection event
v_p(A) != v_p(B) mod 2
has first-order support on the strict A/B divisor tubes, not on the generic exceptional-divisor tubes.
The exact marginal rejection remains
1-alpha_p
 =4p(p-1)^2/((p+1)^2(p^2+6p+1))
 =4/p+O(p^-2).
### 21.2 Stage20 first-order blocker support
The audited Stage14-e11 state is
G: |n1|=|n2|>0,
where (n1,n2)=(v_p(q1),v_p(q2)).
A state-G point specializes to the OPEN STRATUM of one of the four exceptional toric divisors.
After removal of the common valuation, the blocker is the residue condition
chi_p(x^2+y^2)=-1
on that exceptional divisor.
Its exact marginal mass is
delta_p
 =2(p-chi_4(p))/(p^2+6p+1)
 =2/p+O(p^-2).
### Theorem 21-A — FirstOrderBlockerSupportsSeparate
For every sufficiently good split prime, the first-order Stage19 blocker support lies on the strict A/B divisor strata, while the first-order Stage20 state-G blocker lies on the open exceptional-divisor strata.
These mod-p first-order supports are disjoint.
Possible interaction is therefore confined to the finite boundary where strict A/B divisors meet exceptional divisors, or to higher-order p-adic refinements.
Such boundary residue classes have total normalized mass O(p^-2).
Hence, for the KNOWN SIEVE BLOCKERS only,
Pr(B_19,p intersection B_20,p)=O(p^-2).
Equivalently, if sigma_p denotes survival against both known blockers,
sigma_p
 = alpha_p*(1-delta_p)+O(p^-2).
Thus
sigma_p/[alpha_p*(1-delta_p)]
 =1+O(p^-2),
and the relative Euler product of the KNOWN blocker interaction converges absolutely after removal of finitely many bad primes.
### 21.3 Firewall — this is not local independence of the actual completion predicates
The theorem above concerns only the two certified first-order sieve blockers.
It does NOT prove that the exact local third-face-square predicate and exact local space-square predicate are independent.
It does NOT identify the exact joint local lift density for the two degree-two covers.
It does NOT imply any global terminal interaction estimate.
In particular it does not prove
T_j=(P/M3)/(N2,j/M2,j)=O(1)
or any bound on T_j.
What it proves is narrower:
the B-power-sized terminal enhancement, if present, is not generated by overlap of the two already-certified first-order good-prime blocker systems.
### 21.4 Strategic consequence
The known local blocker mechanisms now have:
- marginal first-order dimensions already compared in Stage28;
- finite relative marginal Euler-product bias;
- no new first-order cross-blocker term.
Therefore METHOD A remains a genuinely global correlation problem.
Any successful R1 theorem must exploit information beyond the currently certified first-order local blockers, such as:
- physical-height correlation;
- higher-order local structure with effective growing modulus;
- accumulating multisections / exceptional support;
- global arithmetic correlation of the two quadratic lifts.
R1_KNOWN_BLOCKER_FIRST_ORDER_SUPPORTS_SEPARATE=true

## 22. R5 geometric no-go for naive extra-parameter denominator families
Status: DERIVED_STRUCTURAL_RESULT.
The denominator-upgrade route asks for a mechanism proving
M3(B) >> B^(1/2+delta)
for some fixed delta>0.
Section17 gave a polynomial-family bookkeeping rule: a primitive family with T^(k+o(1)) effective parameter tuples, physical height O(T^d), and T^o(1) multiplicity contributes B^(k/d-o(1)).
A naive idea is therefore to add one genuinely free rational parameter to a Saunderson-type family.
The K3 geometry forbids this in the bounded-multiplicity Zariski-dense form.
### Theorem 22-A — NoDominantRationalSurfaceParameterizationOfKc
Let K_c be the smooth Euler-brick K3 surface over Q.
There is no dominant generically finite rational map
P^2 -->> K_c
over characteristic zero.
More generally, no rational surface can dominate K_c generically finitely.
Reason: such a map would make K_c unirational. A unirational surface in characteristic zero is uniruled, whereas a smooth K3 surface has Kodaira dimension 0 and is not uniruled.
### 22.1 Consequence for explicit polynomial/rational families
Suppose one proposes a homogeneous three-or-more-variable rational parametrization of primitive Euler bricks whose projectivized parameter space has dimension at least 2 and whose generic parameter-to-object multiplicity is finite.
If its image is Zariski dense in K_c, it contradicts Theorem22-A.
Therefore such a construction must instead have at least one of:
- image contained in a proper curve/subvariety;
- positive-dimensional generic fibers / large parameter redundancy;
- failure of generic rational dominance;
- nonuniform arithmetic restrictions that destroy the naive parameter-volume exponent.
Thus simply adding an independent rational parameter to the Saunderson construction cannot by itself produce a bounded-multiplicity Zariski-dense denominator family.
### 22.2 What R5 must look like if it succeeds
A super-half-power M3 lower bound is not ruled out.
But it cannot arise from one naive dominant rational parameterization of the K3 surface.
Plausible surviving species are instead:
1. summation over infinitely many distinct rational/low-genus curves whose degrees/heights vary;
2. moving elliptic-fibration arithmetic with many occupied fibers;
3. an intrinsic rational-point counting lower theorem on K_c;
4. a non-rational parameter space with arithmetic multiplicity controlled in a genuinely new way.
This links the denominator route back to the moving-fiber / accumulating-curve problems rather than to another free-parameter ansatz search.
### 22.3 Updated R5 disposition
R5_SUPERHALF_THRESHOLD=2k>d remains a valid bookkeeping criterion for an individual polynomial family.
But
R5_NAIVE_EXTRA_FREE_PARAMETER_ROUTE=GEOMETRICALLY_BLOCKED.
Do not search random three-parameter rational identities unless their projective image dimension and fiber multiplicity are analyzed first.
R5_STATUS=UNTESTED_GLOBAL_COUNTING_MECHANISM
R5_SINGLE_DOMINANT_RATIONAL_FAMILY_ROUTE=false
This does not prove any upper bound for M3 and does not rule out M3(B)>>B^(1/2+delta).
NEXT_ITEM=CrossRouteMovingFamilyOrAccumulatingCurveMechanism
PRIMARY_ROUTE=UNDECIDED
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false

## 23. METHOD D upgrade — all physical degree-6 rational carriers are collectively negligible
Status: DERIVED_STRUCTURAL_RESULT.
Section37 proved a fixed-curve theorem:
for every fixed integral physical degree-6 curve C subset K_c, the restricted endpoint double cover is non-split, and the endpoint lift over C contributes at most
O(B^(1/6+o(1))).
The previous MAIN only promoted this to a fixed finite collection.
For rational carriers of physical degree 6, the finiteness can be supplied geometrically.
### Theorem 23-A — FinitenessOfDegreeSixRationalCarriers
On the fixed Euler K3 surface K_c, there are only finitely many integral rational curves of physical projective degree 6.
Reason:
1. the physical big-nef line bundle M defines the fixed projective canonical/physical image after contracting only the finite M-null boundary configuration;
2. an integral curve with M.C=6 is not contracted, so it maps to a projective curve of fixed degree 6;
3. fixed projective degree gives a bounded Hilbert-scheme problem (only finitely many relevant Hilbert polynomials occur);
4. a positive-dimensional family of distinct rational curves on a smooth characteristic-zero K3 would sweep a surface and make K_c uniruled;
5. a K3 surface is not uniruled.
Therefore the rational-curve locus in the bounded degree-6 Hilbert problem is zero-dimensional and finite.
This statement concerns embedded curve carriers, not parameterizations of one fixed curve.
### Theorem 23-B — CollectiveDegreeSixEndpointBound
Let R_6 be the union of all integral rational curves C subset K_c with physical degree 6.
Then
# {endpoint points of height <=B lying above R_6}
 = O(B^(1/6+o(1))).
Proof:
there are finitely many such C by Theorem23-A, and Section37 supplies O(B^(1/6+o(1))) for each fixed C. A finite sum preserves the exponent.
Since
M3(B)>>B^(1/3),
all physical degree-6 rational carriers together are negligible in P(B)/M3(B).
### 23.1 Scope firewall
This does NOT classify rational curves of other physical degrees.
It does NOT control moving genus-one families or the Zariski-dense residual population.
It does NOT show that all B^(1/3)-scale denominator mass is carried by degree-6 rational curves.
It does remove one ambiguity from METHOD D:
the degree-6 rational-carrier contribution no longer requires an additional family-count theorem.
METHOD_D_DEGREE6_RATIONAL_CARRIERS_FINITE=true
METHOD_D_ALL_DEGREE6_RATIONAL_ENDPOINT_CONTRIBUTION=O(B^(1/6+o(1)))
METHOD_D_DEGREE6_RATIONAL_LAYER_CLOSED=true
METHOD_D_MOVING_GENUS1_AND_RESIDUAL_OPEN=true
NEXT_ITEM=MethodDLowDegreeCarrierFloorAndMovingGenusOneAudit
PRIMARY_ROUTE=UNDECIDED
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false

Status: FIREWALL_REPAIR.
The Stage29/Testa--Stoll degree-<=6 integral-curve classification belongs to the full endpoint/cuboid surface S and its resolution.
It must not be silently reinterpreted as a complete degree-<=6 rational-curve classification on the Euler K3 denominator surface K_c.
Current denominator-side certified facts are narrower:
- K_c contains the Saunderson rational curve of physical degree 6;
- all physical degree-6 rational curves on K_c form a finite set, by Section23;

## 14. R2 reentry audit — fixed finite-prime transfer is enough
### 14.1 What the later physical work actually repaired
The old R2 wall was not absence of a local squareclass law. The local conditional law was already known.
The old wall was:
parameter / local congruence information
  -> exact primitive canonical M3(B) under R<=B.
Sections32–35 materially repaired the semantic side:
- exact one-object/one-count odd-edge divisor coordinates;
- exact primitivity;
- exact third-face equation;
- exact physical cutoff;
- integral bidegree-(2,2) physical model;
- correct primitive gcd normalization;
- signed squarefree delta descent;
- delta_odd | rad(PQ);
- only (PQ)^{o(1)} squareclass-allocation slices per fixed Pythagorean base.
Thus the remaining gap is no longer “what is the correct physical measure?”.
The remaining gap is a genuine counting theorem on that already-correct measure.
Sections100–104 add useful compression and anti-concentration, but they do not remove this last moving-family counting wall. In particular ambient root-line / common-core lattice summation remains too weak.
### 14.2 Growing-prime uniformity is stronger than necessary
Let S be any fixed finite set of good odd primes.
Let M3_S(B) be the exact primitive canonical M3(B) objects whose space-square local state is accepted at every p in S.
Every endpoint lies in M3_S(B), hence
P(B) <= M3_S(B)
for every fixed finite S.
The existing local calculation gives, at a good prime,
rho_p
 =
Pr(local space-square state | local M3 state)
 =
1/2 + O(p^(-1/2)).
Therefore there is p0 such that for every good p>=p0,
rho_p <= theta
for some fixed theta<1.
Suppose one proves the following exact physical theorem.
### Receiver R2-FIN — FixedFinitePrimeSameM3Transfer
For every fixed finite good-prime set S,
M3_S(B)/M3(B)
 ->
prod_{p in S} rho_p
as B->infinity,
or even only the upper form
limsup_{B->infinity}
M3_S(B)/M3(B)
 <=
prod_{p in S} rho_p.
Then
limsup_{B->infinity} P(B)/M3(B)
 <=
prod_{p in S} rho_p.
Now choose S to contain k sufficiently large good primes.
Then
prod_{p in S} rho_p <= theta^k.
Since k is arbitrary but fixed before taking B->infinity,
limsup_{B->infinity} P(B)/M3(B)=0.
Hence:
### Theorem 14-A — FixedFinitePrimeTransferSuffices
R2 does NOT require:
- a modulus growing with B;
- a growing-prime Selberg sieve;
- an error uniform in |S|;
- an explicit quantitative rate in B.
It is enough to prove the exact same-M3 transfer for every fixed finite set of good primes.
This is a strict weakening of the earlier “growing-prime” target.
### 14.3 Equivalent fixed-modulus formulation
For one fixed squarefree modulus
q = product_{p in S} p
let Omega_q be the CRT local acceptance set corresponding to the space-square condition at all p|q.
A sufficient theorem is:
### Receiver R2-Q — FixedModulusPhysicalM3CongruenceCount
For every fixed admissible q,
# { E in M3(B) : E mod q lies in Omega_q }
 =
mu_q(Omega_q) M3(B)
 + o_q(M3(B)),
with the exact physical conventions:
- primitive;
- canonical;
- R<=B;
- one object = one count;
- third-face square already imposed.
No q-uniform error is needed for the density-zero goal.
An upper-bound version with
limsup count/M3(B) <= mu_q(Omega_q)
is already sufficient.
### 14.4 What Section35 contributes to R2-Q
The delta descent reduces the physical M3 counting problem to only subpolynomially many squareclass allocations per fixed Pythagorean base.
For a fixed signed delta:
C^2-D^2 = delta r^2
is a conic, but the coupled physical slice retains a genus-1 core after the base identities are imposed.
Therefore the required theorem is not “count two independent conics”.
A correct sufficient slice statement is:
for every fixed modulus q and every admissible delta/cross-gcd packet,
count_{packet,q,state}(B)
 =
local_density_{packet,q,state}
 *
count_{packet}(B)
 +
error_{packet,q}(B),
with the summed error over all packets satisfying
sum_packets |error_{packet,q}(B)|
 =
o(M3(B)).
This is enough to prove R2-Q after summing the exact packet decomposition.
The quantitative dependence on q may be arbitrarily bad because q is fixed.
### 14.5 Current theorem gate after the simplification
The minimal missing theorem is now:
FixedModulusExactPhysicalM3Equidistribution
rather than
GrowingPrimeExactPhysicalM3Sieve.
This is materially narrower.
The exact physical coordinate/adaptor side is already available.
The unresolved part is analytic/arithmetic distribution on the coupled genus-1 / K3-selected physical family.
No retained Arsenal theorem currently closes this exact fixed-modulus M3 count.
### 14.6 R2 strategic status
R2 has therefore improved from:
“broad growing-prime local-to-global theorem”
to:
“fixed-modulus same-M3 equidistribution, one modulus at a time”.
This lowers the theorem strength required for closure substantially.

R1 asks for a same-host covariance deficit strong enough to improve the terminal interaction factor below exponent 1/2.
R2 now asks only for fixed-modulus equidistribution on exact M3, with no growing-modulus quantitative uniformity.
Therefore the previous routing statement “R1 clearly has the shorter theorem requirement” is no longer justified.
Current comparison:
R1:

- exact host;
- requires a quantitative correlation saving.

## 25. R3 branch merge — the two-rational-fibration incidence is the existing double-square cover
### 25.1 Exact geometry
Let
t1=a2/a3
be the primary rational fibration coordinate.
Section31 constructs the second rational fibration coordinate
t2=(a2+a3)/(b2+b3).
For fixed generic t1, the map
E_{t1} -> P1_{t2}
has degree exactly 2 and is the quotient by the involution
u -> 1/u.
Therefore the pair map
(t1,t2): K_c -> P1 x P1
is generically degree 2.
Section34 gives its exact quadratic equation. After restoring the genuine Pythagorean base and clearing denominators, the discriminant condition is the bidegree-(4,4) / integral bidegree-(2,2) square condition
E_- = square.
That is exactly the Euler/M3 lift condition.
The endpoint adds the companion plus condition
E_+ = square.
Hence the simultaneous two-fibration model is exactly:
rational base pair (t1,t2)
 -> E_- square = M3
 -> E_+ square = endpoint.
### Theorem 25-A — TwoFibrationBranchMerge
The proposed R3 subroute
“use the two transverse rational fibrations as an incidence theorem”
does not create an independent counting problem.
After the exact Section31–34 adapter it is equivalent to the already-known E_-/E_+ double-square cover model.
Therefore any analytic attack that works only by completing the pair (t1,t2) and counting the two square conditions belongs to the ambient double-square route, not to a new moving-fibration route.
### 25.2 Consequence for the R3 ledger
The following R3 branch is merged:
R3_TWO_FIBRATION_INCIDENCE
 ->
AMBIENT_DOUBLE_SQUARE_ANALYSIS.
The elementary completion ceiling already established there remains about
B^(4/5+epsilon),
so this merged branch does not currently improve the M3 goal.
This merge avoids double credit:
- the degree-2 quotient map is structural input;
- the E_-/E_+ square model is the actual counting receiver;
- no extra “transversality saving” may be multiplied on top without a separate theorem.
### 25.3 What remains genuinely distinct in R3
The surviving moving-fibration route is only the single-base weighted problem.
For the primary rational base t:
m_t(B)=#M3_t(B),
p_t(B)=#P_t(B).
The exact sufficient energy receiver remains
sum_t p_t(B)^2/m_t(B)
 =
o(M3(B)).
Equivalently, a small exceptional M3-mass theorem for fibers with large p_t/m_t suffices.
This route is genuinely different from the double-square model because it asks for arithmetic information across a polynomially large moving family of genus-one fibers, rather than an ambient count on P1 x P1.
### 25.4 R3 status after merge
R3_FIXED_FIBER_ANTICONCENTRATION=true

## 26. METHOD D upgrade — all fixed rational-carrier degrees 4,5,6 are collectively negligible
Status: DERIVED_STRUCTURAL_RESULT.
### 26.1 Finiteness at every fixed positive physical degree
Let M be the exact physical quasi-polarization on the Euler K3 K_c.
For every fixed integer d>0, there are only finitely many integral rational curves
C subset K_c
with
M.C=d.
Reason:
1. M is the fixed big-nef physical projective polarization; a curve with M.C=d>0 is not M-contracted.
2. Its physical projective image has fixed degree d.
3. For fixed projective degree only finitely many Hilbert polynomials can occur for integral rational curves.
4. If infinitely many distinct rational curves of one such Hilbert type existed, one Hilbert component would be positive-dimensional.
5. The universal family of distinct integral curves on a surface cannot have one-dimensional image; it would sweep K_c.
6. A sweeping family of rational curves would make the smooth characteristic-zero K3 surface K_c uniruled.
7. K3 surfaces are not uniruled.
Therefore every fixed positive physical degree has only finitely many rational carriers.
This generalizes the earlier degree-6 finiteness statement.
### 26.2 Nonsplitting for physical rational carriers of degree <=6
Let C subset K_c be an integral rational curve of physical degree
1 <= d <= 6
that carries a nondegenerate positive physical real branch.
Consider the endpoint double cover
pi:S -> K_c.
If the restricted cover over C split generically, an irreducible component upstairs would map birationally to C and would have endpoint canonical/physical degree d.
On the positive physical branch this would produce a positive nondegenerate endpoint curve family of degree <=6.
The audited Testa-Stoll low-degree classification and Stage29 positive-chamber filter exclude every such endpoint curve family through degree 6.
Hence the restricted endpoint cover is generically nonsplit on every physical rational carrier C of degree <=6.
This is a physical-chamber statement. It does not assert that the endpoint surface S has no low-degree curves globally; known low-degree curves lie in degenerate/nonphysical loci.
### 26.3 Uniform fixed-degree endpoint count
For such a fixed rational C of degree d<=6, the integral dominating component D of pi^{-1}(C) has degree
deg(D)=2d.
For a fixed D:
- if its normalization is rational, standard projective height counting gives
  O(B^(2/(2d)+o(1))) = O(B^(1/d+o(1)));
- if genus 1, the fixed Mordell-Weil group contributes only B^o(1);
- if genus >=2, rational points are finite.
Thus one fixed C contributes at most
O(B^(1/d+o(1)))
endpoint points.
By Section26.1 there are only finitely many rational C of each fixed d, so the union of all physical rational carriers of degree d contributes the same exponent:
P_rat,d(B)
 =
O(B^(1/d+o(1))).
### Theorem 26-A — RationalCarrierDegreesFourThroughSixNegligible
For d=4,5,6,
P_rat,d(B)
 =
o(B^(1/3)).
Therefore, using M3(B)>>B^(1/3),
P_rat,d(B)/M3(B) ->0
for each d=4,5,6, and also for their finite union.
In particular the previous degree-6 result extends to the entire fixed rational-carrier band
4 <= physical degree <= 6.
### 26.4 Exact remaining low-degree danger
The same argument gives only
d=3: O(B^(1/3+o(1))),
d=2: O(B^(1/2+o(1))),
d=1: O(B^(1+o(1))).
These are not sufficient against the certified denominator B^(1/3).
Therefore the rational-carrier part of METHOD D is now reduced to:
### Receiver D-LOW — KcPhysicalRationalDegree123Classification
Determine the physical rational curves C subset K_c with
M.C in {1,2,3},
and either:
- prove none meets the nondegenerate positive physical chamber; or
- analyze their endpoint lifts strongly enough to give o(B^(1/3)) total contribution.
No current retained theorem proves this classification.
The Stage19 statement “no physical M4 rational curves / no odd M-degree” belongs to the complementary space-cover K3 and may not be transferred to K_c.
### 26.5 What remains after this upgrade
METHOD D now decomposes into three genuinely distinct layers:
D1. rational carriers of degree 4,5,6:
CLOSED_NEGLIGIBLE.
D2. rational carriers of degree 1,2,3:
OPEN_FINITE_CLASSIFICATION.
D3. moving genus-one/higher-genus accumulating families and the Zariski-dense residual endpoint population:
OPEN_GLOBAL.
This is strictly sharper than the earlier “all accumulating curves remain open” statement.
METHOD_D_FIXED_RATIONAL_DEGREE_FINITE=true
METHOD_D_DEGREE_4_5_6_ENDPOINT_NEGLIGIBLE=true
METHOD_D_DEGREE_1_2_3_CLASSIFICATION_OPEN=true
METHOD_D_MOVING_GENUS1_AND_RESIDUAL_OPEN=true
NEXT_ITEM=KcPhysicalRationalDegree123Preflight
The next bounded task is to test whether the K_c Picard lattice / complete-intersection model already rules out physical rational curves of degree 1,2,3.
Do not import the complementary Stage19 odd-degree theorem without an exact K_c adapter.
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
