## 27. METHOD D breakthrough — no physical rational curves of degree 1,2,3; every fixed rational degree is negligible
Status: DERIVED_EXACT_ALGEBRA_PLUS_GEOMETRIC_COUNTING.
### 27.1 Binary-form Pythagorean lemma
Let A,B,C be homogeneous binary forms over Q of the same degree n with
A^2+B^2=C^2.
Remove g=gcd(A,B). Over Q(i)[s,t],
(C/g+iA/g)(C/g-iA/g)=(B/g)^2,
and unique factorization gives the usual primitive polynomial Pythagorean parametrization.
Consequently
n-deg(g)
is even.
In particular:
- a nonconstant degree-1 Pythagorean triple is impossible after removing gcd;
- a primitive degree-2 triple is, after a source PGL2 change and rational scaling,
  s^2-t^2, 2st, s^2+t^2;
- a nonconstant degree-3 triple has a common linear factor times a primitive degree-2 triple.
This degree-parity fact is used on each of the three face equations of K_c.
### 27.2 No nondegenerate physical lines
Let C subset K_c be an integral rational curve of physical degree 1.
Pull the six physical projective coordinates back to the normalization P1. They are homogeneous linear forms.
For each Pythagorean face, the degree-1 lemma forces the two edge coordinates and their face diagonal to have a common full linear factor and therefore constant projective ratio.
Applying this to the three edge pairs forces all three edge ratios to be constant, hence the projective K_c point is constant unless an edge coordinate vanishes identically.
Therefore:
### Theorem 27-A — NoPhysicalM1RationalCurveOnKc
There is no nonconstant integral rational curve of physical degree 1 meeting the nondegenerate positive physical chamber of K_c.
### 27.3 No nondegenerate physical conics
Assume C has physical degree 2.
If every edge pair has nontrivial gcd, the degree-2 lemma forces every pair ratio to be constant, hence the whole projective point is constant.
Thus a nonconstant curve would have some primitive edge pair.
By symmetry normalize that pair to
a2=s^2-t^2,
a3=2st.
Write a1 as a binary quadratic.
The equation
a1^2+a3^2=b2^2
gives
(b2-a1)(b2+a1)=4s^2t^2.
Since both factors are binary quadratics, up to exchanging them there are only two exponent allocations.
Case Q2-1:
both factors are proportional to st.
Then
a1=lambda st.
Imposing
a1^2+(s^2-t^2)^2=b3^2
forces either lambda=0 or lambda^2=4.
The nonzero option makes
a1^2+a3^2
have coefficient 8 on s^2t^2 and would require sqrt(8) rational.
Hence no nondegenerate Q-rational solution.
Case Q2-2:
one factor is proportional to s^2 and the other to t^2.
Then for k in Q^*,
a1=-k s^2+k^{-1}t^2.
For
a1^2+(s^2-t^2)^2
to be a rational quadratic square, coefficient comparison forces
k=+/-1
and then the leading square coefficient is 2, again requiring sqrt(2) in Q.
Therefore:
### Theorem 27-B — NoPhysicalM2RationalCurveOnKc
There is no nonconstant integral rational curve of physical degree 2 meeting the nondegenerate positive physical chamber of K_c.
### 27.4 No nondegenerate physical twisted cubics
Assume C has physical degree 3.
For any nonconstant edge pair, the binary-form lemma forces a common linear factor times a primitive quadratic Pythagorean triple.
Choose such a pair and normalize
a2=L(s^2-t^2),
a3=2Lst,
where after source scaling
L=s+lambda t;
the missing L=t chart is obtained by swapping s and t.
From
a1^2+a3^2=b2^2
we have
(b2-a1)(b2+a1)=4L^2s^2t^2.
Each factor has degree 3.
Up to exchanging the two factors, the exponent allocation among (L,s,t) is exactly one of
(1,1,1),
(2,1,0),
(2,0,1),
(1,2,0),
with multiplicative constants c and 4/c.
For each allocation substitute the resulting a1 into
a1^2+a2^2=b3^2
with a general rational cubic b3.
Exact coefficient elimination gives the following necessary factors.
Allocation (1,1,1):
(c-2)^2(c+2)^2
(c^2-4c-4)
(c^2+4c-4)
=0.
The two quadratic factors have no rational roots.
For c=+/-2 the resulting a1 is identically zero.
Allocation (2,1,0):
lambda^2=0,
and
(c-2)^2(c+2)^2(c^2+4)=0.
Thus rational candidates have lambda=0 and c=+/-2; the remaining coefficient equations require a rational square coefficient q^2=2.
Allocation (2,0,1):
lambda^3=0
together with the exact elimination polynomial
c^8-32c^6+224c^4-512c^2+256-4096lambda^2=0.
At lambda=0 it factors as
(c-2)^2(c+2)^2
(c^2-4c-4)
(c^2+4c-4).
The only rational candidates c=+/-2 make a1 identically zero.
Allocation (1,2,0):
(c-2)^2(c+2)^2(c^2+4)=0.
The rational candidates c=+/-2 again force the remaining square coefficient q^2=2.
These eliminations were independently reproduced by exact Groebner-basis computation in two CAS paths; no floating-point input is used.
Hence every rational solution in degree 3 is degenerate or non-rational.
Therefore:
### Theorem 27-C — NoPhysicalM3RationalCurveOnKc
There is no nonconstant integral rational curve of physical degree 3 meeting the nondegenerate positive physical chamber of K_c.
### 27.5 Consequence through degree 6
Section26 already proves:
- every fixed positive physical degree contains only finitely many rational carriers;
- every physical rational carrier of degree <=6 has nonsplit endpoint cover;
- for one degree-d rational carrier with nonsplit lift, endpoint count is
  O(B^(1/d+o(1))).
Theorems27-A/B/C remove degrees 1,2,3 entirely.
For d=4,5,6,
1/d < 1/3,
so their finite unions are o(B^(1/3)).
Thus:
### Theorem 27-D — AllPhysicalRationalCarriersThroughDegreeSixNegligible
The union of all nondegenerate physical rational curves C subset K_c with
M.C <= 6
contributes
o(B^(1/3))
endpoint points.
### 27.6 Every fixed rational degree is negligible
For fixed d>=7, even if the endpoint cover over C splits, a rational component upstairs has physical degree d and therefore contributes at most
O(B^(2/d+o(1))).
Since
2/d < 1/3
for d>=7,
and Section26.1 gives only finitely many rational C of each fixed d,
the union of all rational carriers of any one fixed degree d>=7 is also o(B^(1/3)).
Combining with d<=6:
### Theorem 27-E — FixedDegreeRationalCarrierNegligibility
For every fixed positive integer d,
# { endpoint points of height <=B lying above physical rational C subset K_c with M.C=d }
=
o(B^(1/3)).
Therefore every fixed finite band of rational-curve degrees is negligible in P(B)/M3(B).
### 27.7 What remains in METHOD D
This does NOT sum over rational curves whose degree grows with B.
It also does NOT control moving genus-one pencils or the Zariski-dense residual population.
The rational-carrier obstruction is now sharply localized to:
D-RAT-MOVING-DEGREE:
control the aggregate endpoint contribution of rational curves with physical degree d=d(B)->infinity.
D-GENUS1:
control moving genus-one families.
D-RESIDUAL:
control the Zariski-dense residual endpoint population.
This is a real compression of METHOD D.
K_C_PHYSICAL_RATIONAL_DEGREE_1_ABSENT=true
K_C_PHYSICAL_RATIONAL_DEGREE_2_ABSENT=true
K_C_PHYSICAL_RATIONAL_DEGREE_3_ABSENT=true
METHOD_D_RATIONAL_DEGREE_LE6_NEGLIGIBLE=true
METHOD_D_EVERY_FIXED_RATIONAL_DEGREE_NEGLIGIBLE=true
METHOD_D_MOVING_DEGREE_RATIONAL_OPEN=true
METHOD_D_MOVING_GENUS1_OPEN=true

## 28. METHOD F/B refinement — endpoint cover is a thin base-change over w=D/X
Status: DERIVED_EXACT_STRUCTURAL_RESULT.
### 28.1 Base coordinate carrying the entire endpoint predicate
Use the canonical physical Euler-brick labeling
P^2=X^2+Y^2,
Q^2=X^2+Z^2,
D^2=Y^2+Z^2.
On the nondegenerate physical Euler K3 K_c, X is nonzero.
Define the rational base coordinate
w:=D/X.
The endpoint condition is
R^2=X^2+D^2.
Hence on K_c,
(R/X)^2 = 1+w^2.
Put
u:=R/X.
Then the full endpoint cover is obtained by adjoining
u^2=1+w^2.
Therefore, on the dense physical open,
Q(S)
 =
Q(K_c)(sqrt(1+w^2)).
Let
C_base:
u^2=1+w^2.
This conic is rational. With parameter tau,
w=2tau/(1-tau^2),
u=(1+tau^2)/(1-tau^2).
Thus:
### Theorem 28-A — EndpointAsPythagoreanBaseChange
The endpoint surface S is birational on the physical dense open to the normalization of
K_c
 x_{P1_w}
 C_base,
where
C_base -> P1_w
is the degree-two Pythagorean slope map.
Equivalently:
an M3 point is an endpoint
iff
its base value w=D/X lies in the degree-two thin image
phi(P1(Q)),
phi(tau)=2tau/(1-tau^2).
This is an exact Q-defined adapter.
No local independence assumption is used.
### 28.2 Exact fixed-w fiber model
Fix a rational w and scale X=1 on the generic projective fiber.
Then
Y^2=P^2-1,
Z^2=Q^2-1,
while
D^2=Y^2+Z^2=w^2
gives
P^2+Q^2=w^2+2.
Therefore the normalization of the fixed-w M3 fiber is the V4 cover of the conic
C0:
P^2+Q^2=w^2+2
obtained by adjoining
Y=sqrt(P^2-1),
Z=sqrt(Q^2-1).
Over the geometric function field of w:
- P^2-1 has four simple zeros on C0;
- Q^2-1 has four simple zeros on C0;
- the two branch sets are disjoint for w!=0;
- poles have even order and do not add branch.
For rational nonzero w, the exceptional geometric collisions w^2=-1 and w^2=-2 do not occur.
The V4 cover has degree 4.
At each of the eight branch values, two points upstairs ramify with index 2.
Riemann-Hurwitz gives
2g-2
 =
4*(-2)+16
 =
8,
so
g=5.
Hence:
### Theorem 28-B — FixedDXRatioGenusFive
For every nonzero rational physical base value
w=D/X,
the normalized fixed-w M3 fiber is a genus-5 curve.
In particular, by Faltings, each fixed rational w supports only finitely many rational M3 points.
The finiteness constant is not uniform in w and is not used as a population bound.
### 28.3 Endpoint population as thin-base mass
Define
m_w(B)
 :=
# {E in M3(B): D(E)/X(E)=w}.
Then exactly
M3(B)
 =
sum_w m_w(B),
and by Theorem28-A,
P(B)
 =
sum_{w in phi(P1(Q))} m_w(B).
Therefore the M3 goal is exactly
### Receiver F-BASE — PythagoreanThinBaseMass
sum_{w in phi(P1(Q))} m_w(B)
 =
o(
sum_w m_w(B)
).
This is a one-dimensional pushforward problem.
It is weaker in data requirements than full equidistribution of all M3 coordinates:
only the pushforward of the exact physical M3 measure under
rho=[X:D]:K_c-->P1
matters.
### 28.4 Fixed-prime base acceptance is elementary
For an odd prime p define
Omega_p
 :=
{[x:d] in P1(F_p):
 x^2+d^2 is a square in F_p}.
On the affine chart x=1,
sum_{w in F_p} chi_p(1+w^2)=-1.
Including zero square values and the point at infinity gives an acceptance proportion
#Omega_p/(p+1)
 =
1/2+O(1/p).
More explicitly it is
1/2
for p congruent 3 mod 4 and
1/2+1/(p+1)
for p congruent 1 mod 4.
Thus the local thin-base target has an explicit fixed contraction bounded away from 1.
However this is the uniform P1(F_p) measure.
It is NOT automatically the pushforward of physical M3 modulo p.
### 28.5 Strictly weakened METHOD B receiver
The full METHOD B fixed-modulus theorem can now be weakened again.
It is enough to prove, for every fixed finite good-prime set S,
limsup_{B->infinity}
[
# {E in M3(B):
     rho(E) mod p lies in Omega_p for every p in S}
 / M3(B)
]
 <=
product_{p in S} theta_p
with theta_p<1 and a product tending to zero as |S| grows after B->infinity.
No equidistribution of the remaining M3 coordinates is required.
Call this:
### Receiver BF-MIN — FixedFinitePrimeDXPushforwardContraction
This is a scalar pushforward local-to-global theorem on [X:D].
It is formally weaker than a full fixed-modulus M3 residue theorem.
### 28.6 What this does not solve
Ambient thinness of
phi(P1(Q)) subset P1(Q)
does not imply thinness for the M3 pushforward measure.
The usual count of Pythagorean slopes among all rational slopes cannot be imported without an M3 base-height/distribution adapter.
Faltings on each fixed genus-5 fiber is also insufficient because its bound is not uniform in w.
Thus the remaining theorem gate is:
prove that the exact M3 pushforward rho_*mu_{M3,B} does not concentrate on the Pythagorean thin image.
Possible theorem species:
- fixed-finite-prime contraction for rho=[X:D];
- large sieve only on the scalar pushforward;
- quantitative Hilbert irreducibility for this exact M3 pushforward;
- a uniform rational-point theorem for the genus-5 fiber family strong enough to control thin base values.
### 28.7 Routing consequence
The old METHOD F pair-image receiver can be sharpened from the two-coordinate set W_B to the one-dimensional quotient base
w=D/X.
METHOD_F_PAIR_IMAGE_STILL_VALID=true
METHOD_F_THIN_BASE_REDUCTION=true
METHOD_B_FULL_RESIDUE_TRANSFER_OVERSTRONG_FOR_THIS_ROUTE=true
FIXED_D_OVER_X_FIBER_GENUS=5
FIXED_D_OVER_X_RATIONAL_POINTS_FINITE=true
ENDPOINT_IS_DEGREE2_BASE_CHANGE=true
This does not yet select a PRIMARY_METHOD.
But BF-MIN is currently one of the narrowest exact global theorem gates in the ledger because it asks only for one scalar pushforward to avoid one explicit degree-two thin set.
NEXT_ITEM=DXPushforwardContractionArsenalRematch
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false

## 29. Exact reconciliation — the D/X thin-base route is the denominator-side precursor of the Stage35 rank-3 genus-5 fibration
Status: EXACT_ADAPTER_DERIVED.
### 29.1 Coordinate identification
Use Stage29/35 endpoint coordinates
[e:x:y:p:q:z:d]
with equations
e^2+x^2=p^2,
e^2+y^2=q^2,
x^2+y^2=z^2,
e^2+z^2=d^2.
In the canonical primitive Euler-brick labeling choose
e=X,
x=Y,
y=Z,
z=D,
d=R.
Then the Stage35 selected rank-3 quadric
e^2+z^2=d^2
is exactly
X^2+D^2=R^2.
Thus the Section28 base coordinate
w=D/X
is the denominator-side rational function attached to the same rank-3 quadric.
### 29.2 Parameter crosswalk
Stage35 uses
t=(e+d)/z=(X+R)/D.
On the endpoint set put
u=R/X.
Then
t=(1+u)/w,
with
u^2=1+w^2.
Solving the Pythagorean conic gives exactly
w=2t/(t^2-1),
u=(t^2+1)/(t^2-1).
Equivalently, if Section28 uses
w=2tau/(1-tau^2),
u=(1+tau^2)/(1-tau^2),
then
t=1/tau.
Hence the two parameterizations are birationally identical over Q.
### 29.3 Fiber equation crosswalk
Stage35 writes
alpha=((t^2-1)/(t^2+1))^2,
beta=4t^2/(t^2+1)^2,
alpha+beta=1,
and the selected endpoint fiber as
alpha*d^2+x^2=p^2,
beta*d^2-x^2=y^2,
d^2-x^2=q^2.
Under
d=R,
x=Y,
y=Z,
p=P,
q=Q,
the parameter crosswalk gives
alpha*R^2=X^2,
beta*R^2=D^2.
Therefore the three equations become exactly
X^2+Y^2=P^2,
D^2-Y^2=Z^2,
R^2-Y^2=Q^2,
which are the canonical Euler-brick equations together with
D^2=Y^2+Z^2,
R^2=X^2+D^2.
Thus the fixed-w genus-5 fiber of Section28, after the endpoint base change, is exactly the selected Stage35 diagonal genus-5 fiber.
### Theorem 29-A — Stage35ThinBaseFiberAdapter
The Section28 construction
K_c -> P1_w,
w=D/X,
followed by the endpoint base change
u^2=1+w^2
is birationally identical, on the nondegenerate positive physical open, to the Stage35 selected rank-3 genus-5 fibration with parameter
t=(X+R)/D>1.
The crosswalk is
w=2t/(t^2-1),
R/X=(t^2+1)/(t^2-1).
No population, height, field, or multiplicity change is introduced.
### 29.4 Imported Stage35 structure
Because the fiber families are identical, the following Stage35 results transfer as structural input on the endpoint-active D/X base:
- every rational physical t>1 gives a smooth genus-5 fiber;
- the bad-parameter divisor has no physical rational base point;
- the fiber is diagonal;
- its five elliptic quotient Jacobians are explicit over Q(t);
- all five have full rational 2-torsion;
- explicit generic boundary sections are known;
- fixed-fiber covering / elliptic-Chabauty and generic Q(t) Mordell-Weil methods are structurally available.
This is imported structure only.
Stage35 did NOT prove a uniform theorem excluding specialization-new physical points.
Therefore no density credit is added here.
### 29.5 Interpretation of the Stage35 wall in M3 language
Stage35 originally phrases its Class-3 wall as:
uniformly control/exclude specialization-new physical rational points on every rational t>1 fiber.
The Section28 denominator-side interpretation is:
control the exact M3 pushforward mass on the Pythagorean thin subset
w=2t/(t^2-1).
Thus Stage35's theorem wall is one possible strong solution of BF-MIN, but is substantially stronger than BF-MIN itself.
BF-MIN only asks that the total M3 mass of endpoint-active base values be o(M3).
It does NOT require every endpoint-active genus-5 fiber to be empty.
### 29.6 Strategic consequence
This reconciliation prevents two anti-loop errors:
1. do not rediscover the Stage35 genus-5 quotient structure under the new w notation;
2. do not inherit Stage35's pointwise-for-every-t emptiness receiver when the M3 density goal only needs average thin-base mass.
The genuinely weakened target is now:
### Receiver 29-B — AverageStage35FiberMass
With
w(t)=2t/(t^2-1),
prove
sum_{t in Q, t>1}
  m_{w(t)}(B)
=
o(M3(B)),
where duplicate t-values are counted once through w.
Equivalently use one representative of the degree-two conic parameterization.
This is exactly BF-MIN at the rational-point level.
Potential arithmetic use of the five elliptic quotients should therefore target an average/frequency theorem, not pointwise fiber emptiness.
STAGE35_THIN_BASE_ADAPTER=true
STAGE35_FIBER_FAMILY_IDENTICAL=true
STAGE35_POINTWISE_EMPTY_RECEIVER_OVERSTRONG_FOR_M3_DENSITY=true
BF_MIN_AVERAGE_RECEIVER_RETAINED=true
NEW_DENSITY_THEOREM_CREDIT=false
NEXT_ITEM=Stage35QuotientAverageFrequencyScreen
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false

## 30. METHOD B refinement — fixed-delta coupled j=1728 quartic receiver
Status: DERIVED_EXACT_STRUCTURAL_RESULT / NO_DENSITY_CREDIT.
### 30.1 Stage35 average-frequency screen
The Section29 Stage35 quotient rematch does not currently supply an average theorem for the exact M3 weights m_w(B).
The five elliptic quotient Jacobians have genuinely moving j-invariants; fixed-fiber covering/Chabauty, generic Q(t) Mordell-Weil information, and standard twist-average theorems do not control the M3-weighted average required by Receiver 29-B.
Therefore:
STAGE35_QUOTIENT_AVERAGE_DIRECT_CLOSURE=false
STAGE35_FIXED_FIBER_RESEARCH_REPLAY=false
### 30.2 Exact fixed-delta circle reduction
Retain the Section35 primitive normalization
C=c0*c,  B=c0*b,
D=d0*d,  A=d0*a,
and put
U=c*a,  V=d*b.
The Pythagorean-base identity A^2+B^2=2W^2 gives exactly
(U/C)^2 + (V/D)^2
 = 2 (W/(c0*d0))^2.
After dividing by the common square scale lambda=W/(c0*d0), parameterize the rational circle by t:
(U/C)/lambda = (t^2-2t-1)/(t^2+1),
(V/D)/lambda = -(t^2+2t-1)/(t^2+1).
This t is the original Pythagorean base parameter L/M up to the harmless sign/orientation convention.
For the first fixed-delta conic
C^2-D^2 = delta*r^2,
write, on the usual rational chart,
D/C = (1-delta*z^2)/(1+delta*z^2).
The second fixed-delta M3 condition
U^2-V^2 = delta*s^2
then becomes, after removing rational squares,
-delta*A_delta(t,z)*B_delta(t,z) in Q^x2,
where
A_delta(t,z)=2*delta*t*z^2-t^2+1,
B_delta(t,z)=delta*(t^2-1)*z^2-2*t.
Thus the former generic “coupled genus-one core” has an explicit two-factor squareclass model.
### 30.3 Endpoint detector on the same coupled coordinates
Define
P_delta(t,z)
 =(t^2+1)^2*(1+delta^2*z^4)
   -8*delta*t*z^2*(t^2-1).
Then the endpoint condition on this fixed-delta chart is exactly
(1+delta^2*z^4)*P_delta(t,z) in Q^x2.
The M3 and endpoint factors satisfy the exact identities
P_delta - 2*A_delta*B_delta
 =(1+delta*z^2)^2*(t^2-2t-1)^2,
P_delta + 2*A_delta*B_delta
 =(1-delta*z^2)^2*(t^2+2t-1)^2.
Hence the endpoint detector and the M3 genus-one condition are linked by two explicit complete squares; no independence assumption is present.
### 30.4 Physical nonsquare-quartic theorem
Put q=delta*z^2. As a polynomial in t,
P_delta
 =(q^2+1)t^4 - 8q t^3
  +2(q^2+1)t^2 + 8q t +(q^2+1).
Coefficient comparison with a square quadratic shows that P_delta can be a square polynomial only if
q*(q^2-1)^2=0.
The cases q=0,+1,-1 are degenerate or boundary charts (zero/pole of the first conic) and do not meet the nondegenerate positive physical open.
Therefore:
### Theorem 30-A — FixedDeltaEndpointQuarticNonSquare
For every nondegenerate positive physical fixed-(delta,z) chart, P_delta(t,z) is a genuine nonsquare quartic in t.
### 30.5 Inert-prime Fourier structure
For
F_delta(x,y)=x^4+delta^2*y^4
and every odd p congruent 3 mod 4 with p not dividing delta,
sum_[x:y] chi_p(F_delta(x,y)) = 0.
Equivalently the complete affine two-variable trace is zero.
For (h,k) != (0,0), projective-line Fourier decomposition gives exactly
sum_{x,y mod p}
 chi_p(F_delta(x,y))*e_p(hx+ky)
 = p*chi_p(k^4+delta^2*h^4),
while the zero Fourier mode is 0.
Thus the Stage14-4bv flat inert-prime Fourier mechanism has an exact algebraic analogue on each fixed-delta plus quartic. This is structural reuse only: the M3 genus-one selector must still be retained.
### 30.6 Exact physical height bridge to the t-parameter
Section34 gives the primitive face diagonal
b1 = 2*C*D*W/(epsilon*c0*d0)
   = 2*W*c*d/epsilon.
Since every physical face diagonal is <= R_phys <= B,
W*c*d <= (epsilon/2) B.
Because t=L/M and W=L^2+M^2,
H(t)^2 is comparable to W,
so
H(t) << sqrt(B/(c*d)).
This is an exact entry point for a fixed-(delta,z) degree-four endpoint count, but no theorem currently shows that the small-c*d sector is M3-negligible.
### 30.7 Current boundary
The local Fourier analysis is no longer the principal obstacle. The remaining exact gate is:
CoupledDeltaQuarticPhysicalPacketization:
retain
  - primitive/canonical one-object count,
  - exact R_phys<=B,
  - the M3 condition -delta*A_delta*B_delta square,
  - cross-gcd labels,
while exploiting the nonsquare endpoint quartic P_delta(t,z) and its inert-prime cancellation.
Dropping the M3 coupling and counting ambient (t,z) points is too weak and receives no density credit.

## 31. METHOD B refinement — exact M3-fiber endpoint cover and balanced cross-gcd packets
Status: STRUCTURAL_PROGRESS / NO_NEW_LOCAL_DENSITY_CREDIT / NO_RATIO_THEOREM.
### 31.1 Exact M3 genus-one fiber and endpoint double cover
Keep Section30 notation
A_delta(t,z)=2*delta*t*z^2-t^2+1,
B_delta(t,z)=delta*(t^2-1)*z^2-2*t,
q=delta*z^2,
and define the fixed-(delta,z) M3 curve
C_{delta,z}: s^2 = -delta*A_delta(t,z)*B_delta(t,z).
For q not equal to 0,+1,-1 this is generically a smooth genus-one curve.
The endpoint detector is
F_{delta,z}(t)
 =(1+delta^2*z^4)*P_delta(t,z).
The previous polynomial calculation gives
disc_t(P_delta)
 = 2^16*q^2*(q-1)^4*(q+1)^4.
Moreover
Res_t(P_delta,A_delta)
 = Res_t(P_delta,B_delta)
 = 16*(q-1)^4*(q+1)^4,
while A_delta*B_delta is squarefree unless q^2=-1 or q=+/-1.
In a quadratic extension K(t)(sqrt(H)), an element a in K(t)^* can become a square only if a is already a square in K(t), or a/H is a square in K(t).
Therefore for every odd prime p satisfying
p == 3 mod 4,
p does not divide 2*delta,
q mod p not in {0,+1,-1},
the endpoint function F_{delta,z}(t) is nonsquare in the function field of the smooth reduction of C_{delta,z}. The possible repeated-root case q^2=-1 is automatically impossible for p==3 mod 4.
Thus the endpoint double cover
D_{delta,z}: v^2=F_{delta,z}(t)
over C_{delta,z}
is nontrivial at every such good inert prime.
### 31.2 Genus-five compatibility
For physical q not in {0,+1,-1}, P_delta has four simple roots in t and none is a root of A_delta*B_delta. Each simple zero therefore lifts to two simple branch points on the genus-one M3 curve. The poles at the two quartic points at infinity have even order.
Hence the endpoint double cover has eight branch points and Riemann-Hurwitz gives
g(D_{delta,z}) = 2*1-1+8/2 = 5.
This exactly matches the Stage35 fixed-w genus-five geometry after the Section29 base crosswalk.
### 31.3 Same-M3-fiber finite-field contraction
For every good inert prime as above, Weil on the nontrivial degree-two cover gives
sum_{P in C_{delta,z}(F_p)}
 chi_p(F_{delta,z}(P))
 = O(sqrt(p)),
with an absolute constant depending only on the fixed genera/degrees.
Equivalently, on each smooth fixed-(delta,z) M3 fiber,
Pr(endpoint local square | M3 fiber mod p)
 = 1/2 + O(p^(-1/2)).
IMPORTANT CREDIT FIREWALL:
Section6 already proved the local conditional law for M3. Therefore this subsection receives no new local-density theorem credit. Its value is an explicit realization of that law on the Section35 delta/genus-one packets, with the exact genus-five endpoint cover and an explicit good-prime certificate.
### 31.4 Cross-gcd root-line packetization on the first delta conic
Recall
c0=gcd(C,B),
d0=gcd(D,A),
gcd(C,D)=1,
so gcd(c0,d0)=1 and
c0|C,
d0|D.
On the fixed-delta conic
C=f^2+delta*e^2,
D=f^2-delta*e^2
(up to the standard primitive common scaling), every odd prime power ell^a dividing c0 with ell not dividing 2*delta imposes one of at most two root lines
f == rho*e mod ell^a,
rho^2 == -delta mod ell^a.
Similarly ell^a|d0 gives at most two root lines with
rho^2 == +delta mod ell^a.
If ell|delta, primitivity forces the corresponding congruence to a single coordinate line at the odd prime; it does not increase the root multiplicity.
Hence for k=c0*d0 the primitive (e,f) states lie on k^o(1) homogeneous root-line lattices modulo k.
If two distinct primitive integer pairs on the same root line lie in |e|,|f|<=Z, then
k | (e1*f2-e2*f1),
and the determinant is nonzero because distinct primitive pairs cannot be collinear. Farey/determinant spacing therefore gives
# primitive pairs on one root line
 << Z^2/k + 1.
Thus, up to k^o(1),
# first-conic states in a balanced parameter box
 << Z^2/k + 1.
### 31.5 Balanced physical-height consequence and exact limitation
The primitive face diagonal is
b1 = 2*W*C*D/(epsilon*c0*d0),
so R_phys<=B implies
W*C*D/k << B.
On a balanced dyadic first-conic packet C~D~Z^2 this gives
Z^4 << k*B/W.
Combining with the root-line spacing gives
N_balanced(delta,c0,d0,W)
 << B^o(1) * ( sqrt(B/(W*k)) + 1 ).
Thus on balanced C,D packets, a large cross-gcd is not a loss: in the main term it gives an additional k^(-1/2) saving.
This statement is NOT extended to all conic packets. If C<<D or D<<C, the product bound C*D alone does not force e and f into a balanced square box. No existing retained anti-concentration theorem was found that discards the extreme C/D branch in the exact Section34 coordinates.
Therefore:
DELTA_M3_FIBER_GENUS_ONE_EXPLICIT=true
DELTA_ENDPOINT_COVER_GENUS_FIVE=true

## 32. METHOD B refinement — fixed CM twist quotient inside the delta genus-five fiber
Status: NEW_STRUCTURAL_COMPRESSION / NO_RATIO_CREDIT.
### 32.1 Genus-three V4 symmetry
Inside the fixed-(delta,z) genus-five endpoint fiber, write q=delta*z^2 and retain
H_q(t)=A_delta(t,z)B_delta(t,z),
P_q(t)
 =(q^2+1)t^4-8qt^3+2(q^2+1)t^2+8qt+(q^2+1).
The simultaneous M3+endpoint condition gives the genus-three quotient
W^2 = Lambda_q * H_q(t) * P_q(t),
where
Lambda_q = -delta*(1+q^2)
up to rational squares.
The degree-eight polynomial H_q P_q is invariant, up to an eighth power, under
sigma: t -> -1/t,
and also under
tau: t -> (1-t)/(1+t).
These commute; the genus-three curve has a V4 of base involutions.
### 32.2 The sigma quotient is a fixed j=1728 twist family
Set
x=t-1/t.
Then exactly
H_q(t)/t^2
 = -q*x^2 + 2(q^2+1)x - 4q,
P_q(t)/t^2
 = (q^2+1)(x^2+4)-8qx.
Hence the sigma quotient is
Y^2
 = Lambda_q
   [-q*x^2+2(q^2+1)x-4q]
   [(q^2+1)(x^2+4)-8qx].
The untwisted binary quartic factors as
-(x-2q)(q*x-2)
 ((q^2+1)x^2-8qx+4(q^2+1)).
Its classical quartic invariants are
I=-48(q-1)^4(q+1)^4,
J=0.
Therefore the quotient has elliptic j-invariant 1728 for every q not equal to +/-1.
A standard Jacobian model is
Y^2
 = X^3
   +1296*Lambda_q^2*(q^2-1)^4*X.
After rational scaling by 3(q^2-1), this is Q-isomorphic to
E_Lambda:
 y^2=x^3+16*Lambda_q^2*x,
equivalently to the Stage14 plus-twist normalization
E_n:
 y^2=x^3+4*n^2*x
with squarefree twist parameter
n = sf_Q(2*Lambda_q).
### 32.3 Exact twist parameter in original first-conic variables
Write the first delta conic as
C=f^2+delta*e^2,
D=f^2-delta*e^2,
so
q=delta*e^2/f^2.
Then
2*delta*(1+q^2)
 = delta*(C^2+D^2)/f^4.
Since f^4 is a rational square,
n
 = sf_Q(delta*(C^2+D^2)).
Using
C^2-D^2=delta*r^2,
this becomes the especially simple exact formula
boxed:
n = sf_Q((C^2-D^2)(C^2+D^2))
  = sf_Q(C^4-D^4).
Thus the CM twist parameter depends only on the first-conic coordinate pair (C,D), not on the moving endpoint t.
### 32.4 The first conic already supplies a point on the same Stage14 torsor
Orient the nondegenerate pair so 0<D/C<1. Since
C^4-D^4 = n*s^2
for a rational s after removing the square kernel, put
u=D/C,
y=s/C^2.
Then
boxed:
n*y^2 = 1-u^4.
This is exactly the Stage14-s7 torsor
C_{k,xi}: k*y^2=1-xi^2*z^4
with
(k,xi)=(n,1).
Its Jacobian is exactly
E_n: Y^2=X^3+4n^2X.
Therefore every nondegenerate first-delta-conic packet already carries a canonical rational point P1 on the same CM twist E_n.
If n=1, then C^4-D^4 is a rational square. The classical Fermat quartic descent excludes nonzero 0<D<C solutions. Hence every nondegenerate physical packet has n>1.
For squarefree n>1, the merged Stage14-s7-06 torsion theorem gives
E_n(Q)_tors ~= Z/2Z.
Thus the first-conic point is a genuine nonboundary point on a positive-rank plus twist. Consequently:
M3_packet_implies_CM_twist_rank_positive=true.
This means endpoint -> rank(E_n)>=1 is NOT new endpoint credit.
### 32.5 What endpoint adds
The endpoint genus-five point maps through the sigma quotient to another rational point P2 on the same E_n.
The quotient point is nonbranch on the physical open because both the M3 and endpoint square roots are nonzero there.
Therefore endpoint produces a second explicit rational point on the same one-dimensional squarefree CM twist family.
However the following are NOT yet proved:
- P2 differs from P1 by a non-torsion point;
- P1 and P2 are independent in E_n(Q);
- their images in E_n(Q)/2E_n(Q) are distinct;
- rank E_n(Q)>=2;
- the endpoint point lies in the same canonical Stage14 (n,1) quartic coordinate chart with a positive ordered second coordinate.
Any of these would be a materially stronger endpoint receiver. Without one of them, the twist reduction alone does not thin M3 because M3 already forces rank-positive E_n packets.
### 32.6 The other two genus-three elliptic quotients
For tau:t->(1-t)/(1+t), the invariant coordinate
u=(t^2+1)/(t+1)
gives a quartic elliptic quotient whose invariants contain the moving factors
I_tau
 =4(q-1)^4
  (q^4-28q^3+6q^2-28q+1),
J_tau
 =16(q-1)^8
  (q^4+68q^3+6q^2+68q+1).
For sigma*tau the analogous invariants are obtained by q->-q.
Thus only one of the three elliptic factors of the genus-three piece is fixed-j=1728; the other two genuinely move with q.
This does not contradict the Stage35 moving-j ledger.
DELTA_CM_SIGMA_QUOTIENT=true

## 33. Endpoint CM-support synchronization and the second restricted quartic
Status: NEW_ENDPOINT_NECESSARY_CONDITION / FIXED_PACKET_COUNT_AVAILABLE / GLOBAL_AGGREGATION_OPEN.
This section refines Section32 without changing its conclusion that M3 already supplies the first rank-positive point on the CM twist.
### 33.1 First-conic notation and a second squarefree core
Keep
C=f^2+delta*e^2,
D=f^2-delta*e^2,
with gcd(e,f)=1 on the primitive first-conic chart, and put
m:=f^4+delta^2*e^4=(C^2+D^2)/2.
For every odd prime p|delta, primitive gcd(C,D)=1 forces p not dividing f. Hence
gcd(delta,m)_odd=1.
Let
eta:=sf(m)>0
and write, on the endpoint locus,
m=eta*r^2.
The odd primes p=3 mod 4 cannot occur in eta: if such p divides f^4+delta^2 e^4 and p does not divide delta, the sum-of-two-squares property forces p|f and p|e; if p|delta, its contribution to m has even valuation. Thus eta_odd is supported on split primes.
### 33.2 Integral endpoint quadratics
Write
q=delta*e^2/f^2=a/b,
where the primitive conditions give
a=delta*e^2,
b=f^2,
gcd(a,b)=1,
and m=a^2+b^2.
Let
x=t-1/t=X/Y
in primitive projective coordinates. Since t=L/M with gcd(L,M)=1 and opposite parity,
X=L^2-M^2=P,
Y=L*M=Q/2,
gcd(X,Y)=1.
Clearing the square denominators from the two Section31 square conditions gives
-delta*H_{a,b}(X,Y) is a square,
m*J_{a,b}(X,Y) is a square,
where
H_{a,b}(X,Y)
 =-a*b*X^2+2*m*X*Y-4*a*b*Y^2,
J_{a,b}(X,Y)
 =m*X^2-8*a*b*X*Y+4*m*Y^2.
The two binary quadratics satisfy
Res(H_{a,b},J_{a,b})
 =16*(a-b)^4*(a+b)^4.
### 33.3 Endpoint forces the second core onto the Pythagorean support
Let p be an odd prime with p|eta. Then p|m, while p does not divide a*b. Reducing the endpoint equation
m*J_{a,b}(X,Y)=square
modulo p gives
J_{a,b}(X,Y)
 == -8*a*b*X*Y mod p.
Because v_p(m) is odd, square parity forces p|J_{a,b}(X,Y), hence
p|X*Y.
Therefore
boxed:
eta_odd | X*Y | P*Q.
Equivalently,
eta_odd | rad(PQ).
Together with the already-retained M3 condition
delta_odd | rad(PQ)
and gcd(delta,m)_odd=1, this yields the new endpoint-only CM-support synchronization
boxed:
endpoint
 =>
 n_odd | rad(PQ),
where Section32 uses
n=sf_Q(C^4-D^4)
 =sf_Q(2*delta*m).
This condition is not valid for arbitrary M3 packets and therefore is genuine endpoint information.
### 33.4 Second restricted squareclass quartic
The same result may be written as the exact endpoint necessary condition
boxed:
f^4+delta^2*e^4=eta*r^2,
eta_odd | rad(PQ).
For one fixed Pythagorean base (L,M), both
delta_odd | rad(PQ),
eta_odd   | rad(PQ)
have only
2^omega(PQ)=(PQ)^o(1)
possible odd supports. Sign and 2-adic choices cost only a bounded factor.
Thus the Stage15-6aq obstruction “the moving core has polynomially many possible values” does not transfer to this fixed-base endpoint receiver.
### 33.5 Exact Stage15-6ao degree-four adapter
Fix nonzero delta, positive squarefree eta, and one Gaussian orientation K=A+iB with N(K)=eta, with the bounded 2-adic convention separated.
From
f^4+delta^2 e^4=eta r^2
and the primitive coprimality, Gaussian factorization gives, up to units/conjugation,
f^2+i*delta*e^2
 = K*(u+i*v)^2.
Hence the retained point lies on the ordinary P3 intersection of two quadrics
A*(u^2-v^2)-2*B*u*v-f^2=0,
B*(u^2-v^2)+2*A*u*v-delta*e^2=0.
For every nondegenerate physical packet this is the standard smooth geometrically integral degree-four genus-one model of the j=1728 quartic.
Since
u^2+v^2=r,
one has
|u|,|v| << r^(1/2),
|f| << eta^(1/4)*r^(1/2),
|e| << eta^(1/4)*r^(1/2)
(the last bound is only weakened by dropping |delta|^(-1/2)<=1).
Thus on a dyadic block r~R the projective height satisfies
H << eta^(1/4)*R^(1/2).
Heath-Brown's uniform degree-four projective-curve theorem, exactly as used in Stage15-6ao, therefore gives
boxed:
N_{delta,eta}(R)
 <<_epsilon
 B^epsilon * eta^(1/8) * R^(1/4).
The implied theorem constant depends only on epsilon and degree 4, not on the coefficients delta or eta.
This is a fixed-packet upper theorem. It does not by itself sum the moving Pythagorean bases.
### 33.6 Relation to Section32 CM second-point route
Section32 showed:
M3 packet
 -> first rational point P1 on E_n,
endpoint
 -> second rational point P2 on the same E_n.
The present section extracts an arithmetic consequence of the second-point condition that does not require proving P1,P2 independence:
endpoint
 -> eta_odd | rad(PQ)
 -> n_odd | rad(PQ).
Therefore the next quantitative test should compare two possible endpoint receivers:
A. CM second-point independence / Selmer separation;
B. CM twist-support synchronization n_odd|rad(PQ) together with the uniform second-quartic degree-four count.
No independence between these two savings may be assumed. They are alternative or jointly-audited mechanisms only.

## 33. METHOD B refinement — exact xi=1 two-point CM-twist adapter
Status: EXACT_RECEIVER / SAME-MEASURE_CLOSURE_NOT_PROVED.
### 33.1 Correct simultaneous orientation
The two fixed-delta difference equations have the same sign. By simultaneously orienting the two pairs, write
0<z1=min(C,D)/max(C,D)<1,
0<z2=min(U,V)/max(U,V)<1,
where
U=c*a,
V=d*b.
With
n=sf_Q(|C^4-D^4|),
the first conic gives
n*y1^2 = 1-z1^4.
The sigma CM quotient of the endpoint point, pulled back through the same j=1728 model, gives
n*y2^2 = 1-z2^4.
Thus an endpoint object produces two positive rational points on the exact Stage14-s7 torsor
C_{n,1}: n*y^2=1-z^4.
### 33.2 Off-diagonal and torsion exclusion
If z1=z2, then the Section30 circle parametrization forces the Pythagorean base parameter t=1, which is a boundary degeneration and is excluded from the physical open.
Hence z1 and z2 are distinct.
Merged Stage14-s7-06 proves for squarefree n>1 that
E_n(Q)_tors ~= Z/2Z,
and on C_{n,1} the unique nontrivial rational torsion self-correspondence changes the sign of the positive quartic coordinate.
Since z1,z2 are distinct and both positive,
P2-P1 is non-torsion on
E_n: Y^2=X^3+4n^2X.
This is an exact off-diagonal two-point CM-twist incidence.
### 33.3 Exact denominator-product bridge
Let Q1 be the denominator of z1 in lowest terms. Since gcd(C,D)=1,
Q1=max(C,D).
Let g2=gcd(U,V), and let Q2 be the denominator of z2 in lowest terms. Then
Q2=max(U,V)/g2.
The primitive physical height identity is
R_phys^2
 =(C^2+D^2)(U^2+V^2)/epsilon^2.
Therefore
Q1*Q2
 <= max(C,D)*max(U,V)/g2
 <= epsilon*R_phys
 <= epsilon*B.
Hence
boxed:
Q1*Q2 << B.
This is a natural multiplicative height directly on the two quartic coordinates z1,z2.
### 33.4 Multiplicity back to M3
The first ratio z1 determines the first-conic shape q=delta*z^2 up to the bounded simultaneous-orientation choice, hence determines delta and the rational first-conic parameter up to bounded ambiguity.
Given z1,z2, the Section30 circle relation determines the Pythagorean base parameter t by a quadratic equation, hence with O(1) ambiguity.
The retained fixed-t M3 fiber theorem then gives B^o(1) physical multiplicity.
Therefore the endpoint count admits the safe receiver
P(B)
 << B^o(1)
    * #{(n,z1,z2):
        z1,z2 distinct positive rational points on C_{n,1},
        den(z1)*den(z2)<<B}.
No converse is claimed.
### 33.5 2-isogeny descent does NOT separate the two points
Using the rational 2-torsion cubic model, the standard 2-isogeny Kummer map is
alpha(P)=[x(P)] in Q^*/Q^*2.
For the first-conic point one obtains
alpha(P1)=[1+q^2].
For the endpoint CM-quotient point, the M3 condition -delta*A_delta*B_delta=square gives the same class:
alpha(P2)=[1+q^2].
Thus
boxed:
alpha(P1)=alpha(P2).
The tempting claim that their classes differ by [-1] is false.
Consequences:
- simple 2-isogeny descent does not prove rank E_n>=2;
- no Selmer-dimension gain is credited;
- the genuine new information is off-diagonal two-point incidence inside one fixed descent class.
### 33.6 Relation to Stage14-s7 and direct-reuse boundary
This is exactly the xi=1 specialization of the Stage14-s7 torsor species
C_{k,xi}: k*y^2=1-xi^2*z^4.
However Stage14's physical reduced coordinate is u=xi*z^2. For xi=1, a quartic-coordinate denominator Q becomes denominator Q^2 in the Stage14 u-coordinate.
Thus the present bound
den(z1)*den(z2)<<B
corresponds only to the coarse Stage14 multiplicative-height bound
H_mult<<B^2.
Importing the current Stage14 whole-family exponent 13/14 at B^2 gives only B^(13/7+o(1)), which is useless against the M3 denominator B^(1/3).
Therefore the Stage14 theorem stack is structural support, not a direct M3 closure theorem.
### 33.7 External-literature screen
Current small-point literature for quadratic twists controls one lowest point, average rank in small-point subfamilies, or constructs many rank>=2 twists. No checked theorem gives the required upper bound for the exact two-point, product-height, xi=1 family above with enough strength for o(B^(1/3)).
Accordingly this route is retained as a sharply defined new receiver, but not promoted to theorem credit.

## 34. CM two-point audit, automorphism closure, and fixed-q occupied-(X,D) compression
Status: STRUCTURAL_PROGRESS / ROUTE_CORRECTION / NO_RATIO_THEOREM.
### 34.1 Circle compatibility inside the xi=1 two-point receiver
Keep the exact Section33 two-point CM torsor
C_{n,1}: n*y^2 = 1-z^4
with two distinct positive rational points z1,z2 and
den(z1)*den(z2) << B.
The Section30 circle parameter t gives the exact ratio
h := z2/z1 = -(t^2+2*t-1)/(t^2-2*t-1).
Solving the quadratic relation back for t shows that rational t exists exactly when
2*(1+h^2) is a rational square.
Equivalently every physical endpoint pair satisfies
boxed:
2*(z1^2+z2^2) in Q^x2.
This is an additional M3-specific relation not present in the unrestricted Stage14 two-point family.
If t=L/M with gcd(L,M)=1 and opposite parity, then with
A=L^2-2LM-M^2,
Bplus=L^2+2LM-M^2,
one has gcd(A,Bplus)=1 and
A^2+Bplus^2=2*(L^2+M^2)^2.
Writing z1=P/Q and z2=R/S in lowest terms, the circle-compatible pair has the divisor packetization
PS = k*|A|,
RQ = k*|Bplus|
for an integer k, together with the physical height consequence
k*(L^2+M^2) << B.
Thus the physical xi=1 pair is much thinner than an ambient pair of rational points.
### 34.2 The second collision curve has moving j
Fix h=z2/z1. Equality of the two quartic squareclasses gives
v^2=(1-z^4)*(1-h^4*z^4).
With x=z^2 this is the elliptic curve
v^2=(1-x^2)*(1-h^4*x^2),
whose j-invariant is
j(h)=16*(1+14*h^4+h^8)^3/[h^4*(1-h^4)^4].
Even after imposing the circle condition 2*(1+h^2)=square, this j-invariant genuinely moves.
Therefore the xi=1 second-point problem does not collapse to a second fixed-CM family.
DELTA_CM_SECOND_FIXED_CM_COLLAPSE=false.
### 34.3 Prime-by-prime dependence of the second-point restriction
The circle ratio satisfies the exact identities
h^2-1
 = 8*t*(t-1)*(t+1)/(t^2-2*t-1)^2,
h^4-1
 = 16*t*(t-1)*(t+1)*(1+t^2)^2/(t^2-2*t-1)^4.
For an odd prime p in the endpoint support synchronization, the base restriction forces
t == 0, infinity, +1, or -1 mod p,
hence automatically
h^4 == 1 mod p.
If p|n, the first torsor point already gives
z1^4 == 1 mod p.
Since z2=h*z1, one then obtains automatically
z2^4 == 1 mod p.
Thus the second point does not provide an independent local p-restriction after the base-support and first-point restrictions have already been charged.
boxed:
base support + first point => second-point local restriction.
Consequently no cubic-in-n saving may be credited by multiplying three local restrictions.
DELTA_CM_SUPPORT_POINT_LOCAL_DEPENDENCE=true
DELTA_CM_SECOND_POINT_MOD_N_RESTRICTION_REDUNDANT=true
DELTA_CM_THREE_LOCAL_SAVINGS_INDEPENDENT=false
DELTA_CM_N_CUBIC_SAVING_AVAILABLE=false.
The natural small-n / large-n split based only on fixed-twist bounded-point multiplicity and support synchronization again meets a square-root-scale B^(1/2+o(1)) barrier. Therefore the CM-support route is not promoted to a primary closure mechanism.
### 34.4 V4 automorphisms do not mix endpoint states
For the fixed-(delta,z) M3 quartic write
H_q(t)=A_delta(t,z)*B_delta(t,z)
and retain the endpoint quartic P_q(t).
For the three known base involutions
sigma:t -> -1/t,
tau:t -> (1-t)/(1+t),
theta:t -> (t+1)/(t-1),
exact symbolic calculation gives
H_q(sigma(t)) = t^(-4)*H_q(t),
P_q(sigma(t)) = t^(-4)*P_q(t),
H_q(tau(t)) = 4/(1+t)^4 * H_q(t),
P_q(tau(t)) = 4/(1+t)^4 * P_q(t),
H_q(theta(t)) = 4/(t-1)^4 * H_q(t),
P_q(theta(t)) = 4/(t-1)^4 * P_q(t).
Thus both M3 and endpoint squareclasses are invariant under every V4 orbit.
The M3 quartic itself has
j_H(q)
 =
64*(3+q^2)^3*(1+3*q^2)^3
/
[(q^2-1)^4*(1+q^2)^2],
and
j_H(q)-1728
 =
256*q^2*(3-2q+3q^2)^2*(3+2q+3q^2)^2
/
[(q^2-1)^4*(1+q^2)^2].
On the nondegenerate physical rational open q!=0,+/-1 there is no rational j=0 or j=1728 exceptional enlargement. Hence the generic branch-set Möbius automorphism group is the Klein V4 already displayed.
Therefore finite automorphism pairing cannot produce a half-density endpoint contraction.
DELTA_M3_QUARTIC_BASE_AUTOMORPHISM_GROUP=V4
DELTA_ENDPOINT_SELECTOR_V4_INVARIANT=true
DELTA_AUTOMORPHISM_MIXING_AVAILABLE=false.
### 34.5 Third-Pythagorean-triple compression of the fixed-q problem
In the exact Master-Hit coverage let
(U1,V1,W1),
(U2,V2,W2)
be the two primitive Pythagorean face triples, with
g=gcd(U1,U2).
The M3 condition is exactly the existence of a third Pythagorean triple:
(V1*U2)^2 + (U1*V2)^2 = K^2.
In physical coordinates
X=U1*U2/g,
D=K/g.
The endpoint condition becomes
(U1*U2)^2 + K^2 = square,
equivalently
boxed:
X^2+D^2 is a square.
Hence, after conditioning on exact physical M3, the fixed-prime endpoint selector factors through the projective occupied coordinate
[X:D] in P1(F_p).
This identifies the fixed-q form of METHOD B with the occupied-(X,D) incidence form of METHOD F.
### 34.6 Exact inert-prime projective half-set
For an odd prime p define
Omega_p
 :=
{[x:d] in P1(F_p): x^2+d^2 is a square in F_p},
with the usual projective convention and zero included as a square local value.
Using
sum_{w in F_p} chi_p(1+w^2)=-1,
one obtains exactly
|Omega_p|=(p+2+chi_p(-1))/2.
In particular, for every inert prime
p == 3 mod 4,
boxed:
|Omega_p|=(p+1)/2.
Thus under uniform projective residue measure the endpoint local necessary condition has exact acceptance 1/2 at every inert prime.
This does not by itself prove that the exact physical M3 population is uniformly distributed on [X:D].
### 34.7 New minimal fixed-q receiver
Let S be any fixed finite set of sufficiently large inert primes and put
Q=product_{p in S} p.
The new minimal fixed-q target is the joint M3 pushforward
E |-> ([X:D]_p)_{p in S}
     in product_{p in S} P1(F_p).
Every endpoint lies in
product_{p in S} Omega_p.
Therefore it is terminally sufficient to prove that for arbitrarily large fixed sets S,
limsup_{B->infinity}
 #{E in M3(B): [X:D]_p in Omega_p for all p in S}
 /
 M3(B)
 <= rho^|S|
for one constant rho<1.
Full equidistribution, which would give the ideal factor 2^(-|S|), is stronger than necessary.
The already-proved B^o(1) multiplicity for fixed (X,D) does not imply this contraction: the occupied image W_B could in principle concentrate in endpoint-active residue classes. A genuine residue-expansion/equidistribution statement for the occupied M3 image is still required.

## 35. Fixed-q theorem-gate audit, accumulating-curve threshold, and terminal Pythagorean allocation
Status: ROUTE_AUDIT / NEW EXACT IDENTITY / NO_RATIO_THEOREM.
### 35.1 FixedQOccupiedXDResidueExpansion is a genuine same-measure theorem gate
Section34 reduced the fixed-finite-prime problem to the occupied projective image
E |-> [X:D] mod p
inside exact primitive/canonical M3(B).
The relevant endpoint-active set is exactly half of P1(F_p) for every inert prime p==3 mod 4.
A repository-wide StructureRadar rematch against the closest existing mechanisms
SR-STR-161, SR-STR-165, SR-STR-166, SR-STR-169, and SR-STR-173
does not supply the missing theorem.
Those assets provide finite Fourier/Gauss architecture, large-sieve templates, charged-once decompositions, and same-measure support/moment firewalls, but their audited endpoints still require an actual fixed-power same-physical-measure correlation or residue-expansion theorem.
Therefore no existing repo theorem proves
limsup_{B->infinity}
 #{E in M3(B): [X:D]_p in Omega_p for all p in S}
 /
 M3(B)
 <= rho^|S|
for arbitrarily large fixed inert-prime sets S and one rho<1.
This route is frozen against further local-algebra subdivision.
FIXED_Q_OCCUPIED_XD_RESIDUE_EXPANSION_STATUS=THEOREM_GATE_PAUSED
FIXED_Q_LOCAL_REANALYSIS_STOPPED=true
FIXED_Q_REOPEN_TRIGGER=new_same_M3_residue_expansion_or_equidistribution_theorem.
### 35.2 METHOD D summability threshold
For every fixed integral degree-6 curve C on the denominator K3 K_c, the retained theorem gives a non-split endpoint lift of degree 12 and hence
N_endpoint,C(B) << B^(1/6+o(1)).
Since M3(B)>>B^(1/3), any union of
B^(1/6-delta)
such fixed curves, for one fixed delta>0, contributes only
B^(1/3-delta+o(1))
 = o(M3(B)).
Thus a sufficient accumulating-family theorem is:
# relevant endpoint-carrying degree-6 denominator curves of physical height <=B
 << B^(1/6-delta).
However the retained Testa-Stoll classification is on low-degree curves of the endpoint surface, not a summable classification of all denominator-side degree-6 curves on K_c. It also does not control the residual Zariski-dense surface population.
Recent determinant-method curve bounds sharpen individual high-degree curve counts but do not provide either missing global layer: a sufficiently small family count or a residual two-dimensional point bound in the exact physical height.
Therefore METHOD D remains a genuine family-plus-residual theorem gate.

### 35.3 Exact terminal Pythagorean allocation
Retain the exact endpoint factorization
(PQ-YZ)(PQ+YZ)=X^2*R^2
and put
G=gcd(PQ,YZ),
L_-=(PQ-YZ)/G,
L_+=(PQ+YZ)/G.
On the endpoint locus,
L_-=u^2,
L_+=v^2
with u,v positive odd coprime.
Define uniquely
alpha=(u+v)/2,
beta=(v-u)/2
after orienting v>u>0. Then gcd(alpha,beta)=1 and alpha,beta have opposite parity.
The terminal factorization becomes exactly
boxed:
PQ = G*(alpha^2+beta^2),
YZ = 2*G*alpha*beta,
XR = G*(alpha^2-beta^2).
Equivalently
(XR/G, YZ/G, PQ/G)
is the primitive Pythagorean triple generated by (alpha,beta).
This is a canonical endpoint-only allocation attached to every physical endpoint.
TERMINAL_PRODUCT_PYTHAGOREAN_ALLOCATION=true
TERMINAL_ALPHA_BETA_UNIQUE=true.
### 35.4 Limitation of the terminal allocation route
The three displayed product identities alone leave divisor allocations
PQ -> (P,Q),
YZ -> (Y,Z),
together with the original shared-X face equations.
No new independent squareclass or congruence restriction has yet been derived from the alpha,beta allocation beyond restrictions already encoded by the endpoint/M3 system.
Therefore the terminal allocation is retained as exact structural input but receives no new counting saving.
TERMINAL_PRODUCT_ALLOCATION_NEW_SAVING=false.
### 35.5 Current route verdict
The three most direct continuations tested here have the following status:
A. fixed-q occupied-(X,D) residue contraction:
   exact terminal receiver, but same-M3 residue-expansion theorem missing;
B. accumulating-curve summation:
   fixed-curve theorem strong enough, but family count and residual surface control missing;
C. terminal product Pythagorean allocation:
   exact new parametrization, but no independent saving yet.
None is promoted to ratio theorem credit.

## 36. Primitive Master endpoint cross-gcd compression
Status: NEW EXACT ENDPOINT STRUCTURE / LARGE-C LATTICE SAVING CANDIDATE / GLOBAL COUNT OPEN.
### 36.1 Scale-free endpoint equations in primitive Master triples
Let the two primitive Pythagorean triples in the exact Master-Hit coverage be
(U1,V1,W1),
(U2,V2,W2),
and put
g=gcd(U1,U2).
Then
X=U1*U2/g,
Y=V1*U2/g,
Z=U1*V2/g,
while the two face hypotenuses are
P=(U2/g)*W1,
Q=(U1/g)*W2.
Therefore
PQ=(X/g)*W1*W2,
YZ=(X/g)*V1*V2.
Define the endpoint cross-gcd
c:=gcd(W1*W2,V1*V2).
Since the terminal gcd is G=gcd(PQ,YZ), exactly
G=(X/g)*c.
Substituting this into the Section35 terminal Pythagorean allocation gives the scale-free endpoint system
boxed:
W1*W2 = c*(alpha^2+beta^2),
V1*V2 = 2*c*alpha*beta,
g*R = c*(alpha^2-beta^2),
where alpha>beta>0 are coprime and of opposite parity.
Thus the endpoint condition says that, after removing the cross-gcd c,
(V1*V2/c, g*R/c, W1*W2/c)
is a primitive Pythagorean triple.
This removes the physical common factor X from the terminal allocation.
### 36.2 Exact factorization of the cross-gcd
Each primitive Pythagorean triple has pairwise coprime U_i,V_i,W_i.
Hence any odd prime dividing c must divide one W_i and the opposite V_j; it cannot divide a same-index pair.
Consequently
boxed:
c = gcd(W1,V2)*gcd(W2,V1),
and the two displayed factors are coprime.
Write
c12=gcd(W1,V2),
c21=gcd(W2,V1).
Then gcd(c12,c21)=1.
### 36.3 Prime-power double-lattice structure
Write the Euclid parameters as
U1=r^2-s^2, V1=2rs, W1=r^2+s^2,
U2=m^2-n^2, V2=2mn, W2=m^2+n^2,
with each pair primitive and of opposite parity.
Let p be odd.
If
p^a | c12=gcd(W1,V2),
then
r^2+s^2 == 0 mod p^a,
p^a | m*n.
Primitivity implies p does not divide both r,s and does not divide both m,n.
Therefore:
1. p must satisfy p==1 mod 4;
2. (r,s) lies on one of at most two homogeneous root lines
   r == rho*s mod p^a, rho^2==-1 mod p^a;
3. exactly one of m,n is divisible by p^a.
Thus the four-variable parameter state lies in at most four sublattices of index p^(2a).
The same statement, with the two Euclid pairs exchanged, holds for p^a|c21.
Because gcd(c12,c21)=1, CRT yields:
boxed:
for fixed c, the primitive Master parameters lie in c^o(1) sublattices of index c^2.
This is endpoint-specific. It is stronger than an ordinary one-congruence root-line condition of index c.
### 36.4 Counting consequence and limitation
On any dyadic four-parameter box for which standard lattice boundary errors are subordinate to volume, the large-c endpoint branch therefore carries the candidate saving
c^(-2+o(1))
relative to the corresponding unrestricted Master parameter box.
This saving is not yet promoted to a global physical endpoint bound because the exact R<=B region is product-shaped and has strongly unbalanced Euclid sectors. The old raw square-box truncation is forbidden.
The remaining task is to combine the c^2 lattice determinant with the exact physical inequalities for X,Y,Z,R and sum all dyadic/unbalanced sectors without losing the saving.
PRIMITIVE_MASTER_ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD=true
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_FACTORIZATION=true
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_SUPPORTED_ON_SPLIT_PRIMES=true
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_LATTICE_INDEX_C2=true
ENDPOINT_LARGE_C_POWER_SAVING_GLOBALIZED=false

## 37. Primitive Master cross-gcd physical tail audit
Status: EXACT STRUCTURAL UPGRADE / NAIVE LARGE-C GLOBALIZATION REJECTED / NEW SAME-ENDPOINT-MEASURE TRANSFER GATE.
### 37.1 Cross-gcd is coprime to the Master common leg gcd
Retain Section36 notation
c=gcd(W1*W2,V1*V2)=c12*c21,
g=gcd(U1,U2).
Every prime divisor of c12 divides W1 and V2. Since each primitive Pythagorean triple has pairwise coprime U_i,V_i,W_i, such a prime divides neither U1 nor U2. The same holds for c21.
Therefore exactly
gcd(c,U1*U2)=1,
gcd(c,g)=1,
gcd(c,X)=1.
Using the endpoint identity
g*R=c*(alpha^2-beta^2),
we obtain the exact physical divisibility
boxed:
c | R.
Write
R=c*t.
Then the reduced primitive terminal triple from Section36 is
(V1*V2/c, g*t, W1*W2/c).
Because this triple is primitive,
gcd(V1*V2/c,t)=1,
gcd(W1*W2/c,t)=1.
Hence the cross-gcd has the exact physical descriptions
boxed:
c = gcd(W1*W2,R)
  = gcd(V1*V2,R).
These identities are endpoint-specific and retain the exact physical space diagonal.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_COPRIME_TO_G=true
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_DIVIDES_SPACE_DIAGONAL=true
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_PHYSICAL_GCD_IDENTITY=true
### 37.2 Correction to the proposed B^(1/2)/C large-c tail
The proposed estimate
N_c(B) <= B^(1/2+o(1))*c^(-2)
does NOT currently follow from Section36.
Reason: the c^2 determinant acts on the raw four Euclid parameters (r,s,m,n). In a generic balanced dyadic sector
r,s,m,n ~ T,
with g bounded on the sector, one has
U_i,V_i,W_i ~ T^2
and therefore physical coordinates, including R, are of scale
R ~ T^4.
Thus R<=B corresponds to
T <= B^(1/4),
while the ambient four-variable box volume is
T^4 ~ B,
not B^(1/2).
Consequently the Section36 determinant statement by itself gives, at the raw ambient-volume level, a candidate main term of scale
B^(1+o(1))*c^(-2)
in a balanced sector, before the M3/endpoint square restrictions are charged.
The independent global endpoint estimate
P(B) << B^(1/2+epsilon)
cannot simply be multiplied by the c^(-2) lattice density. Doing so would assume exactly the missing fact: that the c^2 determinant saving survives on the same sparse endpoint measure. That is a new conditional counting theorem, not a formal consequence of the two existing estimates.
This is also why the heuristic tail
sum_{c>C} N_c(B) <= B^(1/2+o(1))/C
is not certified.
In particular the split
C=B^(1/6+eta)
has NOT yet proved the large-c branch to be o(B^(1/3)).
LARGE_C_B_HALF_OVER_C_HEURISTIC_REJECTED_AS_THEOREM=true
ENDPOINT_LARGE_C_POWER_SAVING_GLOBALIZED=false
### 37.3 Correct next receiver
The narrow missing statement is a same-endpoint-measure determinant-transfer theorem.
Receiver: PrimitiveMasterEndpointCrossGcdTailTransfer.
A sufficient form is: for fixed epsilon>0, uniformly over dyadic physical sectors and fixed c,
N_c(B)
<< B^(1/2+epsilon)*c^(-2+epsilon),
with summable errors under the exact constraints
X=U1*U2/g,
Y=V1*U2/g,
Z=U1*V2/g,
R^2=X^2+Y^2+Z^2<=B^2,
and with primitive/canonical one-object counting retained.
Any equivalent theorem giving
sum_{c>C} N_c(B)
<< B^(1/2+o(1))*C^(-1+o(1))
would suffice.
At C=B^(1/6+eta), this would yield
P_large-c(B)
<< B^(1/3-eta+o(1)),
which is negligible against the certified denominator M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
But until this transfer theorem is proved, research may NOT discard the large-c branch and work only on small c.
### 37.4 Route verdict
The cross-gcd route remains live because it produced a genuine endpoint-only determinant and now an exact physical identity c|R. However the first attempted globalization exposed a theorem gate rather than closing the tail.
Do not return to raw square-box truncation.
Do not multiply the global P(B)<<B^(1/2+epsilon) ceiling by c^(-2) without a same-measure transfer proof.
Do not claim the small-c branch is the sole remaining branch yet.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_TAIL_TRANSFER_STATUS=THEOREM_GATE_OPEN
P_OVER_M3_LIMIT_PROVED=false
NEXT_ITEM=PrimitiveMasterEndpointCrossGcdTailTransfer

## 7. Terminal Pythagorean allocation
Let
G=gcd(PQ,YZ),
L_-=(PQ-YZ)/G,
L_+=(PQ+YZ)/G.
At an endpoint:
L_-=u^2,
L_+=v^2
with positive odd coprime u,v.
Uniquely write
u=alpha-beta,
v=alpha+beta,
where gcd(alpha,beta)=1 and alpha,beta have opposite parity.
Then exactly:
PQ=G*(alpha^2+beta^2),
YZ=2*G*alpha*beta,
XR=G*(alpha^2-beta^2).
Thus
(XR/G, YZ/G, PQ/G)
is a primitive Pythagorean triple.
TERMINAL_PRODUCT_PYTHAGOREAN_ALLOCATION=true.
The allocation alone supplies no independent global saving.
## 8. Primitive Master endpoint cross-gcd
Using
P=(U2/g)W1,
Q=(U1/g)W2,
define
c=gcd(W1*W2,V1*V2).
Then
G=(X/g)c
and the endpoint equations become exactly
W1*W2=c*(alpha^2+beta^2),
V1*V2=2*c*alpha*beta,
g*R=c*(alpha^2-beta^2).
Therefore
(V1*V2/c, gR/c, W1*W2/c)
is a primitive Pythagorean triple.
Cross-gcd factorization:
c=c12*c21,
c12=gcd(W1,V2),
c21=gcd(W2,V1),
gcd(c12,c21)=1.
Every prime divisor of c is split:
p==1 mod 4.
In Euclid parameters
U1=r^2-s^2, V1=2rs, W1=r^2+s^2,
U2=m^2-n^2, V2=2mn, W2=m^2+n^2,
if p^a|c12 then
(r,s) lies on at most two roots of -1 mod p^a
and exactly one of m,n is divisible by p^a.
Hence for fixed c the primitive Master parameter state lies in
c^o(1) sublattices of determinant/index c^2.
Flags:
PRIMITIVE_MASTER_ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD=true.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_FACTORIZATION=true.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_SUPPORTED_ON_SPLIT_PRIMES=true.

## 9. Stage14 raw-edge cross-gcd theorem
The cross-gcd has a broader exact meaning than previously recorded.
For a Stage14 physical raw two-face edge, write the two primitive oriented face triples as
F1=(S1,X1,H1),
F2=(S2,X2,H2),
with
S_i^2+X_i^2=H_i^2,
and put
g=gcd(S1,S2).
Exact Stage14 gluing gives physical edges
e=S1*S2/g,
x=S2*X1/g,
y=S1*X2/g.
Assume only that the physical space diagonal R is integral.
Then
g^2*R^2
=
S2^2*H1^2 + S1^2*X2^2
and symmetrically
g^2*R^2
=
S1^2*H2^2 + S2^2*X1^2.
Define
c12=gcd(H1,X2),
c21=gcd(H2,X1).
Prime-by-prime valuation in the displayed identities gives exactly
c12=gcd(H1,R),
c21=gcd(H2,R).
The two factors are coprime, hence
boxed:
c_raw
=
c12*c21
=
gcd(H1*H2,R).
For the canonical Master raw pair sharing the unique odd physical edge,
(S1,X1,H1)=(U1,V1,W1),
(S2,X2,H2)=(U2,V2,W2),
so c_raw is exactly the Master cross-gcd c.
Therefore
c|R
is not endpoint-only.
It already holds on the whole Stage14 physical raw two-face graph with integral space diagonal.
RAW_EDGE_CROSS_GCD_IDENTITY=true.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_DIVIDES_SPACE_DIAGONAL=true.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_PHYSICAL_GCD_IDENTITY=true.
## 10. Endpoint physical c^2 root line and large-c audit
For an M3 endpoint,
R^2=X^2+D^2,
and
gcd(c,X)=1,
c|R.
Hence exactly
c^2 | X^2+D^2
and
gcd(c,D)=1.
Thus the primitive physical pair induced by (X,D) lies on one of
2^omega(c)=c^o(1)
Gaussian root lines modulo c^2.
This is an exact endpoint restriction, but it is not by itself a global count.
The earlier tempting estimate
N_c(B)
<<
B^(1/2+o(1))*c^(-2)
still does NOT follow by multiplying the global endpoint ceiling by a lattice density.
That would double-charge savings proved on different presentations/measures.
The balanced raw Euclid four-variable volume is of order B, not B^(1/2).
Therefore
LARGE_C_B_HALF_OVER_C_HEURISTIC_REJECTED_AS_THEOREM=true.
ENDPOINT_LARGE_C_POWER_SAVING_GLOBALIZED=false.
## 11. Active theorem gate — same-measure Stage14 tail
The raw-edge theorem gives a strictly narrower and better receiver than the old endpoint-measure c^(-2) transfer.
Let
E_{>C}^{raw}(B)
be the number of Stage14 physical raw two-face graph edges of integral space diagonal R<=B satisfying
c_raw=gcd(H1*H2,R)>C.
Every M3 endpoint has a canonical raw pair formed by the two integral faces sharing the unique odd physical edge.
Stage14 multiplicity-one reconstruction makes this assignment injective.
On this raw pair c_raw equals the Master c.
Therefore exactly
P_{c>C}(B)
<=
E_{>C}^{raw}(B).
The existing Stage14 proof controls the same whole physical raw-edge measure at square-root scale.
A sufficient new theorem is only
boxed:
E_{>C}^{raw}(B)
<<
B^(1/2+o(1))*C^(-1+o(1)).
At
C=B^(1/6+eta),
this gives
P_large-c(B)
<<
B^(1/3-eta+o(1)),
which is negligible against
M3(B)>>B^(1/3).
Thus a direct tail exponent 1 is enough.
The old fixed-c c^(-2) theorem is no longer the minimal receiver.
Canonical active gate:
Stage14CrossGcdStratifiedRawEdgeTail.
Relevant Stage14 arsenal:
AR-003 exact gluing,
AR-004 raw-pair graph,
AR-005 uniform B^o(1) degree,
AR-006 square-root theorem,
AR-009 primitive Gaussian root-line count,
AR-028 no-double-charge discipline,
AR-030 monotone physical filters.
Remaining blocker:
identify c_raw inside the load-bearing Stage14 balanced-packet variables, or refine the raw-edge count directly without losing the edge-specific c condition.
Do NOT identify the historical Stage14 internal variable called cross-root gcd H with c_raw unless an exact coordinate adapter is proved.
Until this gate is closed:
- do not discard large c;
- do not work only on small c;
- do not multiply the generic endpoint ceiling by c^(-2);
- do not return to raw square boxes.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_TAIL_TRANSFER_STATUS=SUPERSEDED_BY_RAW_EDGE_TAIL.
STAGE14_CROSS_GCD_STRATIFIED_RAW_EDGE_TAIL_STATUS=THEOREM_GATE_OPEN.
ENDPOINT_CROSS_GCD_PHYSICAL_ROOT_LINE_C2=true.
## 11A. Trivial-partition double-square adapter
Stage14-t27 gives the exact endpoint compression
N_{1,1}(B)=3P(B),
where P(B) is the primitive canonical endpoint / triple population. Stage14-t22 also gives fixed-direction all-scale multiplicity B^o(1), so if A_{1,1}(B) is the active reduced-direction count then
P(B) <= B^o(1) A_{1,1}(B).
The later t28 four-linear cover is only a necessary relaxation of actual physical activation. On the trivial partition write
D=(epsilon/2)(a^2+b^2),
C=epsilon*a*b,
W=epsilon*Z,
and let the primitive physical Kummer ratio be p/q=(D+X)/Q. Then exactly
X = D*(p^2-q^2)/(p^2+q^2),
Q = 2D*p*q/(p^2+q^2).
The t28 cover equation gives
Z^2
=
(b p-a q)(a q+b p)(b q-a p)(b q+a p),
and therefore, with Y=W/(p^2+q^2), it enforces
Q^2=C^2+Y^2.
Actual simultaneous physical completion also requires the other normalized face equation
P_1^2=C^2+X^2=D^2-Y^2.
Put
K=(a^2+b^2)(p^2+q^2).
After substituting D and W, this additional requirement is exactly
boxed:
S^2 = K^2-4Z^2,
equivalently
boxed:
S^2+(2Z)^2=K^2.
Thus the M3 endpoint inside the t28 packet requires a second Pythagorean lift. The first t28 square alone is not the exact endpoint condition.
In the affine ratio T=p/q define
F_{a,b}(T)
=
(b^2*T^2-a^2)(b^2-a^2*T^2),
H_{a,b}(T)
=
(a^4+6a^2b^2+b^4)(T^4+1)
-2(a^2-b^2)^2*T^2.
After removal of the obvious q^4 factors, every endpoint candidate must have both
F_{a,b}(T) = square,
H_{a,b}(T) = square,
together with the original physical interval, scale, primitive, canonical and off-diagonal filters.
The two square gates are algebraically independent at every good auxiliary odd prime. Exact identities are
Res_T(F,H)=(a^2+b^2)^16,
disc_T(F)
=
16*a^4*b^4*(a^2-b^2)^4*(a^2+b^2)^4,
disc_T(H)
=
2^16*a^4*b^4*(a^2+b^2)^4
*(a^4+6a^2b^2+b^4)^2.
Hence for any odd prime lambda not dividing
2*a*b*(a^2-b^2)*(a^2+b^2)*(a^4+6a^2b^2+b^4),
the polynomials F, H and F*H are squarefree/nonsquare modulo lambda. Quadratic-character projectors and the Weil bound therefore give
#{T mod lambda :
  F(T) and H(T) are both nonzero squares}
=
lambda/4 + O(sqrt(lambda)).
This is an exact same-variable double-character gate. It does not assume independence heuristically and it does not double-charge the t30 one-quartic saving.
The old t14-t15 genus-5 geometry is therefore not new; the new point here is the exact adapter from the later t28/t30 four-linear packet back to both endpoint square conditions on the same physical variable.
Define A_{1,1}^{(2sq)}(B) to be the number of reduced trivial-partition directions for which at least one primitive off-diagonal packet survives both F-square and H-square gates and all physical height/reconstruction filters. Then
P(B) <= B^o(1) A_{1,1}^{(2sq)}(B).
Therefore the M3 denominator M3(B)>>B^(1/3) is closed by the sufficient theorem
boxed:
A_{1,1}^{(2sq)}(B)=o(B^(1/3)).
No such global bound is proved here.
Finite diagnostic only: reproducing the t28 synthetic box a,b,p,q<=40 gives 587 first-cover square hits, 489 universal p=q=1 diagonal hits and 98 non-diagonal first-cover hits. None of those 98 survives the second square S^2=K^2-4Z^2. This is not asymptotic evidence and carries no theorem credit.
Second-lift scale support lemma:
Put
A=a^2+b^2,
B0=p^2+q^2,
K=A*B0.
For every odd prime ell,
ell | gcd(K,Z)
iff
ell | gcd(A,B0).
Indeed, if ell|A then, using gcd(a,b)=1,
b^2 == -a^2 (mod ell),
and the first cover gives
Z^2
=
(b^2p^2-a^2q^2)(b^2q^2-a^2p^2)
==
a^4*(p^2+q^2)^2
(mod ell).
Thus ell|Z iff ell|B0. The converse is symmetric.
Therefore
rad_odd(gcd(K,2Z))
=
rad_odd(gcd(a^2+b^2,p^2+q^2)).
This identifies the t31 visible common-norm branch with the nonprimitive odd-support branch of the second Pythagorean lift. The t31 kernel-invisible branch is primitive at the canonical direction prime for this second lift.
Naive t31 box-sieve tensorization does not improve the power exponent. A vector detector using the three nontrivial characters attached to F, H and F*H still uses the same auxiliary-prime family and the same two-prime square-root correlation scale. On the full cofactor box the balance remains
L^(-1) versus L^2/H_U,
hence still optimizes at
L=H_U^(1/3)
and gives only
H_U^(-1/3+o(1)).
Therefore the new double square must be exploited together with the thin norm skeleton / physical scale coupling, not merely appended as another local character on the enlarged t31 box.
M3_DOUBLE_QUARTIC_NAIVE_BOX_EXPONENT_GAIN=false

## 11B. Second Pythagorean identity and squareclass-energy receiver
The t28 first cover and the second physical square are linked by a stronger exact identity.
Let
A=a^2+b^2,
B0=p^2+q^2,
D0=p^2-q^2.
Recall
F_{a,b}(T)
=
(b^2*T^2-a^2)(b^2-a^2*T^2)
and
H_{a,b}(T)
=
(a^4+6a^2b^2+b^4)(T^4+1)
-2(a^2-b^2)^2*T^2.
Then exactly
boxed:
H(T)+4F(T)
=
(a^2+b^2)^2*(T^2+1)^2.
In homogeneous variables this is
boxed:
H(p,q)+4F(p,q)
=
A^2*B0^2.
Moreover
boxed:
H(p,q)
=
[A*D0]^2
+
[2ab*B0]^2.
Thus when the first cover is written as
F(p,q)=Z^2,
the endpoint condition H(p,q)=S^2 is equivalent to the linked pair of Pythagorean equations
boxed:
[A*D0]^2+[2ab*B0]^2=S^2,
boxed:
S^2+(2Z)^2=(A*B0)^2.
So the second square is not an unrelated extra quartic. It is an explicit second Pythagorean lift attached to the same t28 packet.
Odd content of the first of these two Pythagorean triples is exact:
boxed:
gcd(A*D0,2ab*B0)_odd
=
gcd(A,B0)_odd * gcd(D0,ab)_odd.
The two displayed odd factors are coprime. This follows from
gcd(A,ab)=1,
gcd(D0,B0)|2,
together with primitiveness of (a,b) and (p,q).
On the t31 visible super-square-root branch,
A=ell*m,
B0=ell*n,
with v_ell(A)=v_ell(B0)=1 and ell not dividing ab*D0. Hence the canonical prime ell occurs exactly once in the odd content of this second Pythagorean triple. Dividing that common ell gives the descended legs
m*D0,
2ab*n,
before the remaining finite 2-adic / other common-content normalization.
Valuation firewall:
the previously proved identity
rad_odd(gcd(A*B0,2Z))
=
rad_odd(gcd(A,B0))
must remain a radical-support statement. It cannot be upgraded to an exact gcd valuation identity. For example the first-cover packet
(a,b,p,q)=(4,7,29,37)
has
A=65,
B0=2210,
Z=32175,
and
gcd(A,B0)=65
while
gcd(A*B0,Z)=325.
This packet does not satisfy the second square; it is a counterexample only to the unjustified valuation upgrade.
### Exact M3 energy receiver
Let H_F(B) be the exact t28 first-cover candidate state set after retaining:
- primitive/canonical physical normalization;
- exact physical height and scale/reconstruction filters;
- the physical interval;
- the off-diagonal condition;
- the first-cover equation F=Z^2;
but before imposing the second square H=square.
Every primitive canonical endpoint raw edge maps canonically into H_F(B). By the exact t27 identity N_{1,1}(B)=3P(B), endpoint raw edges contribute to the trivial second squareclass.
For a rational squareclass kappa define
r_H(kappa;B)
=
#{s in H_F(B): [H(s)]=kappa},
and define the second-squareclass collision energy
Q_H(B)
=
sum_kappa r_H(kappa;B)^2.
Then exactly
3P(B)
<=
r_H(1;B),
hence
boxed:
9P(B)^2
<=
Q_H(B).
Therefore, using M3(B)>>B^(1/3), the following is a sufficient theorem:
boxed:
Q_H(B)=o(B^(2/3)).
This is substantially weaker than demanding a uniform bounded fiber or a near-linear energy theorem. It is tuned directly to the present M3 exponent budget.
Canonical receiver:
TrivialPartitionSecondSquareclassEnergy.
### Fixed-packet collision geometry
The new energy also changes the geometry of the old t41 global collision barrier.
For one generic t28 packet gamma, write the first-cover elliptic curve as
E_gamma:
u^2=F_gamma(x).
Because Res(F,H) is nonzero and H is separable away from the already-listed bad packet locus, the second double cover
C_gamma:
u^2=F_gamma(x),
v^2=H_gamma(x)
is a degree-two cover of E_gamma branched at the eight points above the four roots of H.
Riemann-Hurwitz gives
boxed:
g(C_gamma)=5.
For two generic packets gamma,gamma', a collision of the H-squareclasses on the first-cover host is
u^2=F_gamma(x),
u'^2=F_gamma'(y),
z^2=H_gamma(x)H_gamma'(y).
Birationally this is
boxed:
(C_gamma x C_gamma') /
< (v,v') -> (-v,-v') >.
Equivalently it is a double cover of
A=E_gamma x E_gamma'
whose branch divisor consists numerically of eight vertical and eight horizontal elliptic fibers.
Let F1,F2 be the two fiber classes. The half-branch class is
L=4F1+4F2.
The 64 branch crossings give only A1 double-cover singularities, so canonical resolution is crepant. Since K_A=0,
K_X^2
=
2*L^2
=
64.
Also
chi(O_X)=16,
q(X)=2,
p_g(X)=17.
Thus the generic second-squareclass off-fiber collision surface is of general type.
This is a genuine geometric strengthening of the t41 one-squareclass collision surface, which was Kummer/K3-type. However:
general type does NOT by itself give the quantitative rational-point bound required here.
No unconditional theorem is currently imported that proves
Q_H(B)=o(B^(2/3))
uniformly over the moving physical packet family and exact height.
Exceptional packets where F/H branch divisors degenerate, share roots, or fall into a special correspondence must be isolated separately rather than absorbed into the generic statement.
## 11C. Self-correspondence and t37 common-core second gate
The second Pythagorean lift admits an exact rational parameterization that feeds back into the same first-cover quartic.
Let
A=a^2+b^2
and write the second-triple ratio with a rational Pythagorean parameter t. For the nondegenerate orientation one obtains
boxed:
T^2
=
[A*t+ab*(1-t^2)]
/
[A*t-ab*(1-t^2)].
Put
N=A*t+ab*(1-t^2),
D=A*t-ab*(1-t^2).
Direct substitution into the first-cover quartic gives exactly
boxed:
F_{a,b}(T)
=
[A/D]^2 * F_{a,b}(t).
Hence F(T) is a square if and only if F(t) is a square.
Also
boxed:
N*D
=
F_{a,b}(t)+(2ab*t)^2.
Since T^2=N/D has the same squareclass as N*D, the rationality of T is equivalent, on the first-cover elliptic quartic y^2=F(t), to the extra Pythagorean condition
boxed:
y^2+(2ab*t)^2=w^2.
Thus the fixed-direction genus-five lift can be viewed as a self-correspondence of the same first-cover elliptic quartic followed by one square-coordinate/Pythagorean lift.
This does NOT prove an infinite descent.
No monotone height decrease under t -> T or T -> t is proved.
The historical t14-t18 genus-five / branched-lift machinery is therefore the correct ancestry of this structure.
### Four-linear minor form
Using the t28 linear factors
g1=bp-aq,
g2=aq+bp,
g3=bq-ap,
g4=bq+ap,
one has
g1^2+g4^2
=
g2^2+g3^2
=
A*(p^2+q^2).
The second quartic is exactly
boxed:
H
=
(g1*g2-g3*g4)^2
+
(g1*g3-g2*g4)^2.
The two minors simplify to
boxed:
g1*g2-g3*g4
=
A*(p^2-q^2),
boxed:
g1*g3-g2*g4
=
-2ab*(p^2+q^2).
So the second Pythagorean lift is intrinsic to the same four-linear first-cover packet.
### Exact t37 Gaussian common-core adapter
Use the t37 notation
A_c=a+i b,
P_c=p+i q,
A_c*conj(P_c)
=
g4+i g1
=
c_S*N(R)*S^2,
A_c*P_c
=
-g3+i g2
=
c_R*N(S)*R^2.
Then
g1*g2-g3*g4
=
Re[(A_c*conj(P_c))*conj(A_c*P_c)],
and
g1*g3-g2*g4
=
-Im[(A_c*conj(P_c))*(A_c*P_c)].
Therefore, with
t_core=N(R)N(S),
one gets
H
=
t_core^2
*
(
  Re(c_S*conj(c_R)*S^2*conj(R)^2)^2
  +
  Im(c_S*c_R*S^2*R^2)^2
).
Hence the second squareclass on a fixed t37 common-core packet is exactly
boxed:
[H]
=
[
  Re(c_S*conj(c_R)*S^2*conj(R)^2)^2
  +
  Im(c_S*c_R*S^2*R^2)^2
].
The norm factor t_core^2 disappears from squareclass.
This is the exact analytic interface now preferred for M3:
t37 already reduces the first-cover squareclass to one-variable Gaussian spin factors in R and S;
the M3 endpoint imposes, on that same charged packet, an additional explicit Pythagorean square condition coupling those two spin variables.
No independent saving is claimed yet.
### Sieve anti-loop
A finite number of additional quadratic-character detectors on the enlarged t31 box does not automatically improve the H_U^(-1/3) exponent.
For k independently detected square conditions, the naive k-fold square-sieve balance has schematic terms
L^(-k)
and
L^(2k)/H_U.
Balancing gives
L^(3k)=H_U,
and therefore
L^(-k)=H_U^(-1/3).
Thus finite tensorization alone leaves the same one-third box exponent.
Any gain from the second square must use the thin norm/common-core geometry or a genuinely stronger correlation theorem.
